(`NO CHANGE` of id 111 is the same parameter as OCT, already at its end; id 120 is the read-only frame number by design.) Not toured on the device: the instance selector, type selector, source list, the detail pages (unison, grain window, multisample envelope), the route popup, the modulations page, the type bar of the multisample and granular types; they are covered by the host tests (`osc_ui_host`, `ui_screen_host`: every leaf of every view is hit by a tap on its middle) but were not driven on the hardware in this round.

- **Review 1 round of the oscillator UI on the Key 61 (D plan, trial 19; packages `ov17-osc-ui`, `ov17-osc-ui-stats`, baseline `ov17-base` = the commit of trial 18 plus the render statistics only; results `ov17-osc-ui-results/`).** Key 61 only. The app ran without a preload for every performance number. One table row = one window of 10 seconds with three held notes (48, 55, 60), instance A; the picture time is the helper's (reading the scene, building the vertices and drawing the layers that changed, the GL calls, `glFinish` = the GPU, the swap and the framebuffer) taken from its cumulative histogram of two status snapshots a window apart (mean and 99th percentile of the window, not the last picture); the intervals are the helper's presentation intervals (two vblanks = 33 to 34 ms is the grid). The "knob dragged" rows drag WT POS up and down all the time with a short pause between two drags (no pictures in the pauses, so the pictures a second are lower than 30 while the share of intervals on the grid says how steady the moving part is). The replaced table of trial 18 had negative CPU percentages and empty rows (the measuring script read the helper's counters across a restart); this is the corrected one:

| state | level of detail | before: pictures a second | mean ms | 99th percentile ms | after: pictures a second | mean ms | 99th percentile ms | intervals of 30 to 38 ms before -> after (percent) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| compact, idle | full | 0.92 | 42.2 | 47 | 0.98 | 25.24 | 30 | - -> - |
| compact, 3 held notes | full | 14.66 | 30.93 | 64 | 26.6 | 3.94 | 28 | 0.0 -> 96.2 |
| full screen A, idle | full | 0.92 | 85.14 | 64 | 0.93 | 24.43 | 26 | - -> - |
| full screen A, 3 held notes | full | 13.8 | 38.45 | 64 | 26.75 | 1.24 | 27 | 0.0 -> 96.6 |
| full screen A, 3 held notes, knob dragged | full | 9.39 | 39.62 | 64 | 17.36 | 15.92 | 41 | 0.0 -> 76.3 |
| compact, idle | reduced | 0.83 | 43.85 | 46 | 1.03 | 24.28 | 26 | - -> - |
| compact, 3 held notes | reduced | 18.69 | 20.72 | 64 | 29.45 | 3.86 | 30 | 41.2 -> 96.9 |
| full screen A, idle | reduced | 1.02 | 46.27 | 49 | 1.0 | 24.85 | 29 | - -> - |
| full screen A, 3 held notes | reduced | 12.51 | 35.49 | 64 | 29.01 | 1.2 | 27 | 0.0 -> 96.2 |
| full screen A, 3 held notes, knob dragged | reduced | 9.7 | 36.52 | 64 | 18.53 | 10.71 | 36 | 0.0 -> 92.7 |

Where the time went before: the CPU parts (building the vertices, the GL calls) were 5 to 7 ms; the rest was the GPU, 12 to 56 ms per picture at a clock of 200 to 600 MHz (two hundred to four hundred draws and some twenty thousand vertices per picture, every picture, although only the live read-outs moved). After: the static parts are cached cells in two layers; a picture is one quad per layer and the ops of the live read-outs; the helper paints only the box of what changed (the age of the back buffer is 0 for the first two pictures and 2 from then on, `EGL_EXT_buffer_age`; `painted_px` in the status file). Idle behavior: when nothing changes (`!changed && !needFrame_`), the controller submits no scene, and the helper's event loop executes no draw or swap (`!dirty_`), checking only DRM plane status (`verifyPlane`) once per second via ioctl without presenting frames. The historical idle table rows below reflect trial 19 bench/dump runs where frames were periodically forced (a waking GPU taking ~24 ms in swap).

- **A2, why 30 pictures a second was the ceiling at stride 1 (measured with the helper of the measuring build, `diag/pace-trace-*.txt`).** The legacy plane update (SETPLANE) of the Key 61 blocks for exactly one vblank (16.7 ms) wherever in the period it is called. The pace grid took the next slot AFTER the vblank the previous update had returned at, so a stride of 1 meant a picture every second vblank: 29.1 pictures a second at stride 1 with the grid, 59.2 pictures a second at stride 1 with the update made at once after the render (interval 16.9 ms, 99th percentile 33 ms, 2 skipped vblanks in 131 pictures). The decision "30 on the Key 61, 60 on the others" is the plugin's (`gpu_rate_stride`); with the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-fps` containing 60 the Key 61 presented 61.3 pictures a second of the compact view with three held notes (26.4 with the default), the overlay thread at 4.4 % and the helper at 8 % of a core. What the MPC XL does at stride 1 is NOT measured (the XL was not touched; its plane update was measured earlier to land one vblank late at random when it is called a few milliseconds after a vblank: the trial on the machine decides, the plugin keeps 60 only when 45 or more a second are really presented).
- **The GPU clock.** With `min_freq` held at 400 MHz by hand (before the display data was drawn uncached): compact view with three held notes 3.7 ms a picture (7 ms before), dragged 13.4 ms (20 ms); the plugin now raises it to 300 MHz by itself while pictures are made (`gpu_clock_boost`, the row "GPU MHz" of the table).
- **A4, the audio callback (the package with the callback statistics, four held notes, 300 s per case, the scenario runs on the device, nothing else is sent to the machine meanwhile).** the counters of the callback timing run from the start of the process (the reset file zeros only the MIDI part): case (a) is from a fresh start, the maxima of (b) and (c) are the maxima over all the cases so far, and they stayed at the value of (a) (3472 us, 1.20 times the nominal block of 2902 us): the compact view and the full screen with the knob dragged did not make a longer gap than the overlay switched off (ratio (c) / (a) at most 1.00 against the limit of 1.10); every one of the 338085 callbacks was inside 1.5 times the nominal block; the average processing time per callback (a) 576, (b) 608, (c) 617 us

| case | callbacks | longest gap us | times the nominal block | processing average us | processing maximum us |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) overlay off (kill switch) | 110671 | 3472 | 1.20 | 576 | 1284 |
| (b) compact view | 113706 | at most 3472 | 1.20 | 608 | at most 1284 |
| (c) full screen, a knob dragged all the time | 113708 | at most 3472 | 1.20 | 617 | 1344 |

- **B1, the function tour.** Function tour on the Key 61 (package ov17-osc-ui, review B1). Rows: 284, OK: 282, ERROR: 0, not driven: 2. Configurations: Sub, Noise, A (wavetable, multisample, granular), B and C (wavetable); B and C in the other two types: the type switch (main:...) is in the table of A, the controls are the same code with another prefix.. Every parameter the tour changed was written back. Found and fixed on the way: the route button of the compact view did nothing (it now opens the full screen and the popup).
- **B2, the read-outs.** Engine tests (`core/tests`): the read heads of up to eight voices, the grains (up to 32), the sub phase, a multisample position. On the device: the white read head line of a loaded multisample instrument is on the display while notes are held (`screens/b05b-multisample-live-heads-helper.png`); the grains of a granular cloud are drawn while it plays (a one-shot cloud ends with the sample).
- **B3, loading.** The browser lists the SD card, the plugin content and the USB drive; a WAV from `MPC Documents/Samples` was loaded into the granular oscillator (the name on the source, the min / max picture on the display, `screens/b03-*`) and into the multisample one as a single-zone instrument (`screens/b05-*`). Not done: FLAC (the engine has no decoder), SFZ instruments whose samples lie above their folder.
- **Not verified:** the MPC XL (the stride 1 trial, the arm64 helper); the hardware Q-Link buttons; the sound of a loaded sample or instrument (looked at, not listened to).

- **Quality profile on the Key 61 (bench, 2026-11-17).** Measured on core 3, Cortex-A17 at 1.608 GHz, isolated by the kernel, governor `performance`, the application idle with the test project; all 112 presets, 8 notes, 4 s each): under `low` the dearest presets are Diffusor Cloud Pad (99th percentile 100.7 %, longest block 149 %, 17 blocks longer than the block), Scream Filter Bass (96.3 %), EMS Squelch Bass (87.2 %), Formant Vowel Bass (81.7 %), Diode Saturated Bass (80.9 %); the mean over the presets of the mean block is 36.4 %. Under `low,unison=16,fx=all`: Diffusor Cloud Pad 104.8 % (141 blocks over), Alien Communication 97.2 %, Scream Filter Bass 96.9 %, Dream Grand Piano 94.6 %, Dyno Electric Piano 91.1 %. Read with care: in that run every preset, the lightest one included (median 4.9 %, 99th percentile 23.3 %), has its 99th percentile about 20 to 25 points of the block above its median, while on the XL the two are 1.5 to 3 points apart; a part of a block that does not depend on the preset is not the engine's (the bench is a normal process on a core that also runs one of the application's real-time audio threads). The medians (Diffusor Cloud Pad 75.6 %) are nearer to what the engine itself takes; the earlier bench of 2026-11-13 ("Observed on the device") had that preset at an average of 60 % and a 99th percentile of 68 %. Either way the Key 61 has no room.

- **Standby dim on the MPC XL (2026-11-17, overlay build, the Play page on screen).** After the idle minutes the host blurs its picture and does NOT darken it, and does not touch the backlight: `/sys/class/backlight/backlight-0/brightness` reads 3 awake and 3 dimmed (maximum 32); the status file said `view: none`, `dimmed: no`, `dim_transitions: 0` (the Key 61 build, written for a backlight drop and a half brightness picture, did not see it, and the host's bare skin showed). Two grabs of the host plane (1280 x 800 UI orientation; `.hw-local/mpc-perf/xl-shots/xl-standby-awake-host.png`, `xl-standby-dimmed-host.png`): the background (600, 400) is (30, 29, 30) in both, so no global factor (about 1.0); our left marker (36, 137) goes from (114, 58, 44) to (84, 48, 39), the instance picture (48, 137) from (20, 20, 216) to (24, 23, 149), the right marker (1244, 137) from (58, 114, 100) to (48, 83, 75); the three keep 0.64 of their contrast on every channel (6 px squares), and the dimmed colour lies on the line background -> marker colour. Row y = 137, x = 30 to 54 step 2: awake background, background, marker, marker, marker, background, background, background, instance, instance, instance, background, background; dimmed (37, 32, 31) (56, 38, 34) (75, 45, 38) (84, 48, 39) (72, 43, 37) (52, 37, 34) (35, 30, 47) (27, 26, 87) (25, 24, 130) (24, 23, 149) (25, 25, 122) (27, 27, 79) (29, 28, 43). The blur of the whole host picture (also its text and headers: a black area at (107, 770) is 0 awake and 65 dimmed, the header (100, 60) 45 awake and 61 dimmed) is a box 7.5 px wide centred a quarter of a pixel up and left of the pixel centre (a fit over the three markers: mean error 0.23 levels, largest 7; a box of 7 or 8 px is worse: 0.66 and 0.60 mean on the left marker). The blur reaches 4 to 5 px beyond a marker's edge (the background at 7 px is untouched).

- **Start-up of the plugin (journal, release build).** `dlopen` plus `VSTPluginMain` about 36 ms with the library cached, up to a second on the very first load after boot. The long wait is after "Initialising VST": effOpen and the calls that follow.

- **Status lines of the start-up run.** Status lines of that run (ms): engine_create 14.5, content_store 0.08, scan_presets 13.8 (index), default_params 0.64, vst_main_rest 2.3, host_until_first_call 0.003, host_until_resume 165.7, engine_prepare 186.8 (details: wavetables 8.7, prepare_voices 95.6, prepare_fx 73.6, start_overlay 1.8, overlay_readout 8.9), host_until_first_audio 2401.2, open_ms_total 2785, open_ms_total_ui 3740. The preset index and the lazy tables did what they should (scan 13.8 ms, wavetables 8.7 ms); what is left of ours up to the first audio is about 0.22 s of the numbers above (engine_create to vst_main_rest 31 ms, engine_prepare 187 ms, of which voices 96 and effects 74) plus whatever of `host_until_resume` and `host_until_first_audio` is ours.

- **First pictures and the GPU helper.** `open_ms_at_<step>` (time since time zero, not added up): `overlay_thread_start`, `views_built` (the 24 pages, on the overlay thread), `page_seen` (the page detector found a tab of ours on screen and the first picture was drawn), `helper_asked` (the GPU helper's start was requested), `helper_ready`, `first_frame`. Before this change the helper was started only at `page_seen` (the first picture of a view), so its 584 ms (EGL, GBM, first context) came after the page appeared. Now the overlay thread asks for the helper in its first step after an instance is attached (`Timing::prewarmGpu`, set by the service only; tests keep the old behaviour), so the start runs in parallel with the host's loading and does not touch the host's thread; the helper only takes the plane when a scene is shown (it stays running while hidden, as it already did after a hide). Expected: `first_frame` follows `page_seen` by one scene instead of by the helper's start. To be checked on the device: the plane's zpos is taken (`takeStacking`) at the helper's start now, with nothing shown; the host's pointer must still be above our plane when the first page appears.

- **Content oscillators, round 10 (Sample, Granular, Spectral, Multisample kernels).** **Measured on the devices** (round 9 commands, median of 3 passes, % of the 2.902 ms block; files in `.hw-local/mpc-perf/round9-measure/after/`), Key 61 `low` median / p99 / longest block, before -> after: Frozen Pluck Drone 154 / 189 / 291 -> 62 / 84 / 152; Chord Cloud Pad 108 / 140 / 233 -> 39 / 68 / 135; Bowed MPE Strings 105 / 138 / 211 -> 63 / 83 / 146; Vowel Scrub Choir 100 / 135 / 233 -> 36 / 64 / 136; Gong Grain Swarm 80 / 105 / 212 -> 35 / 62 / 150; Glass Zone Bells 73 / 92 / 180 -> 37 / 66 / 119; Spectral Vowel Freeze 17 / 189 / 1362 -> 17 / 95 / 508; Spectral Gong Pad 18 / 174 / 1260 -> 18 / 90 / 495; Spectral Chord Morph 20 / 152 / 1199 -> 20 / 79 / 468; Shepard Ascent 53 / 99 / 208 -> 53 / 94 / 193. XL (rule p99 under 50): every preset is now under it (Frozen Pluck Drone 52 -> 36, dearest preset Ring Mod Gamelan 38); the Spectral strike block 600 / 547 / 525 % -> 187 / 168 / 150 %, Shepard Ascent longest block 114 -> 83 %.
  **Still over.** Key 61 p99 over 70 % in 23 presets (list in `k61-after-tables.md`), content ones: Spectral Vowel Freeze, Spectral Gong Pad, Spectral Chord Morph, Frozen Pluck Drone, Bowed MPE Strings, Shepard Ascent (most of the rest of those blocks is the engine without content: filters, chorus, wavetable unison). A Spectral strike still takes 3 to 5 blocks on the Key 61 (2 voices per note, 3 priming frames, about 0.3 ms a frame on real content) and 1.5 to 1.9 blocks on the XL: it cannot fit one block with sound-identical output; it would take spreading the priming over blocks (an onset delay of up to a few blocks for the later voices of a chord) or fewer Spectral voices, which is the owner's decision. The analysis is also made for every sample at load, whatever the oscillator type (a load time cost, not on the audio thread).

- **Wavetable oscillator CPU work (bit identical), branch `work/opt-osc`.** The findings of `docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-OSC.md` that keep every output sample: the morph build skips the bins outside 0..K and runs its first two transform stages in NEON (F2, F3), the two morph buffers swap roles instead of being copied and a moving morph is not stored in the chord cache (F4), the spread / pan sines, the detune offsets and the lane increments are kept between updates (F5), the plain table read keeps its phase prefix sum in registers and the level / size per lane (F7), one reciprocal of the chunk length per render for a power of two (F8), an FM warp alone and the Filter LPF / HPF pole gain take the in place path (F10, F11), the update shortcut is a byte compare first (F6), the noise coefficients are cached (F12), and the update of a period with level 0 waits for the next period that is not silent (`WtOsc::deferSilent`, review section 10: the oscillator's phase, morph age and random state do not advance while silent in the old code either, so nothing needs catching up). Tests: `synthyboy_test_own_wt_osc_opt` (old and new path in step on random patches, `SB_TEST_SEEDS=N` for more), `synthyboy_test_own_morph_transform`; `WtOsc::setReference (true)` and `buildMorphCycle (..., referenceBuild)` select the old code. The device numbers are in the round 12 entry below.

- **Round 12: the three bit identical CPU branches together (`work/opt-content`, `work/opt-osc`, `work/opt-filters`, merged in d7cbc890), proof and device numbers.** *Proof of bit identity:* `synthyboy_preset_render --engine own --tier low | medium | high --wav --chord-wav --content-dir <store>` (the store holds the two factory packs under their pack ids, so the content presets sound) over all 160 presets, built at 152b4a45 and at d7cbc890: 960 files per build (160 presets x 3 tiers x single note and chord), `diff -rq` reports no difference. On the devices `tier_bench --verify` (filters, effects and warp samples with the vector code and without) over twelve loads (the Scream, EMS, MG, Diffusor, Smear, Multi SVF, Flanger, Ring Mod, Spectral and Granular presets): identical samples except Spectral Vowel Freeze and Frozen Pluck Drone, which differ by 9e-8 / 1.2e-7 (Key 61) and 9e-8 / 1.8e-7 (XL) in absolute value, the content kernel difference of round 10 (the round 10 binary prints the same figures; `--verify --tolerance 3e-5` passes 12 of 12 on both devices). *Measured on the devices* (round 10 commands: `--all-presets --seconds 4 --repeat 3`, Key 61 `--core 3 --config low --budget 70`, XL `--core 6 --config "high,voices=16,control=economy" --budget 50`, median of 3 passes, % of the 2.902 ms block; files and tables in `.hw-local/mpc-perf/round12/`: `k61-r12-tables.md`, `xl-r12-tables.md`, `k61-r12-fifo-table.md`, `k61-r12-sections-tables.md`, `k61-ab-table.md`), against the round 10 files: Key 61 mean of the 160 medians 33.05 -> 26.57 %, mean p99 55.94 -> 52.58 %, presets with p99 over 70 %: 24 -> 14 (Lorenz Garden median / p99 96.1 / 106.8 -> 61.7 / 82.4 with no block over 100 % any more, Parallel Dusk Pad 88.2 / 97.2 -> 41.1 / 69.7, Ring Mod Gamelan 84.6 / 95.2 -> 42.2 / 69.5, Wah Pressure Clav 73.2 / 84.3 -> 39.7 / 65.9, Triple Fifth Lead 83.5 / 93.8 -> 52.1 / 75.2, Triple Fold Reese 75.5 / 85.1 -> 47.9 / 73.0, Shepard Ascent 62.1 / 94.0 -> 42.1 / 73.8, Stretched Glass Choir 65.8 / 101.3 -> 51.0 / 85.7); XL mean median 16.21 -> 15.35 %, mean p99 21.36 -> 17.20 %, all 160 inside the 50 % rule (dearest Glass Zone Bells 37.0; Ring Mod Gamelan 35.6 / 41.9 -> 24.5 / 30.1, Diffusor Cloud Pad 30.4 / 37.2 -> 24.1 / 26.0, Tine Glass Keys 33.1 / 38.5 -> 27.6 / 29.3). The only presets whose median is higher than in the round 10 files are the three Spectral ones (the deferred start of round 10 moved a quarter of the frames into every block, p99 falls by 19 to 36 points) and, on the XL, four content presets by 0.9 to 1.4 points: an interleaved run of the 152b4a45 binary and this one on the same device shows them equal or lower now (Key 61: none of the 24 presets that were over 70 % has a higher median than at 152b4a45, the gain 1.9 to 37.6 points; XL: Vowel Scrub Choir 26.8 -> 26.2, Reverse Gong Swell 26.9 -> 26.4, Glass Zone Bells 35.0 -> 31.8 .. 34.5, Frozen Pluck Drone 33.8 -> 30.1 .. 33.0), so the difference to the round 10 files is the state of the tree and the device, not this round. The host's audio thread is SCHED_FIFO 20: the same 24 presets that were over 70 % in round 10, run under a SCHED_FIFO 20 wrapper (only the bench process), have a p99 of 29 to 53 % (all 24 inside the rule; the same presets in the normal run: 14 over) and 15 blocks over 100 % in all (the Spectral strike blocks, 5 a pass each) against 28 in the normal run. With `--sections` the Key 61 sections shrink where the filters were: Lorenz Garden filter 1 0.989 -> 0.330 ms (sum without the clock 2.586 -> 1.851 ms), Parallel Dusk Pad filter 2 1.153 -> 0.303 (2.355 -> 1.423), Ring Mod Gamelan filters 1.337 -> 0.557 (2.238 -> 1.425), Triple Fifth Lead filter 1 0.914 -> 0.399 (2.238 -> 1.618), Stretched Glass Choir 1.854 -> 1.613 (chorus -0.053, morph cycle build -0.049, wavetable read -0.034, filter 1 -0.047), Spectral Vowel Freeze 1.085 -> 1.029 (sample / granular / spectral -0.027, oscillator update -0.013); the chorus is 0.05 ms cheaper on every preset that has it, the wavetable read 0.02 to 0.05 ms. Still over the 70 % rule in the normal run: 14 presets (Stretched Glass Choir 85.7, Mid Side Halo 83.3, Lorenz Garden 82.4, Frozen Pluck Drone 82.1, Pentatonic Step Pluck 80.9, Bowed MPE Strings 79.9, then Breath of Machines, Triple Fifth Lead, Karplus Harp, Shepard Ascent, Triple Fold Reese, Brass Swell Ensemble, Bell FM Ripple Lead and Tine Glass Keys at 70 to 76 %), none of them over 100 % for more than a handful of blocks.
- **Round 13 (`work/opt-misc`, bit identical CPU items of `docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-MEMORY.md`).**
  - *M1, the Compressor's look-ahead limiter (`fx_compressor.h`, `Limiter::process`).* The 136 `__aeabi_idivmod` calls per sample (`% cap` in the ring indices) are gone: the indices wrap by compare, the minimum and the sum run over the window as two contiguous runs of the ring (the sum in the old order, so the same rounding), and the minimum uses `vminq_f32` while the ring holds only normal numbers and +0 (a NaN, an infinity, a subnormal or a -0 in the ring switches to the scalar loop for the next `cap` samples, so `std::min` semantics are kept for every input); a look-ahead not below the capacity (never reached: `cap = la + 8`) takes the old code. The old code stays selectable (`Compressor::setReferenceLimiter (true)`). Test `synthyboy_test_own_compressor_opt` (1116 limiter runs over every look-ahead 1 to 200 and capacity la+1 .. la+20, special values, parameter changes, block splits, eight sample rates; outputs and ring state compared with memcmp). Mac (hardware divide, so far less than the arm32 gain): one band, 128 sample block, 0.0095 -> 0.0051 ms. The three limit presets (Pentatonic Step Pluck, Karplus Harp, Detonation Tail) rendered at tiers low, medium and high, single note and chord, `--float32 --hash`: every WAV and hash identical to the build before.
  - *libKeys warm-up.* The key table of the library oscillators (`libKeys ()`, `snprintf` and hashing on its first call) is built in `OwnEngine::prepare ()` now, like the other first-use tables, so no first-use initialiser runs on the audio thread any more (test `synthyboy_test_own_libkeys_warm`).
  - *T1, the engine's two helper threads.* `OwnEngine::fxWorker` (thread name `sb-fx-build`, builds effect slot modules after a live type change) and `FxWorker` (`sb-fx-job`, the Convolve rebuild and spectrum jobs) no longer wake every 2 ms: they wait without a timeout on a lock free wake-up flag (`WakeSignal`, futex on Linux) that the type change, the job submit and the stop notify, so there are no idle wake-ups. At their start they call a hook the shell sets (`synthyboy::setHelperThreadStartHook`, `core/include/synthyboy/helper_thread_hook.h`; the engine core does not know the shell): the MPC shell's hook gives them the normal scheduling class, nice 0 and the cores of the overlay plan (`planCpus`: not the kernel-isolated cores, not the cores the host's audio threads are pinned to), instead of what they inherited from the creating thread. On the device check `ps -eLo tid,psr,cls,rtprio,comm | grep sb-fx` while playing a preset with Convolve. Offline rendering waits for the job as before.
  - *N1, the NEON float compares of the sample and granular oscillators are bit pattern compares.* `equalPower4` and `sanitize4` (sample oscillator) and the grain window and the output clamp (granular) use `core/engine-own/dsp/neon_cmp.h` (two new helpers, `gtConst` and `absGeConstFinite`), so the 32 bit ARM build emits single NEON compares instead of four scalar compares per vector; the masks are those of the float compares for every input (test `synthyboy_test_neon_cmp`). The spectral kernels and `wt_osc.h` still have their float compares.
  - *K6, the render compare is exact now.* `synthyboy_preset_render --float32` writes the `--wav` / `--chord-wav` files as 32 bit float WAVs and `--hash` writes `<preset>.hash` next to each (the 64 bit FNV-1a hash of the float bits and the frame count, also for a render that is not finite). The default 24 bit PCM cannot show a difference below half a 24 bit step (6e-8 of full scale), so **every build to build or old to new bit identity proof must render with `--float32 --hash` and compare the `.hash` files of the two folders** (`diff -rq` alone on 24 bit files is not accepted as proof). Test: `synthyboy_test_preset_render_output` (a one ulp difference below the 24 bit step is invisible in the 24 bit file and reported by the float WAV and by the hash). The round 12 proof above was made on 24 bit files and the build platform was not recorded; a Mac build does not test the arm32 GCC code, the proof for the arm32 build has to run on that build (qemu-arm or the device).
- **Round 13: two audible engine changes the owner decided (branch `work/opt-audible`; what is heard, per preset, with the numbers: `docs/release/AUDIBLE-CHANGES-ROUND-13.md`).** *Spectral Phase Lock* (`core/spectral`): the identity lock now covers both halves of every peak region (it left the bins between the midpoint and each peak after the first one with their own phase; `docs/engine-spec/07-sample-granular-spectral.md` section 6, Phase Lock). Only bin 0 and the peaks are advanced, the lock runs per region (inline for runs under 4 bins, `kernels::lockRun` in NEON for longer ones). Mac per frame with Phase Lock, rich tone / noise: 5.26 / 6.60 us before, 4.84 / 6.40 us now; `tier_bench --config low` on the Mac, mean of the block: Spectral Gong Pad 1.21 -> 1.14 %, Spectral Vowel Freeze 1.38 -> 1.30 %. Key 61 estimate (the measured advance of 33 us per 1025 bins; the two samples have a peak every 3.7 bins, 268 / 280 peaks a frame): the advance shrinks from about 575 bins (18 us) to about 275 (9 us), the lock writes about 750 bins in about 550 runs instead of about 450 (2 to 7 us more): about 4 to 9 us less per frame, 0.3 to 0.6 points of the block for the two frames a block of these presets. *Morph rebuild stagger* (`core/engine-own/osc/wt_osc.h`, hook in `Voice::tick`): after the build of a note's first period a voice's morph age starts at its slot, one of 8 evenly spaced periods of the rebuild period (seed = note serial + 4 x oscillator, order 0 2 4 6 1 3 5 7 of the seed mod 8), so the eight builds of a chord fall in eight different periods instead of one: the same number of builds in every block (test: 256 chord cases, max - min 0 per block, before 8). Mac: the worst block of 8 Harmonic Stretch voices 40.7 -> 13.3 us, its median 3.9 -> 9.3 us; `tier_bench --config low --seconds 10 --repeat 3` (median of the passes, % of the block) Stretched Glass Choir median / p99 1.79 / 3.34 -> 2.12 / 2.30, Shepard Ascent 1.52 / 2.95 -> 1.83 / 2.00, means unchanged (2.15, 1.86). Key 61 estimate from the round 12 build cost (Stretched Glass Choir 0.131 ms of morph build a block = about 65 us a build): the rebuild block carried 8 x 65 = 520 us (18 points), now every block carries about 2 x 65 = 130 us (4.5 points): p99 85.7 % falls by 6 to 14 points, the median 51.0 % rises by about 4.5; Shepard Ascent (8 builds of about 58 us) p99 73.8 % falls by 5 to 12 points, median 42.1 % rises by about 4. Re-levelled: Spectral Gong Pad master.volume -1.8 -> -1.9 dB, Spectral Vowel Freeze -11.7 -> -12.0 dB. To be measured on the device: the `--sections` and p99 runs listed in the AUDIBLE-CHANGES document.
- **Round 13 integration: `work/opt-shell`, `work/opt-misc`, `work/opt-audible`, `work/fix-hidden-touch`, `agy/g65-load-progress` and `work/opt-voice` together (code state c8191271; proofs, packages `ov37`, `ov37h`, `ov37-release`, device numbers; files in `.hw-local/mpc-perf/round13/`).**

The Spectral and morph param audit baselines did not move.

The first arm32 run found test faults, not code faults: the NEON of ARMv7 flushes subnormals whatever the float mode is, so (a) the limit test of the Serum filter unit in `test_neon_cmp.cpp` took the IEEE `std::clamp` of a subnormal as the only right answer (8527 subnormal values differed, nothing else) and now accepts the flushed value too, and (b) the peak and master stage tests had their flush-to-zero test only for AArch64 and their default float mode test failed on subnormals: on 32 bit ARM they now set FPSCR.FZ (the mode of the shell) and the default mode forms are left out there (c8191271). The package builds show one new GCC 12 warning for arm32 (`simd.h` `clampTo`, `-Waggressive-loop-optimizations`, 'iteration 1073741823 invokes undefined behavior', inlined into `FilterUnit::process`): the same warning is in the round 12 package log (line 156 then, 160 now); no warning names `peak.h`, `master_stage.h`, `neon_cmp.h`, `output_stage.h` or `helper_thread.h`. The bit identity proof above ran on the Mac build; the arm32 build itself is checked by the device `--verify` below, not by a render compare.

Against ov36 exactly eight files differ per package: the two Phase Lock presets and their `.xpl`, `contentpack.json`, `index.json` and the two binaries (arm32 3504016 -> 3532712 bytes, arm64 5026768 -> 5092296 in `ov37`). The release builds hold none of the log strings of `docs/ui-components/LOGGING.md`.

Interleaved A/B (round 12 binary, new binary, twice, three passes each, the 24 heaviest presets): Key 61 the median falls by 10.7 points on average (none higher) and p99 by 8.5 (Mid Side Halo 72.7 / 93.6 -> 65.6 / 86.5, Lorenz Garden 70.4 / 92.6 -> 64.1 / 84.8, Stretched Glass Choir 59.0 / 94.1 -> 49.9 / 78.7, Pentatonic Step Pluck 69.0 / 88.2 -> 37.1 / 68.0, Shepard Ascent 46.1 / 76.6 -> 35.3 / 67.7), the blocks over 100 % in the 24 presets in six passes 215 -> 92; XL the median falls by 1.7 points on average and p99 by 1.8 (all 24 lower). All presets run, Key 61 (against the round 12 file, with the 18 % above): mean of the medians 26.57 -> 23.45 %, mean of p99 52.58 -> 48.96 %, presets over 70 % p99: 14 -> 12; the limiter presets: Pentatonic Step Pluck 60.5 / 80.9 -> 36.9 / 66.9, Karplus Harp 53.5 / 74.9 -> 30.5 / 58.5, Detonation Tail 38.1 / 64.3 -> 22.7 / 48.0; XL mean of the medians 15.35 -> 14.27 %, all 160 inside the 50 % rule (dearest Glass Zone Bells 39.7 p99). Under SCHED_FIFO 20 (`rtrun`, the 24 presets of the round 12 table) mean of the medians 38.25 -> 34.26, mean of p99 43.19 -> 39.30, none over 70 %. `--sections` (Key 61, 10 seconds, against the round 12 files, each section figure less the clock): in every preset with sections, voice render routing / mix / output falls by 0.023 to 0.037 ms, envelopes + LFOs by 0.011 to 0.017 ms and the modulation matrix by 0.011 to 0.016 ms, while the unchanged sections (filters, chorus, wavetable read) are 0.01 to 0.03 ms higher on the day, the 18 % of the device; the morph cycle build of Stretched Glass Choir 0.131 -> 0.139 ms (the stagger moves the builds, it does not remove them: the longest block of its sections run 5.53 -> 5.27 ms, p99 in the A/B run falls by 15.4 points).

- **Spectral note start, round 10 (deferred priming frames, start offsets).** What changed (`docs/engine-spec/07-sample-granular-spectral.md` section 4a has the maths): a Spectral voice no longer makes the four frames that overlap its first hop at the strike. It makes frame 1 (offset 0) or frames 1 + 2 / 2 (offsets 128 / 256 / 384) in `noteOn`, the others when their weight in the first hop asks for it (at the latest at the first regular frame), never frame 0, and renormalises the first hop by the squared windows of the frames that exist; from the first regular frame on the samples are the old start's, bit for bit. The voices of a note and a chord start at offsets of 0 / 128 / 256 / 384 samples inside their hop (`LibOsc::beginType`), so their regular frames fall into different blocks. The circular shift of one frame, the first idea for the earlier frames, does not work (an analysed 1 kHz sine starts 9.95 dB low with it, section 4a). On the Key 61 the scalar `findPeaks` is used (10.8 us against 18.7 us, the same peaks); `noteOn` clears only the memory a note needs and `LibOsc` no longer resets the voice twice.
  **Measured on the devices** (round 9 commands: `--seconds 4 --repeat 3`, a chord struck every second, Key 61 `--core 3 --config low`, XL `--core 6 --config "high,voices=16,control=economy"`, `SYNTHYBOY_CONTENT_DIR` with the three samples; median of 3 passes; files in `.hw-local/mpc-perf/round10/spectral-start/a2/`, `tables.md`), % of the 2.902 ms block, round 9 build -> now, median / p99 / longest block: Key 61 Spectral Vowel Freeze 18.2 / 95.0 / 524 -> 29.9 / 59.9 / 307; Spectral Gong Pad 19.4 / 91.1 / 477 -> 29.8 / 60.2 / 288; Spectral Chord Morph 20.9 / 79.6 / 467 -> 29.5 / 59.5 / 257; XL Spectral Vowel Freeze 7.6 / 20.1 / 189 -> 10.4 / 14.0 / 119; Spectral Gong Pad 7.9 / 17.2 / 169 -> 10.0 / 12.9 / 109; Spectral Chord Morph 9.7 / 16.2 / 147 -> 11.0 / 13.3 / 94. The mean falls a little (35.5 -> 34.8 % on the Key 61: frame 0 is gone); the median rises because a quarter of the regular frames now falls into every block; p99 falls by 37 % (Key 61) and 30 % (XL); the strike block by 41 % (Key 61) and 37 % (XL). With `--sections` (the section clock inflates both) the longest block of the Key 61: 15.7 ms (priming 7.1 + first frames 5.1) -> 8.9 ms (priming 5.4); XL 5.6 -> 3.5 ms.
  **What still exceeds one block, and why.** Key 61: the strike block is still 2.6 to 3.1 blocks; XL: 0.9 to 1.2 (Vowel Freeze 1.19). The strike is 12 frames and 8 shapings for eight voices (1.6 ms by the warm kernel times: 12 x 89 us + 8 x 67 us; `--micro` measures 1.74 ms for eight offset 0 voices of 1 frame each, 0.35 ms on the XL), and the benchmark's block takes 5.4 ms in the priming section: cold caches (the analysis frame, the tables and the voices' rings are read for the first time since the last strike) cost about three times the warm time, and the rest of the block (the engine's other 8 voices, effects, 0.3 .. 1 ms) is added. The next block after a strike carries 8 more frames (the frames that wait for the first regular frame: offset 384 voices make two of them there). What would bring the Key 61 strike under a block: (a) a larger share of frames deferred (the limit 0.5 of the squared window of a missing frame raised to 0.75 moves one of the two strike frames of the offset 128 / 256 voices, 4 of the 12 frames, to the next block; the renormalisation error grows with the weight left out: noise gets more than the 0.5 .. 0.7 dB it has); (b) shaped bins kept per voice (+24 KB a voice) so that the later frames need no shaping (saves 67 us of each later group, not of the strike); (c) the rotation of the phases for the sine and cosine (about 3 x 38 us a voice, only where several frames are made together); (d) fewer Spectral voices at the strike at tier low (unison 1 halves it). None is implemented; (a), (d) change the sound, which is the owner's decision.

### Per the SDK documentation / not yet verified on the device

These statements come from the SDK's documents (host-contract.md, install-and-test.md). A project was saved and loaded once (see above), but in which order the host restores the program and the chunk was not observed, so the host side of the program handling below is unconfirmed. Still not verified on the device: the CPU load during playback in the MPC app, and the sound of the final build (shell commit `17d6e11`): it loads in the stock MPC app, but nobody has listened to it.

- The sample rate is fixed, and the user can choose other block sizes (32, 64, 96, 192, 256, 512) in Preferences; the announced block size is a maximum. The shell handles any smaller count.
- The host has no crash protection for plugins: a crash in the plugin takes the MPC application down.
- The host selects program 0 after loading and reads every program name by index.
- The host saves the program number and the plugin's chunk in the project and restores both on load. In which order it does so, and whether it selects the saved program again after restoring the chunk, is not known.

What the shell does with programs and the chunk is its own behaviour and is covered by the acceptance tests, whatever the host's order turns out to be. `effGetChunk` returns a JSON text starting with `{"mpcProgram":N,"mpcProgramName":"<escaped name>",` followed by the engine state. On `effSetChunk` the shell reads the program number and name and calls `resolveProgram` to map them to the current preset list. The name decides: the stored index is used only if the program at that index has that name (or to choose between programs of the same name), otherwise the first program of that name is taken, so a project still finds its program when a later package has a different preset list. A chunk from an older build that has only the number still loads. If the name is not in the list, `resolveProgram` returns -1 and the shell enters a "no program" state: the sound is the one stored in the chunk, the host is given index 0, the saved name is shown as the program name and written into the next chunk again. In case the host selects the saved program number again right after restoring the chunk, that one selection is ignored (the chunk wins); any other program selection or a parameter edit ends this protection, and it is never armed in the "no program" state.

## Robustness rules of the shell (review of 2026-11-16, findings F1 .. F22)

The plugin runs inside the MPC application: a defect of it can take the whole application down. The rules, and where they are tested:

- **Strings for the host (F1).** `host_strings.h`. The plugin does not know the size of the host's buffers. It writes at most `kParamStrBytes` = 32 bytes (31 characters + NUL) into the buffer of `effGetParamName / Label / Display`: the limit the SDK's own framework (`mpc_plugin.h`, `kStr`) writes into the same buffers for every SDK plugin; the ABI header's `kVstMaxParamStrLen = 8` is only called "the conservative limit" and 2637 names do not fit it. The documented sizes for `effGetProgramName` (25), `effGetEffectName` (33), vendor / product (65). Nothing past the terminator is touched (no more `buf[63] = 0`). The host's real buffer sizes were NOT measured (that cannot be done without overrunning a buffer); the old code has run on both devices for weeks writing 38 characters plus a byte at offset 63, so the real buffers are at least 64 bytes, but the plugin relies only on the documented bound. The 38 engine names longer than 31 characters and the Q proxy names (target name + " Oct" / " Sem") are shortened by ONE rule (`shortName`: a fixed word list - Spectral Spec, Wavetable WT, Multisample Multi, Envelope Env ... - then dropping inner vowels, then a cut); the engine has no short names. Tests: `host_strings_host` (every parameter of the table fits, stays readable and unique), `plugin_review_host` R4 (every parameter of the real list into a buffer of exactly 32 bytes with a canary).
- **Files in /tmp (F3, F4, F16).** `safe_file.h`: ONE reader (`O_NONBLOCK | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC`, regular files only by `fstat`, at most 256 bytes into a fixed buffer, never blocks, never allocates in proportion to the file) for every switch / debug / status-input file; the writers do not follow symlinks and replace by rename. Test: `safe_file_host`. The GPU clock only writes numbers the GPU lists into sysfs; the helper's log is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and trimmed only when it is a regular file.
- **No exception leaves a tick (F2).** `poll_guard.h`: every tick of the display thread (cursors, spectrum, overlay report, Play proxies, Q proxies, page writes, content loading, LFO shapes) and of the overlay service thread runs in a `PollGuard`; the exception is caught, counted and written to `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-status.txt`, five failures in a row switch the feature off for the life of the instance. The overlay controller's own tick disables the overlay at the first one. Test: `poll_guard_host`, and `close_hang_host` C (a fault injected into a tick of the plugin, debug build).
- **effClose (F5, F12).** `closing` is set first; the host callback goes through a gate that answers 0 while the instance is closing or before the host has called the dispatcher once (the host does not hold the AEffect pointer before that); a sample read stops at the next 1 MB block. effClose waits for the display thread at most 1.5 s and for the overlay at most 2 s (+2.5 s to stop its thread). When the display thread does not come back it is detached and frees the instance itself when it ends (nothing is joined without a limit, nothing is used after it was freed); when the overlay thread does not let go the instance is kept for good (a few MB lost instead of a crash) and the overlay stays off for the rest of the process. Test: `close_hang_host` (a host callback that blocks).
- **FX type parameters (F6).** In a live run `OwnEngine::setParam` only records the wanted FX type; a worker thread of the engine builds the module (the same pool code); offline runs build it at once. What remains: the module is there up to a few milliseconds after the write, and every other setParam path (all atomics) was already allocation free.
- **Program name and chunk (F13).** `programMutex` and `chunkMutex` (never taken by the audio thread); `effGetChunk` rotates over four buffers so the pointer of one call stays valid for the next three.
- **Display descriptor, zpos (F14, F15).** The borrowed DRM descriptor is compared once a second with what was found (device, inode, still `/dev/dri/card*`, still master): when it differs the overlay disables itself. The zpos a plane had at rest is kept in `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-zpos-rest` while the plane is raised, so the next run after a host crash puts back the true value; after a reboot of the device nothing is needed (the kernel state is gone too).
- **Hygiene.** F17 (speaker arrangements are const objects), F18 (preset file at most 4 MB, regular files only), F19 (overlay view range check), F20 (frame pitch / offset 4-byte aligned). Not done: F21 (chunk members found by `rfind` in the last 160 / 320 bytes: a design limit of the format, noted for the next format version), F22 (musl: the device is glibc).

## Preset list of a plugin track

The PRESET field of a plugin track opens a pop-up that lists the plugin's VST2 programs (`numPrograms`, `effGetProgramNameIndexed`, `effSetProgram`). Ours is flat: Init and the 160 factory presets, in the order of `sortPrograms` (folder category, then name), with no visible grouping (`.hw-local/mpc-perf/xl-shots/owner-trackview-01.png`).

Facts:

- `kVstMaxProgNameLen` is 24; the SDK says `char[kVstMaxProgNameLen+1]` for `effGetProgramName`, and `effGetProgramNameIndexed` gives no size, so the plugin writes at most 24 characters and the NUL (`truncateUtf8`: never inside a UTF-8 sequence, a trailing blank is dropped). Our longest preset name, "Bowed Pressure Strings", has 22 characters. Whether the host shows more than 24 is not known; a buffer bigger than 25 bytes must not be assumed.
- The host re-selects the saved program on load and the project (and every `.xpl`) holds our chunk with `"mpcProgram":<index>,"mpcProgramName":"<name>"`. The chunk holds the preset's own name, so a save loads even when the numbering changed. `resolveProgram` decides: the stored number when that program has the name; else the first preset of that name; a chunk without a name (before names were stored) is mapped by its number. Saves made with today's numbering (the host-saved fixture `scripts/tests/data/synthyboy-host-saved-Space-Wobble-Bass.xpl`: number 20, "Space Wobble Bass") load (`shell-mpc/tests/programs_test.cpp`).
- A display update that rebuilds the host's menu closes the PRESET pop-up (known host behaviour): the list adds no `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` call (the one after `effSetProgram` was there before).
- Hardware Previous / Next Preset steps through the program list one by one.
## Sound differences at tier low

The MPC build runs the engine with the quality profile of the device ("Quality profile per device" below); the profile `low`, the one every device had until the devices were measured, is `Tier::low` (`core/include/synthyboy/quality.h`) with the unison cap of 3. The list below is that profile: each entry names the setting of the profile that switches it (the unison cap, the polyphony, the effect mask, and the four economy groups Warp, Morph, Math and Control of `EconomyFlags`), and a profile that does not have the setting does not have the difference. Everything below applies only at that tier or with that group; the other tiers and the offline render (which runs on an engine at the high tier) are not changed by it, and the stored parameter values and the saved state are never changed, so a preset keeps its shape and a project saved on the MPC sounds in full on a desktop. Error bounds are absolute for a signal whose full scale is 1, unless they are called relative.

- **Unison cap** (profile setting `unison`): at most 3 unison voices per oscillator (the tier's own cap is 4). A patch that asks for 7 voices sounds like the same patch with 3: the same detune width, stereo spread, blend and level law. `setLowTierUnisonCap` changes the number (0 = no cap). What the cap does to the level: within 0.5 dB on a plain saw and within 3 dB over the 14 factory presets that ask for more voices (the beating of the detuned voices falls differently in a render of a few seconds). The peaks at the start of a note are lower with fewer voices (the voices start in phase), so a filter model with a limiter is driven a little less: on the three sine pads (Harmonic Cloud, Space Odyssey Pad, Frozen Aurora) the harmonics that the Digital model's limiter adds lie about 53 dB under the fundamental with 5 or 8 voices and about 40 dB lower still with 3. That is the whole of the large band differences a spectrum comparison shows for these presets (with Filter 1 off, or the oscillator level lowered, they are gone); it is not a fault of the level law.
- **Warps** (group Warp, `warp=2x`; the tanh and sine forms inside a warp belong to Math): a warped oscillator runs 2x oversampled with a 32-tap Kaiser decimator instead of 4x with 128 taps, so extreme warps alias a little more and the warped oscillator is about 8 instead of 16 samples late. The table band limit of a warped oscillator is 1.0 times the host rate instead of 3.2 times. The decimator sums its taps in four partial sums (relative difference about 1e-7).
- **Spectral morph** (group Morph, `morph=half`): the morph cycle is rebuilt every 512 samples instead of every 256, so a modulated morph lags by up to about 12 milliseconds instead of 6. The cycle comes from a transform of half the size (difference to the full transform 4e-7). By default the unison voices keep the morph spread (two cycles); `setLowTierMorphSpread(false)` shares one cycle between them. A new note builds its morph cycle at once (this also fixed a bug that left the first period of a morphing note stale). Since round 13 the voices' later builds are staggered (each voice's rebuild comes in its own period of the 512, so the builds of a chord do not all land in one block); a voice's moving morph steps at other instants, never later than one rebuild period after its last build, and a held morph is bit for bit the same.
- **Spectral morph, one cycle read in the second half of a rebuild period** (group Morph, `morph=half`, `WtOsc::setLowMorphSingleCycle`): while a morph is cross-fading from the old cycle to the new one the weight of the new cycle reaches exactly 1 after 256 samples, and from then to the next build (the second half of the 512 samples) the oscillator reads only the new cycle instead of reading both and mixing them with the weight 1. The sample differs from the two cycle read by the rounding of that mix, at most 3 x 2^-24 of the larger of the two samples (about 1.8e-7 relative, 0.5 of the bound measured on 38400 random chunks, `core/tests/test_own_wt_osc_savings.cpp`); inaudible, listed because it is not bit identical. The other tiers keep the two cycle read. A held morph (nothing changes between two builds) is not rebuilt at any tier and sounds exactly as before.
- **tanh** (group Math, `math=fast`: this entry and the entries sin and cos, exp, Moving filters, Serum filters, Chorus and phaser, and Noise) is a [7/6] Pade rational function with an absolute error below 1e-4 (clamped to plus or minus 1 beyond 4.972) in the classic filter models (the limiter of the Digital model, Ladder, Diode, Dirty), the phaser feedback, the distortion shapes and the warps.
- **sin and cos** come from a 1024-entry table with linear interpolation (error below 5e-6): the chorus LFO, the sine shaper of the distortion, the bend, sine fold and sine shaper warps.
- **exp, exp2, pow, 10 to the power x and log2** are polynomial forms (exp2 relative error about 3e-9, exp, 10^x and pow about 1e-6, log2 absolute 6e-8) in the phaser, the distortion drive and shapes, the compressor's power terms and the modulated parameter values.
- **Moving filters**: for the Ladder and the state variable models (Analog, Dirty, Digital) with a moving cutoff the setup (tan, powers, square roots) is computed at both ends of the 32-sample control period and interpolated linearly over four pieces of 8 samples (relative difference about 1e-4); both channels run in one loop with one setup. The Diode divides by constants of the block through reciprocals (a few units in the last place), and the Ladder divides by `1 + k G^4` through a reciprocal (1 unit in the last place). The Diode (2026-11-26) takes the same ramp while its parameters move: the base setup (square root, tangents, exp2) is made at the two ends of the control period and `g`, the corner of the input high pass, `k`, the input gain, the output gain and the high pass amount are interpolated linearly per 8 sample piece (the generic piece loop moved the cutoff by a power and derived the whole setup for every piece). Measured against that loop (`test_own_filter_savings`, `diode_ramp`, impulse response of the two shipped Diode presets, the 12 dB and the 24 dB style, cutoff, cutoff and resonance, drive, cutoff and blend moving every period): the peak of the response differs by at most 0.083 dB and every bin within 30 dB of the peak by 0.171 dB for a cutoff that moves up to 4 semitones per 32 sample period (the criterion is 0.5 dB); the two shipped presets (12 dB style) stay within 0.02 dB at the peak up to 12 semitones per period; only the 24 dB style with the blend and the cutoff both moving 12 semitones per period leaves the criterion (4.8 dB). The state variable models (Analog, Dirty, Digital) multiply by the reciprocal of the denominator of each section instead of dividing every sample (made once per piece or block): the output differs from the divided form by at most 4.5e-7 for an input of peak 0.05, 103.6 dB below the input RMS in the worst of 120 settings (cutoff 60 Hz to 19 kHz, resonance 0 to 1). The NEON Digital pair loop on ARM multiplies by the same reciprocals (2026-11-26; bit equal to the scalar loops, checked under qemu on aarch64 and armv7 and on x86; the bound above was measured again with the same result).
- **Serum filters**: the smoothed cutoff and resonance that the filter runs on are held for 4 samples (the coefficient lags the smoothed cutoff by at most 3 samples of a 30 Hz one-pole filter).
- **Economy modulators** (this entry and Effect racks on silence, Effects with mix 0, Stolen voices, Events: group Control, `control=economy`): LFOs, random LFOs and envelopes 2 to 6 that no modulation slot or custom shape reads are stepped every fourth control period instead of every period; their displayed values are up to 1.4 milliseconds old, and a route added during a note finds them at the phase they have.
- **Chorus and phaser**: the chorus delay times are held for 4 samples (a change below 0.004 samples), and the phaser stage and band coefficients are updated every 8 samples (a few thousandths of an octave).
- **Compressor limiter gain** (every tier; listed here because the compressor is active at tier low): the box filter of the look-ahead limiter (Limit mode) averages the released gain over the look-ahead window (66 samples at 44.1 kHz, 72 at 48 kHz) as a running sum in double, summed again exactly at the start of every control period and whenever the window holds a gain below -96 dB, a negative or a non-finite one, instead of a serial float sum over the whole window every sample. The gain differs from the old sum by at most 3e-6 relative (1.4e-6 measured on the factory range of settings at 44.1 kHz; against the exact sum the new one is within 1.2e-7, the old one is the less accurate); the minimum filter, the release and the delayed dry path are bit identical, and nothing feeds the gain back. Test: `core/tests/test_own_compressor_opt.cpp` (`limiter_running_sum_stays_within_the_bound_of_the_serial_sum`).
- **Compressor crossover** (every tier): in the Multiband mode the low band is aligned with one second order all pass at the upper crossover (the poles of the crossover filters) instead of the sum of the squared low pass and high pass at that frequency (four biquads; the two are the same filter for an exact Butterworth Q of 1 / sqrt (2), the crossover filters use Q 0.7071). The sum of the three bands differs from the four biquad form by less than -100 dB (worst of tones from 0.25 to 3 times the upper crossover at 44.1 and 48 kHz and nine crossover pairs; -121 dB on noise; -92 dB was the first estimate). The crossover filters are designed once per control period and copied to the right channel; the unused power of ten in the biquad design of the low pass, high pass and notch is skipped (both bit identical). Test: `core/tests/test_own_compressor_opt.cpp` (`compressor_low_band_all_pass_merge_stays_below_minus_80_db`, `biquad_design_without_the_unused_power_is_bit_identical`).
- **Delay high quality interpolation** (every tier, effect Delay with hq on): the eight weights of the 8 point Lagrange read are products of (t - m) times constants 1 / prod (k - m) instead of 56 quotients per read. The weights differ by at most 5.1e-7 relative from the old ones (their sum is 1 within 3.6e-7); the largest sample difference to the old delay is 2.4e-7 on a program of amplitude 0.5 (about -126 dB). The cubic read (hq off), the glide of the delay time and the filters are bit identical to before: the read position and the weights are kept while the glide has settled. Test: `core/tests/test_fxopt_cpu_delay.cpp`.
- **Phaser stage states** (every tier): the 24 all-pass stage states of the phaser are guarded against the decay into denormals once per control period instead of at every stage and sample (the feedback path keeps its guards). While the states stay above 1e-18 the output is bit identical to before; where a state decays below 1e-18 inside a period the old code flushed it to 0 at once and the new one at the start of the next period, so the output differs by that state's contribution, 3.1e-17 absolute at most (measured, tails of 48 random settings at 44.1 and 48 kHz, both math forms); both end in exact silence (the new tail is about 2 % longer in samples). Test: `core/tests/test_fxopt_cpu_phaser.cpp`.
- **Noise**: the six uniform numbers of the Gaussian noise come from three draws of the generator (16 bits from each half); the variance and the colour are the same, the random sequence is a different one.
- **Effect racks on silence**: a rack whose input has been exactly zero and whose output has stayed below minus 120 decibels full scale for longer than the longest gap between echoes (20 seconds if a delay or a pool slot is in the rack, 1 second otherwise) is not processed; its LFOs keep their pace through `idleAdvance` calls, and the rack wakes with the first non-zero input.
- **Effects with mix 0** (group Control): an effect that is switched on with its mix at exactly 0 (after modulation, for two control periods in a row) is not processed: its output is its input, which is what the processed effect puts out at mix 0 too (the same samples). This holds for chorus, delay, distortion, the effect filter, flanger, phaser and reverb; a module with latency (the compressor's limiter, Convolve) is never skipped. Of the 112 factory presets 58 have the reverb, 49 the chorus, 36 the delay, 31 the distortion, 17 the phaser and 11 the effect filter switched on with mix 0. While the module is skipped its free running LFO keeps its pace. A module that still holds a tail when its mix reaches 0 is flushed first: it runs on silence until what comes out of it has stayed below minus 120 decibels full scale for 1 second (delay and reverb: 20 seconds), and is skipped from then on. The one difference that can be heard: when the mix is opened again the effect starts from silence, so the echoes or the reverb of the notes played while the mix was 0 are not there (medium and high keep them); there is no click, because nothing old is left in the module. `shell`-independent: the rule is the engine's (`core/engine-own/fx/fx_module.h`), tested in `core/tests/test_own_fx_mute.cpp`.
- **Stolen voices**: when a note is stolen (Kill mode) the voice stops at once and its last output sample on each bus is faded to zero in 3 milliseconds by the engine (`setLowTierKillFade`; the medium and high tiers let the voice itself fade in 50 milliseconds). The ramp is a held value, like a short thump, not the voice's own sound.
- **Events**: note and controller events take effect at the start of the 32-sample control period that contains their frame, up to 0.73 milliseconds early, instead of sample accurately; a block is no longer cut into pieces by events.
- **Bypassed effects and polyphony** (profile settings `fx` and `voices`): `Tier::low` bypasses reverb, flanger, Hyper/Dimension, Bode, and Convolve (the bit mask in `quality.h`). A preset that uses them plays without them. The remaining effects (chorus, compressor, delay, distortion, EQ, the effect filter, phaser) are still active. The tier caps polyphony at 8 voices.

Changes that do not alter the sound at any tier (they compute the same samples with less work) are not listed here: the pair kernels of the voice filters (the two sides of a Scream, EMS Ladder, MG Ladder, Diffusor, Smear Allpasses, Multi SVF (all twelve styles), Flanges (all nine styles) or Ring Mod filter in one pass on a NEON register, with what follows the held cutoff made once per hold and the carrier of a Ring Mod made once when both sides run at the same cutoff, `Filter::processPair` in `core/filters-serum`: the samples of two plain calls to the last bit, tested in `core/filters-serum/tests/test_filter_pair.cpp`; ARM builds only, a build without NEON makes the plain calls), the runaway test and the limit of the Serum filters in NEON (`FilterUnit::process`, tested with the kernels in `core/tests/test_own_filter_unit_lanes.cpp`), the per call parameters and the g of the plain path computed once per call and per held cutoff, `sanitize ()` on the bit pattern, the NEON float compares made with integer instructions on the bit patterns (`core/engine-own/dsp/neon_cmp.h`: GCC for 32 bit ARM makes a float compare of a NEON register lane by lane through the VFP flags; the integer forms give the same masks for every input, `core/tests/test_neon_cmp.cpp` checks all 2^32), the chorus LFO refresh of four taps per register at tier low and its phase step once per call (`core/tests/test_own_chorus_lanes.cpp`), the two channel loop of the Digital filter model on a NEON register, the four resonators of the Formant model as the four lanes of one register, the oversampled samples of a warped oscillator made four at a time for the warps that only move the phase (Sync, Sync Var, Formant, Bend and its variants; `WtOsc::warpedGroup4`, tested with the Digital loop in `core/tests/test_own_warp_lanes.cpp`), the kept setups of the Diode and Formant models, caches of filter and morph results, coefficients computed once per block, no work for sources that are switched off, NEON loops that equal the scalar code bit for bit, and the exact floor. The level meters and the end of the engine's block follow the same rule (branch `work/opt-shell`, tests `core/tests/test_own_peak.cpp`, `test_own_master_stage.cpp`, `test_own_displays.cpp`, `shell-mpc/tests/output_stage_lanes_host.cpp`): the peaks of the mixer strips, the buses, the master and the effect chain (`core/engine-own/dsp/peak.h`: `vmaxq_f32` over magnitudes with a NaN lane masked to zero by an integer compare, the exact maximum the `std::max` loops gave, NaN passed over); the per voice source peaks of the Mixer strips 0 to 6 are measured only while the Mixer page of the overlay is on screen (`IEngine::requestMeters`, called by `MixerUi::update` on every update, a hold of 256 blocks, about 0.7 s; the bus and master strips 7 to 9 are always measured, so their release memory is exact; the source strips read 0 while nobody asks, and the first measured block carries real values, only the release memory of the 0.3 s before the page opened is not rebuilt); the shells' output stage (`core/include/synthyboy/output_stage.h`, section "Output limiter": four samples at a time with the two `isfinite` tests and the ceiling test as integer compares while the limiter is idle; the gain smoothing, the crossfade of the switch and any group with a sample over the ceiling through the scalar body) and its output peak (`shell-mpc/output_peak.h`); the engine's master stage (`core/engine-own/dsp/master_stage.h`: the volume smoother stepped only while it ramps, the stereo routing decided once per block, the Direct ramp, the sum, the gain and the safety limit four samples at a time, any group above 1.8 through the scalar knee). All of them are tested bit for bit against the loops they replaced, in the default float mode and with flush-to-zero set. Not done (S5, the display readout every fourth block): see `docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-PROFILE.md`.

## Level makeup of bypassed effects

A profile that bypasses an effect (`fx=low`: reverb, flanger, Hyper / Dimension, Bode, Convolve) keeps the loudness of the preset: for every effect that is switched ON in the preset and bypassed by the profile, the engine puts a gain at the place of the effect in the chain (`Quality::fxMakeup`, default on). The gain is computed from the effect's own settings (mix, level, for Convolve the spectrum of the loaded impulse, for the others calibration tables) and from the program at the effect's place: a control rate meter takes the energy in five bands, the stereo width and how sustained the sound is from the input of the gain (about 30 operations per sample while an effect is bypassed; the audio path of the gain is one multiply). On a bus that carries a copy of a source that also plays in Main, the gain is set at the bus so that the copy does not add up coherently when the effect is bypassed. The unison cap of tier `low` gets NO gain: a makeup of (wanted / cap) ^ 0.15 on the oscillator level was designed and measured (it improved the mean of the cap effect but made single presets worse) and is not in the code; see section 10 of the document below. Nothing happens when no effect that is on is bypassed, so the XL output (`fx=all`) is bit identical. The character of the effect (the room, the detune, the shift) is still missing. Design, tables, measured table of all 160 presets and the cases that stay over 1 LU: `docs/release/TIER-LEVEL-COMPENSATION.md`. The sound lab accepts `fxoff=NAME+NAME` and `fxon=NAME` in a profile text for measurements.

## Quality profile per device

The shell takes the engine's quality from a profile, chosen once per process for the device it runs on (`shell-mpc/device_profile.h`). A profile is a text, a base and changes to it:

`low | medium | high [, voices=1..32] [, unison=1..16] [, warp=2x|4x] [, morph=half|full] [, math=fast|exact] [, control=economy|full] [, start=stagger|off] [, fx=low|all] [, samples=low|medium|high] [, startbudget=100..10000]`

- `low` is the quality the MPC build always had: 8 voices, 3 unison voices per oscillator, the four economy groups on (`warp=2x`, `morph=half`, `math=fast`, `control=economy`), the heavy effects bypassed (`fx=low`: no reverb, flanger, Hyper / Dimension, Bode, Convolve), the cheapest interpolation of the sample oscillators. `medium` and `high` are the tiers of `quality.h` (16 voices / 8 unison, 32 / 16; no economy group; all effects).
- `startbudget=<us>` (integer 100 to 10000, absent = the engine's default of 1700) is the Spectral start budget of a block in microseconds (`Quality::spectralStartBudgetUs`): the real host can be swept with the override file `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-profile` without a rebuild. The status line and the report (`profile_settings`) begin with the base word that matches the sample interpolation (`low`, `medium`, `high`) and name every setting after it.
- Each key changes one setting; what a setting changes in the sound is the list in "Sound differences at tier low". Example: `low,unison=7,fx=all` keeps the cheap forms and gives seven unison voices and all effects.
- In the engine the settings are `Quality::maxVoices`, `maxUnison`, `fxMask`, the interpolation tier and `Quality::economy` (`EconomyFlags` in `core/include/synthyboy/quality.h`: Warp, Morph, Math, Control). A quality without explicit groups behaves as the three tiers always did; the three tiers render the same samples as before the groups existed (all 112 factory presets at the three tiers, compared file by file).

**Which device gets which profile.** The table `kDeviceProfiles` in `device_profile.h`, keyed by the exact name in `/proc/device-tree/model`:

| Device name | Profile | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `Akai Professional MPC XL` | `high,voices=16,control=economy` | the highest step of the bench's ladder inside half of the block with the round-3 engine (tables below) |
| `Akai Professional MPC Key 61` | `low` | `low` already fills the block on its dearest presets (below): no room for a step up until the engine is cheaper |
| a name that is not in the table | `low` (`kProfileUnknownDevice`) | not measured |
| no name readable | `low` (`kProfileNoNameArm64`, `kProfileNoNameArm32`: by the architecture of the build) | not measured |

So on the MPC XL a preset plays 16 voices with up to 16 unison voices, with its reverb, flanger, Hyper / Dimension, Bode and Convolve, the warps 4x oversampled, the morph at the full rate and the exact math; only the Control group stays on (the full control rate doubles the dearest blocks and is over the budget). Raising a device is an edit of its row (and of the check in `shell-mpc/tests/device_profile_host.cpp` that states what the package runs), after a bench run on it.

The rule a row follows: the highest step of the bench's ladder whose dearest factory preset, held with all notes of the step's polyphony, stays at a 99th percentile block time of at most N percent of the block (2.902 milliseconds) on one audio core (the median of three passes of 30 s, the highest pass within N as well), with no block of a factory preset longer than the block; N = 50 on the XL, 70 on the Key 61.

Measured on the MPC XL on 2026-11-17 (A76 core 6 at 2.4 GHz, governor `performance`, the application idle with the test project; `synthyboy_tier_bench --core 6 --set ladder --seconds 30 --repeat 3 --budget 50`, loads Diffusor Cloud Pad, Lush Analog Pad, Twin Warp Lead, EMS Squelch Bass: the dearest of all 112 on that core under `low` and under `low,unison=16,fx=all`):

| Step | Notes | 99th percentile of the dearest preset, % of the block (lowest - highest pass) | Longest block, % | Dearest preset | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `low` | 8 | 15.28 (15.27 - 15.32) | 22.21 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=7` | 8 | 16.36 (16.35 - 16.39) | 23.43 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=7,fx=all` | 8 | 21.81 (21.77 - 21.82) | 28.99 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=16,fx=all` | 8 | 21.77 (21.76 - 22.01) | 28.60 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=16,fx=all,voices=12` | 12 | 28.05 (28.04 - 28.07) | 39.01 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=16,fx=all,voices=16` | 16 | 34.30 (34.26 - 34.42) | 48.14 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | inside |
| `low,unison=16,fx=all,voices=16,warp=4x` | 16 | 47.12 (47.08 - 47.21) | 65.08 | Twin Warp Lead | inside |
| `low,unison=16,fx=all,voices=16,warp=4x,morph=full` | 16 | 46.93 (46.63 - 47.12) | 63.96 | Twin Warp Lead | inside (the XL's row until the round-3 engine) |
| `high,voices=16,control=economy` | 16 | 54.32 (54.24 - 54.36) | 73.39 | Twin Warp Lead | over |
| `high,voices=16` | 16 | 103.16 (103.09 - 103.31) | 118.80 | Twin Warp Lead | over |
| `high` | 32 | 115.79 (115.61 - 115.89) | 146.01 | Twin Warp Lead | over (525 blocks longer than the block) |

The same ladder with the round-3 engine (NEON warp lanes, the Digital filter NEON loop; the tier bench on A76 core 6, 2.4 GHz governor `performance`, all 112 presets, 16 notes held, one pass, the output of every step equal to the reference: 0 of 30 differ; the files `xl-r3-all.txt` and `xl-r3-all.csv` of the bench results):

| Step | Notes | 99th percentile of the dearest preset, % of the block | Longest block, % | Dearest preset | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `low,unison=16,fx=all,voices=16,warp=4x,morph=full` | 16 | 31.51 | 42.13 | Lush Analog Pad | inside |
| **`high,voices=16,control=economy`** | 16 | 35.39 | 45.73 | Lush Analog Pad | inside: the XL's row (no block over the block's length) |
| `high,voices=16` | 16 | 65.80 | 83.03 | Diffusor Cloud Pad | over |

The measurement of the Key 61 (all 112 presets under `low`, the dearest ones at or over the block) is in "Observed on the device" above, as "Quality profile on the Key 61".

**Overrides**, the first that holds a valid profile wins; one that does not parse is skipped and named in the report:

1. the first line of the file `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-profile` (`SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PROFILE_FILE` names another file): trials on the device without a rebuild; the app has to be restarted, see below;
2. the environment variable `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PROFILE`;
3. the table.

**Fixed for the life of the process.** The profile is resolved when the first engine is prepared and never again: every instance runs the same profile and it does not change while notes sound. A change while a note sounds would be heard: the warp decimator's history would be read at the other rate for one filter length and the warped oscillator would move by 8 samples against the others, the morph cycles would be rebuilt at once, a lowered voice cap would release notes. (The engine itself survives such a change, `core/tests/test_own_profile.cpp`; the shell just never does it.)

**What is in force** is written to `/tmp/synthyboy-mpc-profile.txt` (`SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PROFILE_REPORT` names another file) when it is resolved: `profile`, `profile_settings` (every setting spelled out), `profile_source` (table row, file or environment), `profile_skipped`, `device_model`, `build`. The overlay build also has the line `quality_profile:` in its status file.

**Projects move between devices unchanged.** The saved state is the parameter values the host and the player set, the content, the program and the shell's own members (`buildStateChunk` in `synthyboy_mpc.cpp`); nothing of the profile is in it and the engine never writes a value back (tested: the same patch played at `low` and at `high` saves the same bytes). The patch parameters that touch quality (`quality.oversample_4x`, `quality.no_smoothing`, `reverb.force_enable`, `master.polyphony`) are ordinary stored values that act the same way on every device: `reverb.force_enable` lets the reverb run even with `fx=low`, and a preset's `master.polyphony` caps the voices below the profile's (the factory presets say 16 or less, so `voices` above 16 changes nothing for them).

**How far the sound of one state moves between profiles** (measured, `core/tests/test_own_profile.cpp` and `bench/profile_compare.cpp`; not derived from the per function bounds above, which do not add up to a bound for a whole voice): Math alone on a saw through the resonant ladder -138 dB relative to the signal, on a patch with distortion, compressor, phaser and modulated filters the level stays within 0.01 dB and the waveform drifts (-26 dB); Control alone with notes on the control grid and no stolen voice: the same samples; Morph on a static morph -137 dB; the warp at 2x against 4x: the same level, the 4x signal 8 samples later, the rest (aliasing) -25 dB; the unison cap: level within 0.5 dB on a plain saw, another waveform; the effects of `fx=all`: the level moves with their mix (-2 dB on the bench's heavy patch).

**Choosing a profile: the bench.** `synthyboy_tier_bench` (`bench/tier_bench.cpp`) plays loads through the engine the way the shell does (44.1 kHz, 128 frames, the parameters by `setParam` and then the content, a real-time quality, flush-to-zero), pinned to one core (`--core N`), under a list of profiles, and prints the time of a block as a share of the block's real time (minimum, median, 99th percentile, maximum), the frequency of the core after every run, and per profile its dearest factory preset. `--set settings` changes one setting at a time over `low`; `--set ladder` runs the candidate profiles from the cheapest to the dearest, each with the notes of its polyphony; `--all-presets` measures every factory preset; `--repeat N` repeats the list and shows the spread; `--budget PCT` marks each profile as inside or over; `--paced` starts each block at its real time. `--no-fx-mute`, `--scalar-filters` and `--scalar-warp` switch off the skipped effects with mix 0, the vector forms of the filters (the pair kernels of the Serum filters, the NEON runaway test and limit of the Serum filter unit, the NEON loops of the classic models) together with the four lane LFO refresh of the chorus, and the four lane form of the warp samples; `--no-voice-savings` runs the control code of the voices and the modulation matrix without the caches and skips of round 13 (`docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-VOICE-MOD.md`: the per envelope, LFO, oscillator and sub settings caches, the zero skip and the right lane copy of the matrix, the first writes of the routing buffers, the active voice set) (the engine as it was before each: a before / after with one binary; what stays on with `--scalar-filters` is what has no scalar twin: the integer compares inside the NEON loops, the per call constants and the bit pattern `sanitize ()` of the plain Serum path), and `--verify` renders every load with and without them and compares the samples, which is the check that the vector code of the CPU it runs on computes what the scalar code computes. It can be built fully static for both MPC architectures, so it needs nothing of the device but the kernel and a copy of `content/presets`; with `--sections` it also builds a second binary per architecture (`tier-bench-<arch>-sections/`) that times the engine by section: its `--sections` report prints, per load, the time of each part of a block, the calls per block and the time with the cost of the clock reads taken off (the clock of a scope costs about 1.3 microseconds on the Key 61; a scope reads it twice, and about one read lands in the section around it, so the report takes the scopes entered directly inside a section off that section too: without that the voice tick, which holds ten small sections, looked like the largest part of a block). `synthyboy_profile_compare` (`bench/profile_compare.cpp`) renders every factory preset under two profiles and prints the level and third octave spectrum differences: what a step is worth to the ear.

**Tests.** `core/tests/test_own_profile.cpp` (the groups, bit equality with the tiers, the same state at every profile and the measured bounds), `shell-mpc/tests/device_profile_host.cpp` (the profile texts, the table, the overrides and their order, the report, the choice of the process; CTest `device_profile_host`).

## Content oscillators on the MPCs (Sample, Granular, Spectral, Multisample)

Round 9 measured the 24 presets that use content and found them far over the rule on the Key 61 (tier `low`, the p99 rule of the observations below) and the Spectral ones stalling a block at every chord strike. Measured with `--sections`, `--profile` (a sampling profile of the audio thread by program counter, `scripts/bench-profile.py` maps it to functions) and `--micro` (the kernels one by one, microseconds per call); the base is commit 2952f44e.

**Causes (file:line in 2952f44e).** Convolve is not one of them: `kFxConvolve` is not in the tier `low` mask, `fx_chain.cpp:330` keeps the module from running, no `fx: convolve` section appears in the Key 61 report, and `core/tests/test_own_convolve_bypass.cpp` shows the render with Convolve on equal to the one with it off at tier low (and different at high). Sample voice (`sample_voice.cpp`): per sample region and loop bounds in doubles, the pan `cos`/`sin` (465), `log2` for the mip level (103), `floor` and 64 bit conversions of a double (25: `__aeabi_d2lz` / `l2d`, library calls on 32 bit ARM), the loop crossfade `cos`/`sin` in double (452), `pow` twice per control period (390). Granular (`granular.cpp`): `log2` per grain sample (79), `windowValue` with two divisions per sample (334), `floor` and 64 bit conversions in the Hermite read (67), a test per sample for the spawn (299). Spectral: the analysis is already made once at load (`content_set.cpp:115`) and shared; the stall is the note start, which synthesises 3 frames (543) plus the first hop, each a full length scalar complex FFT (755; its time on the Key 61 is in the observations), 1025 `cos` + `sin` (747), `fmodf` per bin (716), scalar shaping loops with `cos` / `%` in the warps (281, 307). Shepard Ascent: the morph cycle is rebuilt every control block per voice (scalar `inverseFft`, `morph.cpp:54`), 4 builds a block on the XL (its share of the block is in the observations).

**Fixes** (each with a scalar form of the same bits and a test, `--scalar-content` runs the old code with the same binary; `--scalar-warp` also covers the morph transform): block kernels for the sample voice (identical samples; the loop crossfade weights in float lanes, 3e-6), the granular voice (reciprocal window, at most 3e-5 of a block's peak), the spectral voice (half length NEON transform, polynomial sine / cosine, the exact old phase wrap, vector shaping loops, the priming frames sharing one shaping (since round 10 only frames 1 .. 3 of the priming, deferred as their weight asks: engine-spec 07 section 4a), cached band and comb gains), the morph transform (NEON, identical bits). On the Key 61's Cortex-A17 a Q-register NEON operation costs about two cycles and 64 bit integer / double conversions are library calls: kernels count instructions and use 32 bit integers there.

**Render equality**: 150 of 160 presets give identical samples; the ten granular / spectral ones differ by -120 to -140 dB of their RMS (level difference 0.0000 dB, third octave bands at most 0.001 dB), except Spectral Gong Pad (Phase Lock, Add Subharmonics): -98.6 dB, level 0.0000 dB, bands 0.0008 dB.
