The cause is isolated (docs/engine-spec/07 section 7): the oscillator alone differs by -127 dB there; the Mono Bass crossover of the preset's Utility lifts round-off noise to -98.6 dB, constant over a 60 s held note. On XL arm64 the compiler no longer fuses multiply-add in these files, so XL renders differ from round 9 at the 1e-7 level.

**Waste removed from the content engines without changing a sample (round 11, `docs/release/CPU-REVIEW-CONTENT.md`).** Each item keeps its old code path selectable for its test and is proven bit for bit by it: SP1 (2a402953) a note start no longer clears the 150 KB of player buffers (`SpectralVoice::reset` clears flags only: `noteOn` clears or writes what it reads, proven by notes started on buffers filled with a pattern; `GranularVoice::reset` skips its 48 grain flags while no grain was spawned); SP2 (b2a0f581) the start schedule is a table made in `prepare` instead of up to 654 double cosines per note; SP3 (52fc6cdf) the real inverse transform, the overlap-add window, the start gains and the schedule table are built in `SpectralVoice::prepare` (`SpectralVoice::tablesBuiltForTest`), not at the first Spectral note; MK1 (c2d3accf) the bypassed Convolve's makeup caches the meter constants, the weights of the two program means (rebuilt only when the program's shares move), the impulse key (two `powf`), the level gain and the smoothing constant by their inputs (`makeup::setLegacyCachesForTest`); GR1 (f01a6a63) a scanning cloud makes the two divisions and the loop bounds of its head once per call; SA1 (e53de443) the sample kernel keeps the `log2` of the pitch ratio and the pan gains until their inputs change; LU1 (9946509c) `LibOsc::update` keeps the pan law, the detune curve, the spread pan and the pitch ratios until their inputs change; SP5 (2a36556d) Phase Lock advances only bin 0, the peaks and the kept half regions (gathered, advanced by the same kernel, put back) instead of every bin; SP6 (7fce2783) the priming frames are overlap-added straight into the ring (`overlapAdd` has a `first` sample); SP10 (25488aa2) the Spectral and Granular output stages (gain, mask of non finite values, clamp) and the library oscillator's lane mix (`simd::rampLerp` + `simd::mulAddTo` on the ARM builds) run in NEON lanes with the operations of the scalar code. The one caveat of every vector form of the engine applies to SP10: NEON on 32 bit ARM flushes subnormal numbers, so a sample or product below 1.2e-38 may be 0 instead of the subnormal value (the Mac tests run the aarch64 form, which does not flush). Not done: CS1 (the analysis only for Spectral oscillators): a live switch of an oscillator to Spectral would have no analysis for the 0.1 to 0.2 s that the background build takes on the Key 61, where it is instantaneous now; keeping the old type until the analysis is there means deferring the type switch in `voice.cpp`, which that round did not touch. Not done on purpose (rounding level or audible, owner decides): GR3, SA2, SP13, SP14, every D item. Tests: `synthyboy_test_spectral`, `synthyboy_test_granular`, `synthyboy_test_sampleosc`, `synthyboy_test_own_content_opt`, `synthyboy_test_own_convolve_bypass`, `synthyboy_test_own_mix_lanes` (arm64 Mac only).

The measurements of these kernels on the devices (median block times before and after, the presets still over the rule, the cost of a Spectral note strike) are listed under "Observed on the device" (entry "Content oscillators, round 10").

## Benchmark switches

The benchmark `synthyboy_bench` (`bench/bench_engine.cpp`) and `synthyboy_noteon_bench` run on the device as they are (built by `scripts/build-mpc.sh --bench`, or by `cmake` for the armv7 / aarch64 toolchain with `-DSYNTHYBOY_BUILD_TESTS=ON`). The switches used for the measurements above:

- `--preset FILE` and `--preset-dir DIR` play `.sbpreset` files through the same loading path as the shell (parameters by `setParam`, then the content); the directory mode prints one line per preset, the presets over the block budget in the worst block and the top 15 by average. `--csv FILE` writes name, average, 99th percentile and worst block of every preset.
- `--voices K` plays K notes (default: the voices of the tier); `--tier low|medium|high`, `--seconds N`, `--sr 44100`, `--retrigger S`.
- `--sections` prints the exclusive time of each part of a block, and the same for the slowest hundredth of the blocks with the number of voice ticks and render calls. It needs a build with `-DSYNTHYBOY_SECTION_TIMING=ON`; the clock of a scope costs about one microsecond on the device, so small sections are inflated.
- Legato and the voice control (`work/fix-legato-env`): a legato note (`Voice::legatoTo`) takes the envelope time scaling of its voice control step also when nothing of the voice is modulated (the all envelope settings cache is cleared there, in the old `--no-voice-savings` code too); no factory preset changes (renders identical, 960 files), `core/tests/test_own_voice_legato_env.cpp` is in CTest.
- `synthyboy_tier_bench` also: `--scalar-content` (the content oscillators run their reference code), `--no-voice-savings` (the voice control and the matrix without the round 13 caches and skips; `--verify` includes it), `--profile FILE`, `--micro`, `--tolerance X` (with `--verify`) and, with `--sections`, the sections of the longest block.
- `--unison-cap N` (0 = none), `--morph-spread 0|1`, `--kill-fade MS` set the tier low options of the engine described above. `--ftz` sets flush-to-zero like the plugin; `--idle` plays no notes.

## What the display reads after the CPU follow-up (2026-11-26)

The control-period caches of `docs/dsp-knowledge/voice-control.md` (the section "Per-period derivations") leave every output sample of a fixed patch as it was (offline tests: bit for bit, x86). Two of them change what the display can read, not what is heard:

- An LFO that no modulation slot reads is evaluated in the newest voice only (the voice the display reads); the other voices advance its clock and its display position but do not make its value. The cursors of the LFO pictures (`lfoVoicePhase`, `lfoPhase`) are unchanged for every voice. The value of such an LFO as a source for the newest voice is unchanged; when the newest voice ends in the last control period of a block, the voice that becomes the newest shows an older value of it for up to one block. After an edit that routes an LFO that a voice had not evaluated, its smoothing starts at the value (the sound differs from the old code to the smoothing and agrees again within a few smoothing times).
- The readout (`copyReadout`, the voice statistics) is written only while something polls it (a 256 block countdown that every `copyReadout`, `voiceStats` and `setReadoutTargets` call restarts). A display that polls 30 times a second reads exactly what it read before; a host that does not poll saves the work, and the meters start from nothing at the first block after a pause.

Neither is measured on a device; the instruction counts are in the voice control file (x86 callgrind, not the Cortex-A17 or A76).

## LFO live display and the display thread
The LFO cursor is a display-only plugin parameter, "LFO N Cursor", at the end of the parameter list (index: number of engine parameters plus N minus 1), holding the step divided by 32. The values are not saved in the state or a preset, the parameters are not automatable and no index before them moves. Because the host redraws only on `audioMasterAutomate`, a thread of the shell (`shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`, `shell-mpc/lfo_live.h`) reads the engine's live values (`LiveValues::lfoPhase`, the voice played last) and calls `audioMasterAutomate` only for a cursor that changed cell.

Thread model of the display (the reason is the host: `audioMasterAutomate` runs synchronously on the host's interface thread, and a continuously moving display was reported by the community wrapper "Open MPC" (`wrapper/vst2_wrap.c`, a comment there; measured by its authors on an MPC Live III) to hold up the host's audio threads for about 20 milliseconds every few seconds; that wrapper therefore reports from a separate thread of normal priority, every 100 milliseconds, changed values only):

- One display thread per plugin instance, created in `VSTPluginMain` (the host's loader thread, not the audio thread; whatever that thread is, the display thread sets its own scheduling first, see below). Tick rate `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_HZ`, default 10: a tick every 100 milliseconds. A tick reads the cursor positions at that moment (the cursor is always where the cycle is now, not a queue of old positions), and calls `audioMasterAutomate` for each of the four parameters whose cell changed, in index order, at most once per parameter and tick. With a fast LFO the 32 cells are therefore stepped over (the cursor jumps), which is accepted. A tick that comes late (the host call blocked) is not made up for: one tick, then the grid goes on from that moment. With no voice sounding the thread looks every 100 milliseconds and does not call the host.
- The thread is a normal thread: `shell-mpc/thread_policy.h` sets, from inside the thread, policy `SCHED_OTHER` with priority 0, nice 0 and the affinity of all CPUs. Nothing is inherited from the thread that created the plugin: a real-time policy or a single-core affinity of the creator would otherwise carry over to a `std::thread`. Nice 0 (not lower) because the thread holds host locks while it is inside the host call, and a starved holder makes the host's own threads wait. Lowering a nice value needs the privilege `CAP_SYS_NICE`; a host process without it keeps a nice value inherited from a creator with nice above 0 (policy and affinity are always reset). The thread is named `sb-lfo-display` (`top -H`, `/proc/<pid>/task/<tid>/comm`). The host test (`shell-mpc/tests/lfo_live_host.cpp`, Linux) creates the plugin from a thread that is pinned to one CPU and, where the process may, nice 5 and `SCHED_FIFO`, and checks policy, nice and affinity of the display thread; it ran on aarch64 Linux in a container, with and without `CAP_SYS_NICE`.
- The audio thread (`pluginProcessReplacing`) does nothing for the display: no call to the host for it (its one host call is the transport query `audioMasterGetTime`, as before the display existed), no allocation, no lock. The only connection is that the engine writes its readout (`OwnEngine::writeReadout`, relaxed atomic stores) at the end of a block, and the display thread reads those atomics (`copyReadout`) and the parameter mirror. The cursor values the host reads (`getParameter`) are atomics too.

Known limits of the cursor, from the engine's readout: swing and direction are not drawn; in free-run mode and for mono envelope LFOs the engine does not advance the voice's own position, so the cursor stays at the start; an envelope-type LFO that has finished jumps back to the start; with several voices only the voice played last is shown.

Build options of the MPC shell (CMake): `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE` (default ON; OFF builds the plugin without the cursor and spectrum parameters, only for comparisons). The refresh rate of the thread is the constant `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE_HZ` (10 ticks a second) in `shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`, not a CMake option. The compile switch defaults to 0, which makes the shell identical to one without the display. `shell-mpc/accept.sh` builds the ON build and runs it under ThreadSanitizer.


## Spectrum

The Macros page draws a live spectrum of the plugin's own output over its bottom row: 32 columns, from about 30 Hz to 18 kHz on a logarithmic scale, x 36 to 1244, y 480 to 616 (1208 pixels, centred). The Q-Link frames of the tab end at y 474 (rows 1 to 3), so the columns, which cross the four frames, stand below them. The plugin computes 32 band values (display-only parameters) and the smooth curve below.

- **Parameters.** 32 display-only parameters "Spectrum 1" to "Spectrum 32" at the end of the list, after the four LFO cursors: indices 2641 to 2672 (2637 engine parameters, 2637 to 2640 the cursors). Value 0 to 1 in steps of 1/64 (display text "n/64"). They are not in the mirror, the state chunk or a preset, `effCanBeAutomated` is 0, writes are ignored, no index before them moves (a test checks the names, the indices and that the chunk is the same with and without them).
- **Thread model.** The audio thread (`pluginProcessReplacing`) copies the mono sum (left + right) / 2 of the engine's output, after the engine has rendered the block, into a ring of 8192 samples (`SpectrumRing` in `shell-mpc/spectrum.h`: relaxed atomic floats, the write position released after the samples): no allocation, no lock, no call to the host. The display thread of the LFO cursors (`sb-lfo-display`, normal priority, 10 ticks a second by default) does the rest on every tick: the newest 2048 samples (about 46 milliseconds at 44.1 kHz), Hann window, FFT (`shell-mpc/spectrum_fft.h`, own radix-2, no dependency), 32 bands with equal ratio from 30 Hz to 18 kHz (`shell-mpc/spectrum_bands.h`; a band takes the largest bin inside it, a band narrower than a bin interpolates between the two neighbouring bins, so the lowest columns share bins: at 44.1 kHz a bin is 21.5 Hz wide), to dB (a full scale sine on a bin gives 0 dB), to a level (linear in dB from floor `kSpectrumFloorDb` = -60 dB -> 0 to ceiling `kSpectrumCeilingDb` = -12 dB -> 1, clamped to 0..1: levels above -12 dB show a full column because on the device a single loud note peaked at about half height with the earlier 0 dB ceiling, as a single band never reaches 0 dB), smoothing (the distance to a higher target is covered by 0.7 per tick, to a lower one by 0.15, about 3 seconds from the top to nothing at 10 ticks a second), quantised to 1/64 so that a small flutter makes no report. Every column whose quantised value changed is reported with `audioMasterAutomate` in index order: at most 32 calls a tick besides the at most 4 of the cursors, none while all columns stand on the floor. A ring that did not move since the last tick (the host stopped calling) counts as silence.
- **Build option (CMake).** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_SPECTRUM` (default ON in `shell-mpc`; the source default is 0, so a build that does not define it has no spectrum code, no parameter and no string of it; it needs `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_LFO_LIVE`, the thread is shared). The overlay's Macros page reads the spectrum parameters, so the MPC package must be built with the spectrum ON.
- **Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/spectrum_bands_host.cpp` (the band mapping on a 1 kHz sine, other sines, silence, white noise, the quantiser), `shell-mpc/tests/spectrum_host.cpp` (ring and analyser: silence, a sine, the smoothing, a stale ring, two threads), both also under ThreadSanitizer through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`.
- **The curve over the meters.** The Macros tab shows the signal as one smooth, filled, anti-aliased spectrum CURVE (Serum / EQ analyser style) over that row, the view `spectrum` of "Overlay (experimental)". Where the picture comes from: nothing new on the audio thread, the curve reads the same ring `SpectrumRing` as the meters; the analysis is `shell-mpc/overlay/spectrum_curve.h` on the overlay thread, and it runs only while that tab is on screen, once per picture (about 30 a second, and not at all in silence).
  - FFT of 4096 samples with a Hann window, hopping by one picture (about 33 milliseconds, so a new analysis overlaps the last by about 65 percent). At 44.1 kHz the window is 93 milliseconds and a bin is 10.8 Hz: the partials of a note from about 40 Hz up are separate peaks (the 2048 samples of the meters would show the lowest octave as one lump), and a longer window would smear the attack of a note. The mean of the window is taken out first (a DC offset of the output is not sound and would show as a hump at the low end).
  - Level: `S[k]` is the power in the three bins of the Hann main lobe divided by 1.5 (the noise bandwidth of the window), so a sine gives its own amplitude whatever its distance from a bin centre (a plain bin read-out loses up to 1.4 dB between two bins), and the dB scale is the one of the meters (`kSpectrumFloorDb` at the bottom to `kSpectrumCeilingDb` at the top, the same constants, a full scale sine is over the top).
  - 256 points, log axis from 20 Hz to 20 kHz. A point narrower than a bin (the low end) is interpolated between the two bins. EVERY point takes the peak of `S` over its stretch, whatever its width (an earlier version took the mean of `S` over the wide points above about 3 kHz and crossfaded: it lowered every tone there and the curve drew visibly under the meters, which show the maximum of the bins of their band); a tone keeps its level wherever it lies between two points.
  - The height of the meters. The meter of a band shows the maximum of the bins of that band in a 2048 point analysis of the newest samples (`spectrumBandDb`); the curve computes exactly that value for each of the 32 bands (the same functions, without the meters' smoothing in time; the mean of that window is taken out as for the curve) and is tied to it: in each band the highest point of the curve is exactly the meter's value, and no point inside a band is above it. The gain needed at the highest point of a band (the two windows differ, a tone between two bins reads up to 1.4 dB low on a plain bin, and the skirts of a loud tone reach farther in the 2048 window than in the 4096 one) is applied to the whole band, so the shape inside a band, the order of its peaks and the valleys between partials are kept, and is blended linearly with the neighbouring band's gain over a stretch of one and a half points on either side of the band edge (no step in the picture). Points under 30 Hz or over 18 kHz take the gain of the first or the last band. Only the level is tied to the meters; the resolution of the 4096 point analysis is not given up.
  - Light smoothing across frequency: a point is raised towards the mean of itself and its two neighbours, never lowered (valleys between partials fill a little, every peak keeps its height). In time, per point: attack with a time constant of 20 milliseconds, release with 350 milliseconds, by the real time between two analyses; a point under 1/256 of the scale with a target at the floor goes to exactly 0.
  - Silence: a window whose largest sample is under -80 dB below full scale (far under the floor of the scale), or a ring that did not move (the host stopped calling), is not analysed (no FFT); the curve falls to the floor and, when it has settled there, the view reports "unchanged": no picture is drawn, the helper sleeps, the thread polls. The 32 display parameters and their reports are not touched.
  - Drawing: an opaque dark background over the whole row (the meters do not show through), faint grid lines at 100 Hz, 1 kHz and 10 kHz, a filled area under the curve with a vertical gradient (accent colour, strong under the line, almost transparent at the bottom) and a line 2.5 pixels thick in the accent colour, 30 pictures a second while the curve moves. The same picture from the GPU (a scene with the gradient fill, protocol version 2) and from the CPU (`draw.h`, `fillToBaselineGradient`); the geometry of the row is `meterRow` in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.json`, the one place the overlay reads (the host Meters that also read it are gone).
  - Tests: `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/spectrum_curve_host.cpp` (sines at several frequencies and levels, the worst offset of a tone between two points, the curve against the 32 meters of the same signal: twelve frequencies at four levels and a chord of seven partials, in every band the highest point of the curve is the meter's value within one row of the view and never above it, two sines, a DC offset, white noise, silence, a ring that stands still, the attack and release times, other sample rates, the audio thread pushing while the overlay thread analyses, also under ThreadSanitizer through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`), `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/spectrum_view_host.cpp` (the view and the controller: no data without a ring, the scene is the CPU picture, no picture at rest). The measured numbers are in "Known host behaviour".
- **Limits.** The spectrum is the sum of both output channels of the plugin, not the stereo image; the plugin's own effects are in it. A pure tone shows in the column of its band and a little in the neighbours (the Hann window). The audio callback gaps with the 32 columns are in "Known host behaviour" (Key 61 with the self-test wave and with a real chord, MPC XL with the self-test wave); the CPU cost of the analysis on its own (about one FFT of 2048 points per tick) has not been measured, nor has how the peak hold looks or the MPC XL with real sound in the test project.

## Q-Link frames and alignment

**Overlay skin (the release variant): `qlinkBoundsData` is `[]` on all 15 tabs** (22 before the Env, LFO and Filter merges) (`97_no_fallback.py`, 2026-11-21). The host draws its OWN Q-Link highlight (the frames and the numbered index badges of the Q-Link-mapped knobs, shown while a knob is touched or turned) from `qlinkBoundsData`; with the four fallback frames kept, the XL (16 physical knobs in one row, a 4 x 4 virtual editing grid) showed four stray yellow badges 3 / 7 and 4 / 8 INSIDE the Env and LFO graphs of the plugin's Play page while Q 9 (LFO 1 Shape) was turned: the x columns of the badges match the frames 3 (x 656 .. 944) and 4 (x 976 .. 1264), which cover the Env panel (x 440 .. 840) and the LFO panel (x 857 .. 1257) in x (the y of the badges is not derived from any document: verdict "host-drawn (likely)", the device check decides). `[]` is valid ("Use `[]` to draw no highlight", `ui-reference.md` 3.6; the SDK validator accepts it). The plugin pages draw their own Q frames and 1 to 12 badges. The Q proxies and `Q-Links.json` are unchanged. The hardware strip showing the value (`0.23`) while a knob is turned is the host's own knob display, not ours. Not yet verified on a device (the open-items file).

## Control names


On the Key 61 (MPC 3.10.1.12), a static native `Text` label needed a valid `Data` binding to appear on a plugin tab.

## Function keys and sub-tabs

The skin (`skin/TUI.json`, `skin/defs_<family>.json`, `skin/Q-Links.json`) is edited directly.

**Where the definitions live.** `skin/TUI.json` holds the tabs, `info` and `replacementStrings`; its `importFiles` lists the definition files (flat, at the skin root next to the PNGs, so image paths resolve) and its `localComponentDefinitions` is empty. Each `skin/defs_<family>.json` has the wrapped shape `{"componentDefinitions": {"version": 2, "importFiles": [], "localComponentDefinitions": [...]}}` (the unwrapped SDK shape does not load). The family is the screen group that uses the definition (via the tab instances and the definitions they reference); a definition used by several families, or by none, without a family key prefix, is in `defs_common.json`. Keys must stay unique across all files (a local definition would win over an imported one). To add a family: create `skin/defs_<name>.json` and list it in `importFiles`; the validator rejects a listed file that is missing or not wrapped, and a `defs_*.json` that is not listed (the host drops it silently). Rule: edit the family file, then run `python3 scripts/skin-format.py`. The validator, the schema check and the baseline (`scripts/skin-baseline-update.py`) work on the merged union of all definitions; the package copies the whole `skin/` folder, so the files are in the package and in the install hash list (`find 'Plugin Skins' -type f`).

**Screen components.** A screen that repeats is ONE component: the tab holds an instance of it, the plugin binds the shown oscillator, envelope or LFO to it, so the Parameter handles of the screen are the same for every tab of the family. `oscScreen` (+ `oscScreenTop`) serves Osc A, Osc B, Osc C, Sub and Noise; `envScreen` (+ `envScreenTop`) serves Env 1 .. Env 6; `lfoScreen` (+ `lfoScreenTop`, `lfoScreenTop2`, `lfoScreenTop3`) serves LFO 1 .. LFO 10 and RND 1 .. RND 4; `filterScreen` (+ `filterScreenTop`) serves Filter 1 and Filter 2. The `...Top` parts are the runs of shared items that sit above the per-tab items (marker squares, captions, sentinels) in the z-order; every tab of a family holds the instances in that order. Play, Macros, Mixer, FX, Mod, Note, Velo, Master, Tuning and Voice are one tab body each. The per-screen pages (what a screen shows, its definitions, its data, its Q groups, its C++ files) are in [docs/ui-components/screens/](ui-components/screens/README.md). `scripts/validate_synthyboy_skin.py` merges the import files and checks that every tab of a family holds its screen instances in order (`SCREEN_FAMILIES`); `scripts/skin-format.py` formats every `skin/*.json`; `scripts/skin-baseline-update.py` writes the key baseline `scripts/skin-baseline/TUI.keys.json` from the merged definitions.

The skin groups its tabs under function keys. Several tabs with the same `fnKeyIndex` and the sub-tab indexes `fnKeySubIndex` 0, 1, 2 ... are sub-tabs of one key; the host steps to the next sub-tab when the key is pressed again, the key shows the `tabName` of the current sub-tab, and `Q-Links.json` has an entry with `Tab` = `fnKeyIndex` + 1 and `SubTab` = `fnKeySubIndex` + 1 for every one of them (observed on the Key 61: sub-tabs work; the SDK allows at most 8 sub-tabs per key and says "at most 32 tabs in total", see below).

- **The overlay layout** (`skin/TUI.json`) has 37 tabs in 10 function keys, grouped into 14 screen components (4 repeating, 10 unique) (the screens: [docs/ui-components/screens/README.md](ui-components/screens/README.md)). Function keys and their sub-tabs, in order: `Play` (Play, Macros); `Osc` (Osc A, Osc B, Osc C, Sub, Noise); `Env` (Env 1 .. Env 6: one tab page = one screen, the sub-tab Env n shows envelope n and has no selector; the view `envn` of `overlay_layout.json` has the index n and the page takes it from `page::Input::instance`; the Play page keeps its own selection `Play Env` and the expanded Play editor its selector strip); `LFO` (LFO 1 .. LFO 8) and `LFO 9` (LFO 9, LFO 10, RND 1, RND 2, RND 3, RND 4): the 14 sources are the sub-tabs of TWO function keys (the SDK allows 8 sub-tabs a key, `SUB_LIMIT` of `95_subtabs.py`); one tab page = one screen, the sub-tab shows its LFO (1 .. 10) or random LFO (RND n, the views `rnd1` .. `rnd4`), the view `lfo<n>` / `rnd<m>` has the index n (1 .. 14: the RND m is 10 + m) and the page takes it from `page::Input::instance`; there is no selector cell and no dropdown on the page (the handle `LFO Reserved` of the LFO block is never published) and the page shares nothing with the Play selection (`Play LFO`, `mpcTabView.rnd`: the Play page keeps its carousel and its own `selectLfo`); the second key text is the tab name `LFO 9`; `FX` (FX); `Mix` (the tab Mix, view `mixer`: one screen of ten strips, no peek tab bar); `Filter` (Filter 1, Filter 2, Note, Velo: one tab page = one screen, the views `filter1`, `filter2`, `filternote`, `filtervelo` have the index 1 .. 4 = the state of the page; there is no in-screen F1 | F2 | NOTE | VELO selector, no 60/40 split and no expand toggle; the chunk view state `mpcTabView.filter` is neither read nor written by these pages; the F1+F2 curve view toggle stays on the filter pages); `Mod` (Mod); `Master` (Master, Tuning, Voice: the sub-tab Master is the MAIN side, Tuning the TUNING & MPE side of the Master page, view `tuning` has index 2; no side buttons). The key text of Env, LFO, `LFO 9` and Filter is the tab name (no label). The view names `env1`, `lfo1` and `filter1` belong to the tabs Env 1, LFO 1 and Filter 1. With 37 tabs it is above the SDK's limit of 32 tabs (`tools/validate.py` reports exactly that finding, which `scripts/build-mpc.sh` accepts for the page count of the layout file, and the layout file names `"tabLimit": 37`) but below what the devices loaded: a skin with 39 tabs in 11 function keys loaded on the Key 61 and on the XL (2026-11-14, below) (the host's own limit is therefore not 32). The page detector holds 40 views (`Detection::kMaxViews`, `kMaxPages` of `kit/page_set.h`, `kNumBackgroundTabs`): per frame at most 10 pixel reads a view (two markers of 5 samples) and the instance picture, about 370 reads with 37 views. The views `env2` - `env6` (tabs `Env 2` - `Env 6`), `filter2` - `filtervelo`, `tuning` and the 13 LFO / RND views (`lfo2` - `lfo10`, `rnd1` - `rnd4`) have marker colours of the second bank: left `RR5a2c`, right `5aRR64` with `RR` = 0x10 + 7k, distinct from every marker of the first bank by far more than the detector's tolerance.

## MPC XL pads

The measurement was done on an MPC XL running MPC OS 3.10.1.12, using a diagnostic build of the plugin with callback statistics and MIDI counters (format 2). The owner pressed the pads by hand because pad input cannot be simulated remotely. The plugin was on a plugin track in the `Sbtest` project.

| | Full Level on | Full Level off |
|---|---|---|
| note on / note off | 19 / 19, channel 1 | 16 / 16, channel 1 |
| note-on velocity | always 127 | varied (96 and 127 seen) |
| poly aftertouch | 0 | 112 messages, values 1..127, one stream per held pad (per note), about one message every 6 milliseconds |
| channel pressure | 0 | 0 |
| pitch bend | 0 | 0 |
| CC (including CC 74) | 0 | 0 |
| channel | 1 | 1 |

What this means: the host passes note, velocity and per-pad pressure (poly aftertouch) to an external plugin; the X/Y position of a pad does not arrive in any form (no pitch bend, no CC 74, no other CC) and there is no MPE channel rotation; with Full Level on neither velocity nor pressure passes.

The host asked `effCanDo` only for `"bypass"` (answer 0) and `"receiveVstMidiEvent"` (answer 1), never for `"MPE"`, and made no `effVendorSpecific` call.

Poly aftertouch drives the modulation source "Poly Aftertouch"; the factory preset "MPE Pressure Lead" routes it to Filter 1 Cutoff and Osc 1 Unison Detune, and on the device pressing harder audibly opened the filter (heard by the owner, not measured).

Not measured: the MPCe Q-Link mode from a drum track to plugin parameters, 16 Levels, Note Repeat, pad lighting from a plugin.

## Tab view state (`mpcTabView`, chunk only)

Step 1 of the Env / Filter / LFO merges (the Filter number of the member was retired with the Filter sub-tabs). The selector of the merged tabs (`ui::UsedSelector`, `docs/ui-components/used-selector.md`) needs a selection that survives a project reload:

- **Env and LFO use the existing Play selectors** `Play Env` (1 to 6) and `Play LFO` (1 to 10), chunk members `mpcPlayEnv` / `mpcPlayLfo`: the tab and the Play page share ONE selection, no new host parameter.
- **The random LFO state** is a chunk-only field `"mpcTabView":{"rnd":0}`: `rnd` 0 to 4 (0 = a standard LFO is edited, 1 to 4 = the random LFO is; `Play LFO` keeps its last standard number). A member of an older chunk with a `"filter"` number is still read (the number is ignored: the Filter page state is the hardware sub-tab). No host parameter, no engine parameter, no preset content (`shell-mpc/tab_view.h`, `IInstanceView::tabView / setTabView`, `PluginInstance::tabViewState`).
- **Where in the chunk.** Right after the Play selectors, before `mpcOutputLimiter` and the pad pressure members (their readers look at the tail; `tabViewFromChunk` reads the last 400 bytes, the Play reader the last 320, both with room to spare). `lfo_live_host` strips the chunk from the Play selectors up to the limiter member, so the new member is stripped with them and the overlay chunk stays comparable to the other builds' chunk.
- **Compatibility.** The state version of the document is unchanged (the member is a shell member, like the Play selectors). A chunk WITHOUT the member (an older project) loads with the default (0); a member that is not well formed or out of range is refused and gives the defaults. An older plugin build reading a chunk with the member ignores it (unknown top-level member).
- **Project reload vs program change (decided by reading the code).** The plugin tells them apart by the opcode: `effSetChunk` restores the view state exactly (the saved selection is shown as "being edited" even when unused, because the selector always shows the selected item); `effSetProgram` leaves the selectors and the view state as they are. No snapping to the first used item: the selector shows the selected item whether or not the new preset uses it ("the selected item is always shown"), and a snap would write a selector behind the user's back.
- **No writes.** Choosing an item (`uibind::chooseEnv / chooseLfo`) sets view state only; no engine parameter is written until the user edits a control or adds a target. Tests: `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/ui_used_selector_host.cpp` (list, `+` choices, no writes, shared selection, the chunk functions) and `shell-mpc/tests/play_restore_host.cpp` (round trip through the plugin, old chunk, out of range, program change).

## NOTE / VELO curves (content, step 5)

The curves the sources `Note` and `Velocity` are read through are CONTENT of the state, not parameters: `content.curves.velocity` / `.note` (`StateContent::velocityCurve` / `noteCurve`, up to 64 points of x, y, bend power, an empty list is the identity; `core/include/synthyboy/content_state.h`). Only `curve.velocity.legato` and `curve.note.legato` are parameters (glide time: `master.portamento.time`). The editor (`ui::ScalarCurveEditor`, `docs/ui-components/scalar-curve-editor.md`) writes the points through `IInstanceView::setScalarCurve`: the overlay thread posts the request (the latest per source wins), the display thread (`scalarCurvePoll`) puts it into the content under `contentMutex`, runs `validateContent` (a curve it refuses is not applied, the old one stays), gives it to the engine (`engine->setContent`, baked by `own::ContentHub::publish` into a 2048 entry table) and to the overlay's copy. The chunk keeps the content like every other content: nothing is added to the shell members. Test: `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/scalar_curve_ui_host.cpp` (save, load, second save byte identical, the engine's evaluation equals the editor's at 128 keys). The editor is a component; the Filter tab embeds it as its NOTE and VELO states in step 4.

**In the Filter tab (step 4, 2026-11-21).** The editor is the NOTE and VELO state of the Filter tab (`filter_ui.h`): one `ScalarCurveUi` per source (VELO = source 23, NOTE = source 24), TARGETS opens the `ModTargetsPage` of the source. The Q groups are EDIT [POINT, X, Y, CURVE] (the virtual targets 0 .. 3) and LEGATO AND GRID [LEGATO, GRID X, GRID Y, SNAP] (the legato parameter and the virtual targets 4 .. 6; the Filter page has no virtual target of its own). The NOTE axis is named by `ui::keyName` (`note_mask.h`, the MPC convention key 60 = C3, key 0 = C-2: C-2, C1, C3, C5, G8). The live marker: the x of the key / velocity of the last note-on, `IInstanceView::lastPlayed (which)` (`PluginInstance::lastNoteOnKey / lastNoteOnVelocity`, one relaxed atomic store per source in `effProcessEvents`, no lock, no allocation), drawn by `ScalarCurveCanvas::drawLive` on the animated layer (the cached cells do not change). The request -> `scalarCurvePoll` -> `validateContent` -> content -> chunk path is proven in a live plugin by `shell-mpc/tests/scalar_curve_chunk_host.cpp` (debug hook `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_SCALAR_CURVE_FILE`, in `shell-mpc/accept.sh`); what stays for the device is the real touch, the sound and the marker.

## Pad pressure

The MPC pad sends polyphonic aftertouch for every held pad ("MPC XL pads"). The plugin turns it into an opening of Filter 1 by default, for every preset, without changing what a preset contains:

- **What it does.** The own engine (the engine of the MPC package) adds, per voice, an offset to the Filter 1 cutoff: pressure 0 to 1 of that voice (poly aftertouch value divided by 127) times the amount times 36 semitones, added in the control domain of the cutoff (the same domain the modulation matrix uses, `core/engine-own/voice/voice.cpp`). Only the cutoff of Filter 1: not Filter 2, not the frame, the level, the vibrato or the unison. It comes on top of the knob and of any modulation slot of the patch (if a preset's modulation matrix already contains a route from Poly Aftertouch to Filter 1 Cutoff, such as "MPE Pressure Lead", the layer does not add to that voice, preventing a duplicate effect). The pressure of each voice is smoothed with a one pole filter of 12 milliseconds, so the stream of values the pad sends (about one every 6 milliseconds) moves the cutoff in fine steps and does not make it zipper. A note starts with its first pressure value as it is. The layer is a function of the pad pressure only: the knob, the presets and the saved state of the engine are not written to. It costs nothing while the amount is zero or the switch is off (no smoothing, no addition), and a voice does not leave the envelope cache of the engine for it (`core/engine-own/own_engine.h`, `setOwnPadPressureToCutoff`).
- **Polyphonic vs Channel Aftertouch (0xA0 vs 0xD0).** The layer reacts strictly to polyphonic aftertouch (0xA0, per-voice/per-pad pressure). Channel aftertouch (0xD0), which keyboard instruments such as the MPC Key 61 may send from their keys, is currently ignored by the pad pressure layer; measurement from the designer's test rig is pending, and until then the layer reacts strictly to 0xA0.
- **Two parameters.** "Pad Pressure" (off or on, display "Off" / "On") and "Pad Pressure Amount" (0 to 100 percent, display "35") come after the 32 "Spectrum n" columns: indices 2673 and 2674 (2637 engine parameters, 4 cursors, 32 spectrum columns); in every build except the overlay build they are the last two parameters of the list (the overlay build has "Overlay Instance" after them, see "Parameter order"). Both are automatable and have parameter properties (a switch; a step of 1 percent). No index before them moves. A build without the LFO display has them at 2637 and 2638, one without the spectrum at 2641 and 2642; the overlay build refers to the indices of the default build.
- **Parameter order (fixed).** The parameter list of the MPC shell is, in this order: the engine parameters (`docs/params.json`, 2637), 4 "LFO n Cursor" (2637 .. 2640), 32 "Spectrum n" (2641 .. 2672), "Pad Pressure" (2673), "Pad Pressure Amount" (2674), and the overlay block (always built in, in this order): "Overlay Instance" (2675), the three Play selectors "Play Osc", "Play Env", "Play LFO" (2676 .. 2678), the 24 Play proxies (2679 .. 2702, group after group: oscillator, envelope, LFO, eight each) and the sixteen Q proxies "Q 1" .. "Q 16" (2703 .. 2718, see "Q proxies and the display pages"). This is the 2719-parameter prefix. The native UI handles follow it: their blocks, their order and their count (`nativeui::kCount`) are defined in one place, `shell-mpc/native_ui_params.h`; the skin refers to them by `Parameter <2719 + offset>` and `scripts/validate_synthyboy_skin.py` checks that no handle reaches the plugin parameter count.
- **Pad pressure in the overlay.** The overlay draws the Filter 1 curve from the engine's readout of the modulated cutoff (`copyReadout`, `setReadoutTargets`). The readout of a knob is the value as the voice that sounded last hears it (`Voice::modulatedParam`), and the pad pressure layer adds its offset to exactly that modulation delta, so the displayed cutoff and the curve include the pad pressure contribution of that voice, without changing the sound (the readout only reads; test `pad_pressure_is_in_the_filter_1_cutoff_readout` in `core/tests/test_own_pad_pressure.cpp`). With several held notes the curve shows the voice played last, like for every other modulation. Channel aftertouch (0xD0) is not used by the layer, so it does not move the curve either.
- **Defaults (changed 2026-11-16).** OFF, 35 percent. Pressure is the PRESET's business now: a saw pad wants it, a punk bass does not, so the plugin-wide layer no longer opens Filter 1 on every sound. The factory presets that suit it (held pads, leads, held keys, one drone: 49 of 112) carry the route Poly Aftertouch -> Filter 1 Cutoff in their own matrix (the route is stored in the preset files (the scripts that proposed and wrote it were removed on 2026-11-28); a few demo presets have 70 percent); the layer skips a voice whose preset has that route (no doubling). The layer itself stays for whoever wants pressure on every sound: switch it on (the host's Master tab of the production skin, or PAD PRESS on the overlay Master page, VOICES block) and set the amount (PAD %, 35 percent: a full pressure opens the cutoff by about one octave, 100 percent three). The two parameters and the chunk members did not move; a project saved with the layer ON keeps it ON (the chunk members win over the default; only a project without them gets the new default OFF). The settings are not part of a preset, a program change leaves them alone. The pad's Full Level must be off (below), or the pad sends no pressure at all.
- **State.** The two settings are saved in the state chunk as two members at the end of the document (`"mpcPadPressure":1,"mpcPadPressureAmount":35.00`, `shell-mpc/pad_pressure.h`) and restored with it. A chunk without them (a project saved before this feature) leaves the settings as they are. The chunk is the same with and without the display parameters.
- **Full Level must be off.** With the pad's Full Level on, every hit has velocity 127 and the pad sends no pressure at all ("MPC XL pads"), so the layer has nothing to follow. The skin says so beside the controls.
- **Skin.** Master MAIN binds native PAD PRESS and PAD % to the shell parameters at indices 2673 and 2674. PAD % is visible only while PAD PRESS is on. Switching the layer off preserves the amount.
- **Build options (CMake).** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PAD_PRESSURE` (default ON in `shell-mpc`; the source default is 0: a build that does not define it has no pad pressure code, no parameter, no string of it and no chunk member). The skin refers to the parameters, so the MPC package must be built with it ON. 
- **Tests.** `core/tests/test_own_pad_pressure.cpp` (the layer off is bit identical to a stream without pressure, it opens the filter more with a larger amount, only the voice that is pressed, only Filter 1, the step response of the smoothing, no zipper in the stream of 1/127 steps, the cutoff returns to the knob, no doubling when the mod matrix routes Poly Aftertouch to Cutoff, MPE Pressure Lead audio byte-identical with layer on vs off); `shell-mpc/tests/pad_pressure_host.cpp` (the plugin: indices, names, defaults, properties, writes, byte-identical chunk round trip and an older chunk, a program change, no doubling with mod matrix route, the audio with a pressed note, the build without the feature; also under ThreadSanitizer) through `shell-mpc/accept.sh`; the skin test of the host controls was deleted with them on 2026-11-21.
- **Not measured on a device.** How the opening of the filter sounds from a pad, whether the default amount is right on the MPC XL and the Key 61, and the CPU of the layer with a full pad bank; see "Known host behaviour" for what has been measured so far.

## Output level

Every shell passes the engine's output through ONE output stage, `core/include/synthyboy/output_stage.h` (`synthyboy::OutputStage`): the MPC shell (through `shell-mpc/output_stage.h`, which adds the plugin setting LIMIT) and the offline renders that play what a shell plays. The stage is:

- a make-up gain, **`synthyboy::kMakeupGainDb` = 0 dB** (unity), the one value of every shell, smoothed over 20 milliseconds when it changes. **The rule (the owner, 2026-11-20): unity gain staging, stock-like headroom, no limiter, no guard.** With every level control at 0 (the preset's `master.volume` aside, the host fader at 0 dB) the track output carries the engine's output as it is: no hidden gain, no limiter, no latency. The level lives in the presets' `master.volume` and per-profile `tierTrimDb` (`docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md`): single note C3 at velocity 127 targets stock instrument level parity across all categories (see `docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md` for the exact measured stock reference numbers and chord headroom targets), and every chord at velocity 127 stays below full scale without limiter on both profiles (`low` Key 61 and `high,voices=16,control=economy` XL). Master volume is set from the XL solve, and any difference on the Key 61 is trimmed by the top-level preset member `tierTrimDb` (applied via `OutputStage::setGainDb` on Key 61 only). Every preset keeps at least 5 dB room up to +6 dB (`master.volume <= +1.0 dB`). Velocity tracking is fully linear for all presets; note that pad Full Level sends fixed velocity 127 from the hardware on every hit (lines 705-717). Existing saved projects do not migrate automatically; users must reload presets to apply updated levels.
- a **stereo-linked look-ahead peak limiter** behind it (section "Output limiter"), **off by default in every shell**: the user's choice (LIMIT on the MPC), or a preset's own (the member `outputLimiter` of a `.sbpreset`, read when the program is loaded). When it is on, the 3 milliseconds of look-ahead are added to the latency each wrapper reports.

Why no limiter and why headroom: stock MPC instruments maintain headroom rather than driving into a ceiling (see `docs/release/OUTPUT-LEVEL.md` for stock instrument measurements; no stock instrument clips). With engine voice-count gain alpha = 0.5 (1/√n), polyphonic chords naturally sum without excessive crest factor, allowing the output stage to run cleanly at unity gain without limiter or guard. The engine's safety stage (`core/engine-own/dsp/master_stage.h` `outputLimit`: identity up to 1.8, tanh knee towards 2.0) sits before the shell stage.

The spectrum of the MPC shell shows the signal after the output stage, that is what the host gets. Tests: `shell-mpc/tests/run_output_stage_test.sh` (the stage alone, the NEON form against the scalar one, the setting), `core/tests/test_output_stage_presets.cpp` (every factory preset through the stage) and `shell-mpc/tests/output_level_host.cpp` (the plugin against a build with 0 dB, limiter forced off and on); `shell-mpc/accept.sh` runs the first and the last.

### Output limiter

- **The design** (`synthyboy::OutputStage`, the comment at the top of the header has the details). After the make-up gain every sample goes into a delay line of the look-ahead, **3 milliseconds** (132 samples at 44.1 kHz, 144 at 48 kHz, 288 at 96 kHz). A detector sees the samples as they enter: the gain a sample needs is 1 under the ceiling, else the ceiling over its peak (the louder of the two channels: one gain for both, the stereo image does not move); the minimum of that over the look-ahead window (a streaming van Herk / Gil-Werman minimum, constant work per sample); a two-stage release; then two moving averages that together span the look-ahead window (W = D + 1 samples: B1 + B2 - 1 = W), so the gain falls along an S-shaped ramp that ends **exactly** at the peak when the peak leaves the delay line: no overshoot, no step. The output is the delayed sample times that gain; a hard clip at the ceiling behind it is the guarantee for anything the design does not cover (rounding, input above 2^60): in the tests it never acts.
- **Constants.** Ceiling **-0.5 dB full scale** (0.94406): close to full scale, half a decibel of room for the inter-sample peaks a DAC reconstructs and for a host stage after the plugin that rounds or adds a little (the MPC's track and master stages). Look-ahead 3 milliseconds: the attacks of the factory presets (velocity 127 chords) take from under a millisecond (Fast Arp Bass, Pitch Drop 808, Tine Glass Keys) to about 30 milliseconds from the onset to their crest; 3 milliseconds is the upper end of what is inaudible as latency for a played instrument and makes the gain ramp long enough that it does not click on a low note, and the overloads that come sooner than that come from silence, where the ramp has nothing to change. Release in two stages: the fast stage releases with **30 milliseconds** but only down to the slow stage; the slow stage is the reduction a lasting overload needs: it follows the needed reduction up at a rate that grows with how long the overload has lasted (the weight `s` rises with 300 milliseconds while overloads come within 25 milliseconds of each other, and falls with 300 milliseconds when they stop; at full weight the slow stage follows with 20 milliseconds) and releases with **300 milliseconds**. So a drum hit or a pluck recovers fast (the slow stage has hardly moved), a held chord or a bass held over the ceiling brings the slow stage to the reduction its crests need and the gain does not move at the rate of the waveform, and its release after the overload is slow; a pluck followed by a held sound recovers from its attack down to what the held part needs, so the loudest 400 milliseconds of a sound never fall as the make-up gain rises. The make-up gain is smoothed over 20 milliseconds; a switch of LIMIT crossfades over 10 milliseconds.
- **Off and idle.** While nothing needs a reduction the detector is not run (the "idle" path, the NEON form on ARM) and the output is the gained input delayed by exactly the look-ahead, to the bit. With LIMIT off the output is the gained input **without** the delay (latency 0), to the bit; the detector keeps running so that switching on finds the reduction already computed. A switch crossfades the two paths (no click; switching on, the crossfade is clipped at the ceiling).
- **Cost.** No division (the reciprocal of the peak is an integer seed and three Newton steps from below) and no libm call per sample; the coefficients are made in `prepare`. The idle path costs a gain multiply and the delay line: NEON on ARM, four samples at a time, the same samples as the scalar loop to the bit (`output_stage_lanes_host.cpp`, also with flush-to-zero). Measured figures: "Observed on the device" has none yet; the Mac figures are in the report of the branch `work/loudness-fix` and in the output of `output_stage_host` (TIMING line).
- **Latency.** The look-ahead is reported to the host while the limiter is on, where the host has a field. MPC shell: `AEffect::initialDelay` is the engine's latency (Bode's frequency shifter) plus the look-ahead while LIMIT is on; set when the instance is made and at every `effSetSampleRate`; any change (the switch, a chunk, a program with its own `outputLimiter`, the engine's latency) marks it stale, and the next dispatcher call that is not `effProcessEvents` (so never on the audio thread) writes the field and sends `audioMasterIOChanged` (`PluginInstance::latencyDirty`, `latencyNow`). Whether the MPC compensates `initialDelay` for an instrument track is not known; 3 milliseconds are inaudible for a played synth.
- **The switch.** Master MAIN displays a native **LIMIT** toggle for the output stage. It has no Q-Link assignment. The default is **OFF** (`kOutputLimiterDefaultOn`).
- **Where it is stored.** The setting rides in the state chunk as `"mpcOutputLimiter":0|1`, before the pad pressure members. A chunk without this member keeps the current setting. When a program is loaded, its optional `"outputLimiter":1` enables the stage. Init and programs without the member use OFF. The native switch at index 2983 reflects the running stage and uses the same instance setter as the overlay-only screen.
- **Forcing it for tests.** `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OUTPUT_LIMITER` in the environment (read when the instance is opened): `1` forces the limiter on, `0` off, anything else leaves it to the setting. `output_level_host` sets the variable for the state it measures. While it is forced the toggle shows the state the stage runs.
- **Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/output_stage_host.cpp`: the gain alone when off (to the bit, no delay), transparency when on (to the bit, delayed by the look-ahead, also after a limited passage), the ceiling (bursts, steps, noise, a single spike, NaN, infinities, huge values, mono, one buffer for both channels, the first samples after a reset, 44.1 / 48 / 96 kHz, any block size), the attack (the crest comes out at the ceiling with exactly the needed gain, the gain falls over the look-ahead before it), the gain curve (its second difference bounded: no zipper; a held chord's gain does not rise by more than a third of a decibel in any 5 millisecond step), the two release stages measured from the gain trace, the distortion of a held sine 3 dB over the ceiling (100 Hz and 1 kHz), the loudness order over make-up gains (tested over 0 to 18 dB, historic sweep), latency and reset, switching on and off while playing (no step larger than the signal's own plus the crossfade), the chunk member and the environment; `output_stage_lanes_host.cpp`: NEON = scalar to the bit; `core/tests/test_output_stage_presets.cpp` (`synthyboy_test_output_stage_presets`, label `slow`): the 160 factory presets, a velocity 127 chord and a single note, the Key 61 and XL profiles, at make-up gains 2, 4, 6, 9 and 15 dB: no sample above the ceiling, every sample finite, no safety clip, no loudness inversion beyond 0.15 LU; it prints the gain reduction per category.

There is no separate parameter for the level of the instrument: that is Master Volume (first knob of the second row of the Macros tab), which is saved with the project.

## Overlay (experimental)

Live pictures on seventeen tabs (the three views of the Play page included), drawn by the plugin itself on a display plane that the MPC firmware does not offer to plugins: the response curve of Filter 1 and Filter 2, the shape of LFO 1 - 4 with the cursor of the playing voice, (the oscillator pictures of this first overlay are gone: the OSC tab is now drawn entirely by the plugin, see "The OSC tab"), the live phase marker of Envelope 1 - 4 on top of the native envelope widget, and the spectrum of the plugin's output as a curve over the row of meters of the Macros tab. EXPERIMENTAL, off by default; proven on the MPC Key 61 (32-bit) and on the MPC XL (64-bit) with the same code. The skin alone cannot do this: the SDK widgets have no curve that follows the real filter, no LFO shape with modulation and smoothing, no oscillator output with warp and morph (the `Envelope` widget draws broken lines from a few stage values, see "Envelope tabs").

**What it is.** The panel is driven by a display controller with several planes: the whole UI of the application on one (the primary plane), a few the application uses for itself (a cursor, a small sprite) and some that are free and sit above the UI (on the Key 61: UI 33, cursor 35, free 38 and 40, CRTC 37; on the XL: UI 34, an application sprite 40, free 46, 52, 58, 64, 70 and 76, CRTC 82). The plugin lives inside the MPC process, which holds the display device (`/dev/dri/card0` on both) as DRM master. The overlay module (`shell-mpc/overlay/`, always compiled in) finds the CRTC and a free plane at run time ("Discovery") and puts an ARGB picture on that plane over the region of the view whose tab is on screen; the host's own UI keeps working underneath (it still gets every touch). Only one view is visible at a time (one plane), of ONE instance at a time: with several plugin instances (several tracks) the one whose page is on screen is drawn ("Several instances"). Every picture comes from the real DSP code of the engine, not from a drawing of its own: the filter curve from `FilterResponse` (`core/include/synthyboy/filter_response.h`), the LFO shape from `LfoDisplay` (`core/include/synthyboy/lfo_display.h`, the shape function `Voice::lfoValue` calls), the oscillator cycle from `OscCycle` (`core/include/synthyboy/osc_cycle.h`, the oscillator class of the voice), the envelope phase from the engine's readout.

**Views.** A view is a region of a tab, a pair of marker squares and a data source with a draw function (`shell-mpc/overlay/views.h`). They are listed with their numbers in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.json`.

- `Filter` (region at the top of the tab; the view `filter1`, ONE tab with the states F1 and F2 since 2026-11-21): the response of the filter for the knobs, and for the live (modulated) cutoff and resonance of the voice that sounded last. With nothing playing, macros, the mod wheel and the other sources that have a value without a voice move the readout too (engine: the idle readout); a modulation by an LFO or an envelope needs a voice. A filter that is switched off draws a flat line.
- `LFO 1` - `LFO 4` (the picture region of the tab): the shape as the engine plays it (built-in or custom shape with its point modulation, swing, direction, the phase offset, the trigger type, the smoothing), a smooth line with a fill. The x axis is the time since the note started, in cycles, so the phase offset shifts the picture; a cursor (vertical line and a dot on the curve) runs at the live phase of the voice that sounds, and is hidden when none does. Not drawn: the delay and fade in, the stereo lane (the left lane is shown). The tempo for synced rates is the host's.
- `Osc 1` - `Osc 3` (the wavetable picture region): one cycle of what the oscillator produces for the current settings: the wavetable at the frame position including smooth or stepped reading, the frame remap, warp 1 and 2 and the spectral morph, following the modulated frame, warp amount and morph amount live. The type Wavetable is covered (the built-in tables and the preset table of the content); the types Sample, Granular, Spectral and Multisample have no single cycle: the view shows the empty frame, an oscillator that is off the same. The DATA for those types exists in the core since `OscCycle::computeDisplay` (`core/include/synthyboy/osc_cycle.h`: the min / max overview of a sample or of the zone of a multisample with the start, end and loop positions, the grain window of the granular oscillator, the magnitude spectrum of the spectral oscillator on a logarithmic axis, the noise colour), tested in `core/tests/test_own_osc_displays.cpp`; the spectrum is computed by the very code the voice runs for a hop (`shapeFrame` in `core/spectral/src/spectral.cpp`, checked by `spectral_display_spectrum_is_what_the_voice_synthesizes`). The views, the scene protocol and the helper do not draw it yet. Not in the picture: unison, pan, level, the pitch parameters, the start phase, warps that read another oscillator.
- `Spectrum` (the row of the 32 meters of the Macros tab, 1208 x 136 pixels): the spectrum of the plugin's own output as a smooth filled curve, 256 points on a log axis from 20 Hz to 20 kHz on the dB scale of the meters; the analysis, the drawing and the tests are in "Spectrum" ("The curve over the meters"). The meters stay underneath; the markers and the instance picture are drawn in the 8 pixel gaps between two meters.

**Discovery (nothing of a model is compiled in).** The same code runs on both devices (`shell-mpc/overlay/discovery.h` holds the rules and is tested against the plane tables of both devices, `shell-mpc/overlay/drm_linux.h` reads the display, `shell-mpc/overlay/cpu_plan_linux.h` the cores). It happens when the screen is first read, and everything it finds is in the status file (`display_*`, `cpus_*`, `rotation*`, `pace_*`).

- The display and the CRTC: the application's descriptor is found by scanning `/proc/self/fd` for a link to a `/dev/dri/card*` device that has master rights (`DRM_IOCTL_AUTH_MAGIC` with magic 0 answers EINVAL to a master and EACCES to anybody else) and CRTCs. What is on the display is read through a descriptor of OUR OWN on the same device (universal planes are switched on there, a capability of that descriptor only; the application's descriptor never gets a client capability): the primary plane that is on a CRTC with a valid mode and shows a framebuffer is the application's UI plane, its CRTC is ours. A driver that reports no plane types: the CRTC with a mode and a framebuffer, and the plane that shows it.
- The overlay plane: free (no CRTC, no framebuffer), possible on that CRTC, offers ARGB8888, not a cursor plane, and stacking above every plane the application has on the CRTC (the `zpos` property when the driver has it; an overlay type plane stacks above the primary one by the hardware order otherwise). The lowest numbered one is used, the others are spares. The plane is checked through its reported state (`GETPLANE`: our CRTC and a framebuffer; the GPU helper also checks that the framebuffer is its own, once a second, right after an update): when another program takes the plane, or an update of it fails, the module drops what is its own WITHOUT switching that plane off (it is not ours any more), moves to the next spare and shows the picture there; with no spare left it disables itself with the reason. A plane that is found switched off (no CRTC and no framebuffer: a mode set of the application switches off every plane it does not name) is not taken but set again on the same plane (`display_plane_refreshes`), and a failure while the CRTC has no mode (the display is off) moves nothing. The helper also checks a picture that stays on the plane (once a second), so that a plane taken between two updates is found as well.
- **Stacking against the host's pointer (the zpos rule, `discovery.h` `chooseZpos`, `shell-mpc/overlay/stacking.h`).** The host keeps its mouse pointer on a small plane of its own. Measured on the MPC XL (2026-11-16): planes 34 (the host's picture, zpos 0), 40 (the host's pointer, 64x64, type Overlay, zpos 1) and 46 (ours, zpos 2); every plane has a `zpos` property that is a MUTABLE range 0 .. 7; with ours at 2 the pointer was hidden UNDER our picture; after setting our plane's zpos to 0 the kernel's normalised order was host 0, ours 1, pointer 2 (planes with equal zpos are ordered by plane object id, the lower id below) and the pointer was visible on top, the overlay kept working. On the Key 61 no plane has a `zpos` property and the pointer is on top by the hardware's own order (measured). The rule: when the driver has a MUTABLE `zpos` on our plane, the value is the LOWEST that puts our plane directly above the application's primary plane and below every other plane the application has on the CRTC (its pointer and sprite planes), by the kernel's order (zpos, then plane id): the XL table gives 0; a plane whose id is lower than the primary's needs the primary's zpos + 1, and none is possible when that is not below the others, when the range does not allow it, when the property is immutable (or missing: the Key 61), or when the primary plane or the order of another plane is unknown; then today's behaviour stays (above every application plane, as the discovery chose it). The lowest value is chosen even when the plane's own would do, because the pointer plane may be hidden at that moment (a plane with no framebuffer is not on the CRTC and is not in the list) and come later at a low zpos. The set is the property call `DRM_IOCTL_MODE_OBJ_SETPROPERTY` through the application's descriptor (the master), the values are read through our own; it is made ONCE when the plane is taken (CPU path: before the first `SETPLANE` of a shown picture; GPU path: before the helper is started), again whenever the plane is taken again after a loss (another plane after `nextPlane`, a helper restart) and when the plane was found switched off by a mode set of the application and is set again (CPU path: `planeWasSwitchedOff`; GPU path: the helper counts it in `PaceBlock::planeRefreshes` and the plugin takes the stacking again), because a mode set may have reset the property with the plane. The zpos the plane had at rest is remembered when the plugin first touches it and written back when the overlay releases the plane (the picture is hidden: `disablePlane`; the helper stopped, failed, or was told to quit: `cleanDisplay` -> `releaseStacking`; the plugin took the plane away after a helper failure), but NOT on a plane that was lost to another program (`abandonPlane`, a `SETPLANE` error of the helper on a plane that is not free): that plane is not ours to write to. A failing set or restore is not fatal: it is counted and written to the status text, the plane stays as it is. A crash of the whole application leaves the changed zpos on the plane until the next start (the next start reads it as its rest value; the rule gives the same target). **Not verified on a device:** the XL (the only device with a mutable `zpos`) was not available to this work; the rule and the guard are tested with the XL's table and a fake driver (`stacking_host`, `gpu_backend_host`), the Key 61 run shows the no-property path. Status file: `display_plane_zpos` (`rest 2, set 0`; `none (the plane has no zpos property)`; `unchanged (...)` with the reason; `set failed: ...`; `(restored)` after the release), `display_pointer_plane` (`above ours` = the pointer is visible, `covered by ours`, `none`, `unknown (no zpos on every plane)`), `display_plane_zpos_counts`.
- The panel size comes from the mode of the CRTC. The real refresh period is measured from the time stamps of forty consecutive vblanks (the number in the mode is wrong on the XL); the vblank is waited for with `DRM_IOCTL_WAIT_VBLANK` on the pipe of the CRTC (its index in the resource list).
- The rotation of the UI on the panel needs no table: the skin draws marker squares of colours that no other view uses (see "How it knows its page is on screen"), and the marker pair of the view on screen is looked for in the primary framebuffer under each of the four rotations (90, 270, 0 and 180 degrees); exactly one rotation may match, otherwise nothing is decided (`rotation: unknown`, `hidden: rotation ambiguous`) and the next check tries again. The result is kept for the life of the process (`rotation_source: found from the markers`). The file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-rot` (0, 90, 180 or 270) overrides it; removing the file brings the found one back.
- The cores: the overlay thread and the helper stay off the cores the kernel isolates (`/sys/devices/system/cpu/isolated`) and off the cores the audio threads of the application are pinned to (the threads whose name contains "audio", from `/proc/self/task/*/status`, when their affinity is narrower than the online set); re-read every ten seconds, the helper is set again when the cores change. The isolated cores are never used; the audio threads' cores are taken out only as long as other cores are left: the Key 61 pins AudioWorker0 to AudioWorker3 to its four cores, so there only the isolated cores 2 and 3 are kept off and the overlay shares 0 and 1 with the audio threads, while on the XL (AudioWorker0 to 3 on the cores 4 to 7) it runs on 0 to 3. When even that leaves nothing, nothing is changed.
- The GPU: the helper tries every `/dev/dri/renderD*` (the GPU drivers first, then the others; `SYNTHYBOY_OVERLAY_RENDER_NODE` names one first) until one gives a GBM device, an EGL display and an ES2 ARGB8888 configuration, and opens `libEGL`, `libgbm` and `libGLESv2` by their sonames, then their plain names, then the usual library directories (`SYNTHYBOY_OVERLAY_GL_DIR` first); the error lists what was tried.

**Pacing.** A plane update through the legacy call `SETPLANE` waits for the vblank (the thread that makes it is never the audio thread). The MPC XL panel really refreshes at a rate other than its mode says, and its plane update is not a fixed one vblank per picture: while the application's own UI is moving (it commits a frame of its own on the same CRTC at every vblank: a knob dragged, a page with live meters) updates called back to back alternate between waiting one and two vblanks (uneven pictures), and a call made a few milliseconds after a vblank gets the next vblank only part of the time, the later the less often. With a static page on the screen the same calls are exactly one vblank each, so what decides is the competition with the application's commits for the display's locks, which a blocking update holds while it waits. A call made the moment a vblank wait returns (nothing sleeping in between) is taken for the very next vblank in both cases, and with one call on every second vblank the intervals between the pictures are exactly two vblanks. So the pace is a GRID: the stride is the whole number of vblanks nearest to a thirtieth of a second (two on both devices: 30 pictures a second on a 60 Hz panel, a little over 28 on the XL), the helper (`shell-mpc/overlay/vblank_pacer.h`, `shell-mpc/overlay/vblank_linux.h`) draws just early enough, waits for the vblank counter of the next slot of the grid and calls `SETPLANE` at once; a picture that is late takes the next slot of the same grid; after a pause a new grid starts at the next vblank; where no vblank can be waited for a timer of a thirtieth of a second paces (`pace_mode: timer`). The CPU drawing does the same inside its plane update. Tried and not adopted (the measurements are in "Known host behaviour"): back-to-back updates, updates after a delay of a few milliseconds, the update from a thread other than the one that waits for the vblank, an update with the same framebuffer or a one pixel move, and the non-blocking atomic update of the plane only: the application's descriptor does have the atomic capability (an empty test-only request is accepted, which sets nothing), the non-blocking update returns at once, but when it reaches the screen cannot be measured without a completion event, and such an event would go into the application's own event queue; so the proven legacy path stays. Because the update holds the display's modeset locks while it waits, any other call that needs them (reading the CRTC, `GETPLANE`) waits too: the overlay thread therefore hands over the next scene FIRST in every step and schedules its steps from the start of the previous one, and the plugin leaves the checking of the plane to the helper while the helper draws.

**Build.** The overlay needs a skin made for it (the curve area, two marker pictures, the controls re-arranged), so the package is built with both: `scripts/build-mpc.sh --arch all` (the overlay and the overlay skin layout are the defaults since 2026-11-26) (both architectures in one package: the plugin and the GPU helper of each, `VST/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-arm32` and `VST/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-arm64`; with the option OFF the plugin is byte for byte the one without the module, `shell-mpc/accept.sh` compares the binaries). Against the main line (`origin/develop` `f88e533`, merged 2026-11-14) the production package is identical in everything but the plugin binary: the skin is byte for byte the same (checked by building both with `scripts/build-mpc.sh --arch arm32` in the same image and comparing the `Plugin Skins` folders; `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_overlay.py` keeps the production skin equal to the one the modules without the overlay module make), the package files the same. The arm32 plugin binary is NOT byte for byte the one of `origin/develop`, and cannot be with the overlay branch's engine: the display additions of the own engine are compiled in whether or not the overlay is on (the readout of an idle engine with the source mask of the modulation matrix, the finer envelope stages in `LiveValues`, the filter set-up shared with `FilterResponse`, the LFO display code), so five objects differ (`own_engine`, `voice`, `mod_matrix`, `fx_modules`, `synthyboy_mpc`; the last one only by the stack frame, `LiveValues` is 48 bytes larger). The sound is not changed (the core test suite passes on the merged state); making the binary identical would need these engine additions behind a compile switch, which they do not have. The numbers shared by the skin and the plugin (region of every view, marker squares and their colours, and `meterRow`, the geometry of the row of the 32 meters that the earlier host Meters read and whose bounding box is the region of the `spectrum` view) are in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.h` (edited directly) and, for the skin validator, in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.json`; keep the two in step by hand.


**Native page artwork.** The overlay skin uses the checked-in backgrounds and separate detector images listed in `shell-mpc/overlay/backgrounds_manifest.h`. The skin (`skin/TUI.json`) holds the PNGs verbatim and binds each page background in both focus states. Native controls occupy the host layer above that background. The display helper supplies the live overlay. The page detector uses the sentinel on the host framebuffer to decide when the helper can expose the host layer. See [MPC display](dsp-knowledge/mpc-display.md) for device measurements.

`python3 scripts/render-ui-gallery.py --export-backgrounds <directory>` copies the enabled production PNGs, including their detector images, without building the host gallery executable. `backgrounds_manifest.h` (edited directly) is the C++ view-to-background table.

**The skin variant.** `skin/TUI.json` (`"variant": "overlay"`) is the production layout with two more pages: `Filter 1` and `Filter 2` (curve area at the top, below it two rows of four controls: On, Model, Style, Drive and Cutoff, Resonance, Mix, Key Tracking; Q-Link map and frames match the two rows) with the sub-tabs `F1 Model` and `F2 Model` (Blend, Comb Blend Offset, Formant X, Y, Resonance, Spread, Transpose) under the same function key: 36 tabs, so every control of the production tabs is still reachable. The extras module `skin/TUI.json` does the work and does nothing unless `skin-meta.json` names that variant, so the production skin stays as it was (`scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_overlay.py` compares it byte for byte with a build without the module). The Macros tab is the production tab plus the two marker squares and the instance picture of the `spectrum` view, in the gaps between two of the 32 meters, which stay as they are. The LFO, Osc and Env tabs are the production tabs plus the two marker squares of their view on top of the existing pictures: the shape and wavetable pictures and the native envelope stay where they are and are what the player sees when the overlay is off or disabled. `scripts/build-mpc.sh` accepts the one validator finding about the number of tabs of the layout it builds (36 here, 34 for the default; any other count is still an error).

**How it knows its page is on screen.** The host does not tell a plugin which page is visible. The skin draws two small solid squares inside the region of every view, near its two ends, in colours that no other view uses (the markers: the colour pair says WHICH view is on screen, so the plugin needs no knowledge of tab names; the numbers in the JSON are checked against each other: no two marker colours are within the detector's tolerance). Ten times a second the plugin reads the pixels of the marker pairs of all views from the application's primary framebuffer (GETCRTC, GETFB2, dma-buf export, read only mapping on a descriptor of its own, the mappings cached by the identity of the buffer); both markers of a view present (within 2 in every colour channel at four corner samples and the centre) means that view's tab is on screen and not covered. The overlay is on another plane, so the markers stay readable while it covers them; the overlay's picture covers them (an opaque view has a dark background of its own over the whole region; the transparent envelope view paints only its two squares), so the player never sees them. A popup, a menu or another tab replaces or covers the markers and the overlay goes within one check.

**Several instances (which instance's page is on screen).** The host does not tell a plugin whose page is visible, so the SKIN does. Every plugin instance owns a SLOT, 0 to 15: the lowest free one is given out when the instance is created (`VSTPluginMain`) and kept until it closes (`effClose`); a slot is reused only after its instance closed, and no two live instances share one (`shell-mpc/overlay/instance_slots.h`, the table is in the controller under its lock). The slot is exposed as one more display-only parameter, "Overlay Instance": the LAST parameter of the list, after the two pad pressure parameters (index: engine parameters + 4 LFO cursors + 32 spectrum columns + 2 pad pressure parameters = 2675; "Parameter order" in the section "Pad pressure"), not automatable, not in the state chunk, writes ignored. Its value is the frame number of a film strip divided by 32 (a multiple of 1/32 is exact in a float, so the host's choice of frame cannot round to the neighbour): frame 0 is blank (nothing reported yet), frames 1 to 16 are the slots 0 to 15, frame 17 is "none" (an instance beyond the sixteenth), the rest is blank. The skin places on every tab with a view a tiny film strip `Image` (33 frames, 6 x 6 pixels, on the envelope tabs 4 x 4) bound to that parameter next to the two markers, inside the region of the view; every frame is one exact colour of the table in `shell-mpc/overlay/overlay_layout.json` (`instance`; the PNG settings are those of the marker pictures, and the colours are checked against every marker colour, the background and each other). The host draws the frame of THIS instance's value, so the colour the page detector reads from the primary framebuffer says which instance the page on screen belongs to; the overlay thread then reads the parameters and the live values of that instance only. The overlay is visible when the markers of a view match AND the instance picture has one of the 16 slot colours (all five sample pixels) AND that slot is alive; every other case is hidden, with the reason in the status file (`hidden: instance of the page not identified`, `hidden: instance slot N is not alive`, `hidden: instance has no slot (more than 16 instances)`, `hidden: instance closed`). The view objects keep cached data (curves, history, smoothing), so a view that last showed another instance is made new before it reads the new one. The instance picture is covered by the overlay like the markers (the dark picture of an opaque view, a painted square on the envelope views). A blank instance picture keeps the page of the active slot while its stack is open or for a grace time (`kBlankGraceMs`, `controller.h`) after the stack went from open to main at a page check: a page that comes over a full screen page of the Voice or FX tab finds the host still drawing the blank frame the instance reported while the full screen was up; the arriving page tells the instance at its first picture (`OscUi::update`, `setUiFullOpen (false)`) and the host redraws with the slot colour some checks later. A real popup (Preset panel, Plugin dialog) covers the markers, not only the instance picture, so it still hides (`hidden: no view on screen`). Test: `page_view_host.cpp` (Play arrival over the FX page, slow host redraw: no hidden tick). Measurement: see the Observed section, Several instances.

- Reports. The display thread reports the parameter with `audioMasterAutomate` once after the instance is created, when its value changes, and whenever the overlay thread asks (`IInstanceView::requestOverlayReport`, which only sets a flag; the call to the host is the display thread's, never the audio thread's and never the overlay thread's). The overlay thread asks all live instances when the markers of a view are on screen but the instance picture shows no valid colour (the host has not drawn it yet, or shows a stale frame until the parameter is reported): at once for the first time, then at gaps that start at 0.4 seconds and double up to 3.2 seconds while no valid colour is read, and the overlay stays hidden until one is read. A valid colour of a slot nobody holds, and the none colour, ask for nothing (the answer is complete). Pages without markers ask for nothing.
