- More than 16 instances: the instances beyond the sixteenth get no slot (their parameter says "none"); they never show the overlay, and they do not take a slot from the others. The slot of an instance is assigned at creation and does not change when another instance closes.
- A frame number that is not a slot colour (a blank frame, a colour that belongs to no slot, one of five sample pixels differing) is never taken for a slot.
- Debug: while `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-fake-slot` exists the plugin reports the slot named in it instead of its own: a number (0 to 15 is that slot, any other number the "none" frame), `none`, `blank` (frame 0), or `stale` (frame 0 until the overlay thread asks for a report, the real slot after that: what a host looks like that shows an old picture until the parameter is reported). It is read by the display thread once a second and at every report, and meant to show on a device with ONE instance that the host redraws the picture with the other colours and that the overlay hides when the colour belongs to a slot that is not alive; remove the file to get the real slot back.

**Threads and cost.** One thread for the whole process (`sb-overlay`, same scheduling as the display thread: `shell-mpc/thread_policy.h`, plus the cores of the audio threads and the cores the kernel isolates are taken out of its affinity, "Discovery"). The audio thread does nothing for it. The CPU drawing (the fallback; "GPU drawing in a helper process" below is the other renderer) makes 30 pictures a second while the curve changes and none while nothing changes (the plane keeps showing the last buffer); with the helper this thread only computes the data and hands scenes over. A full computation of the curve costs several milliseconds on the device, so the full computations are limited to a share of one core; between two of them, when only the cutoff moved, the last exact curve is translated along the logarithmic frequency axis and the exact one follows as soon as the budget allows, so the picture is exact again when the motion stops. The measured numbers are in "Known host behaviour", "Observed on the device".

**GPU drawing in a helper process.** With the build option `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_OVERLAY_GPU` (CMake: `AUTO` by default = on for an ARM cross build, `ON` / `OFF` force it) the pictures are drawn by the GPU of the device (the Key 61: Mali-T760, the XL: Mali-G610, both Mesa panfrost, OpenGL ES 2) instead of the CPU, in a SEPARATE PROCESS: a crash or a driver fault of the helper must not take the MPC application down, and Mesa's libraries are not loaded into the application. The CPU drawing stays in the plugin as the automatic fallback; with the helper missing, failing or switched off the behaviour is exactly the one described above.

- The helper is the executable `synthyboy-overlay-gpu-arm32` (`synthyboy-overlay-gpu-arm64` for the 64 bit build) in `VST/` next to the plugin binary; the plugin finds it relative to its own path (`dladdr`, `shell-mpc/overlay/service.h`). `scripts/build-mpc.sh` puts the helper of every architecture it built into the package when the target exists (overlay build on ARM, `--arch all` gives both); the production package has none, so it is unchanged byte for byte. Source: `shell-mpc/overlay/gpu-helper/` (`main.cpp`, `gl_api.h`, its own `CMakeLists.txt`), built by `scripts/mpc-container-build.sh` with the same ELF checks as the plugin (libc only, glibc symbols up to the ceiling of the device of that architecture: 2.39 on the Key 61 and on the XL, read from `/lib/libc.so.6` of both; no Mesa library in the dynamic section). A build host of the target's own architecture (Docker on an arm64 computer building arm64) has no DRM uapi headers in its native `linux-libc-dev`: the container script then copies them from Debian's armhf cross package (they do not depend on the architecture).
- Build without the device's libraries: the build image has no Mesa headers or libraries and the repository carries no device binary. `gl_api.h` declares the few EGL / GLES2 / GBM entry points it needs (stable ABIs: Khronos values, Mesa's `gbm.h`) and finds them with `dlopen` / `dlsym` of `libEGL.so.1`, `libgbm.so.1` and `libGLESv2.so.2` at run time. So the helper links against libc only, the build needs nothing but the cross compiler (reproducible from the repository alone), and a missing library or function is a clean `error` message to the plugin and the CPU path, not a crash at start. The rendering device is a render node found at run time ("Discovery"); the libraries are searched by name and in the usual directories.
- Start: the overlay thread starts the helper the first time a view is on screen (never earlier), with `posix_spawn` (no `fork` without `exec` in this multi-threaded real-time process: only the spawning overlay thread waits for the `exec`). The child gets its socket on descriptor 3, `/dev/null` and the log file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu.log` as 0 - 2, every other descriptor of the application is closed (`addclosefrom_np`), an empty signal mask and default handlers. Scheduling and affinity are the overlay thread's (SCHED_OTHER, nice 0, never the isolated audio cores). The first picture is drawn by the CPU; when the helper has said it is ready the pictures move to the GPU.
- Channels (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/scene.h`, one header for both sides, `kProtocolVersion` 6; version 6 is described in "Overlay look and drawing"; version 4 added the long scenes, the polyline with its own span and the text op, version 5 the ops `kOpLine` (a segment at any angle), `kOpArc` (a ring segment) and `kOpFrame` (a rectangle outline) for the UI component library ("UI components"), validated against the shared block by `lineGeom`, `arcGeom` and `frameGeom` in `scene.h`, and `kMaxOps` 640; version 2 added the second colour of an op, the op `kOpFillGradient` (a fill coloured by a vertical gradient) and the scene kind `kKindSpectrum`, version 3 the vblank pipe, the stride and the measured period in the hello, the helper's statistics of the presentation intervals in the shared block, and the rotations 0 and 180; the plugin and the helper are shipped together, and a helper of another version answers the hello with an error message (`protocol version mismatch`), so the plugin counts a failure and draws with the CPU, never a misdrawn picture): a `SOCK_SEQPACKET` Unix socket pair with fixed 64 byte messages, and a shared block (memfd, mapped by both). The `hello` carries two descriptors with `SCM_RIGHTS`: the application's own DRM descriptor (the same open file description, so the helper has the application's master rights on plane 38; checked on the device before anything was built) and the memfd. Messages from the plugin: hello, show (region, plane position, rotation, view name), scene (a kick with the sequence number), hide, quit. From the helper: ready (GL renderer, start-up time), shown, frame (frames presented, the scene sequence number, render and SETPLANE times, skipped vblanks, late SETPLANE calls), hidden, error (text).
- The scene is a display list in canvas pixels of the landscape region (`SceneData`: clear, rectangle, vertical line, disc, and one polyline of up to 512 row values used by `fill`, the gradient fill and `stroke`), written by the plugin under a seqlock, so the helper never reads a half written scene. Every view describes itself (`IView::describe`): the geometry, order and colours are those of the CPU drawing; `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/gpu_scene_host.cpp` interprets the scene of every view type with the CPU drawing helpers and compares it with the CPU picture. The helper does everything GPU: GBM surface (ARGB8888, scanout and rendering) of the panel's portrait size, shaders for the feathered 4 pixel line, the translucent fill, the grid, the discs, the rotation in the vertex shader, `glFinish`, then the finished buffer object goes to the plane without a copy (dma-buf, `PRIME_FD_TO_HANDLE` on the shared descriptor, `ADDFB2` cached per buffer object, legacy `SETPLANE`, which waits for the vblank). The scene changes at most 30 times a second; the helper draws only when the plugin kicks it and sleeps otherwise.
- Clean-up: on hide, quit, a closed socket (the plugin or the application went away) or SIGTERM the helper takes the plane off, removes its framebuffers, closes its GEM handles and destroys the surface. Every DRM object it makes on the shared descriptor is entered in a ledger in the shared block: those objects belong to the application's file description and would otherwise outlive a killed helper, so the plugin removes what is left in the ledger itself.
- Health (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/gpu_overlay.h`, tested with a fake helper in `gpu_backend_host.cpp`): `idle` -> `starting` -> `running`; a failure (the channel closed or the process gone, an `error` message, no `ready` within 3 seconds, a scene without a frame acknowledgement for 0.7 seconds, a hide without `hidden` for 0.7 seconds) kills and reaps the helper FIRST, then takes plane 38 off (only when the helper had been told to show, or had objects in the ledger: a helper that failed before that leaves the plane to the CPU picture that may be on it) and removes the ledger's framebuffers and handles through the plugin's own descriptor, then the CPU draws within one tick (`renderer: cpu`). The helper is started again after 1, 2 and 4 seconds (`backoff`); the fourth failure keeps the CPU for the life of the process (`exhausted`); a helper that ran five minutes without a failure gets its failure count back to zero. A planned stop (the file switch, closing) is no failure.
- Switches: `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-nogpu` (exists = CPU drawing, the helper is stopped; checked once a second, switches live in both directions). Debug: `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-dump` makes the helper read back its finished frame (`glReadPixels`, rows bottom up, RGBA premultiplied) once a second into `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-frame.raw` with the numbers in `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-frame.txt`.
- The status file says who draws: `renderer: gpu` or `cpu` (the helper is running and acknowledged, and the last picture was its), `gpu_state` (`idle`, `starting`, `running`, `backoff`, `exhausted`, `off` = the file switch, `not built`), `gpu_helper_pid`, `gpu_helper_starts`, `gpu_helper_restarts`, `gpu_failures`, `gpu_last_error`, the helper's own counters (`gpu_helper_frames`, `_render_ms`, `_setplane_ms`, `_skipped_vblanks`, `_late_setplanes`, `_startup_ms`) and `gpu_renderer` (the GL renderer string). A change of the renderer is written at once, not at the next second.

**Switches and files** (all in `/tmp` of the device):

- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-off`: while this file exists the overlay stays hidden (read once a second; removing the file brings it back). `touch /tmp/synthyboy-overlay-off`.
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-nogpu`: while this file exists the CPU draws and no helper runs (see "GPU drawing in a helper process"); removing it brings the GPU back.
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-fake-slot`: debug, see "Several instances": the slot (or `none`, `blank`) the plugin reports for "Overlay Instance" instead of its real one.
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-pointer-hotspot`: debug, "The mouse path": `x y`, the pixel of the host's pointer plane picture (panel orientation) that is the arrow's tip; overrides the measured / default hot spot, read once a second.
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-rot`: `0`, `90`, `180` or `270`, read once a second: overrides the rotation of the landscape UI on the panel that the overlay finds itself ("Discovery"; 90 on the Key 61, 270 on the XL). The same function places the plane, rotates the picture and finds the marker pixels.
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status.txt`: written by every build EXCEPT the release package (`scripts/build-mpc.sh --release`, `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_DEV_LOG` OFF): the release writes it only while `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-status-on` exists (see "Logging in the plugin", "What a release package writes"). Rewritten once a second (temporary file and rename): the state (`visible`, `hidden: <reason>`, `disabled: <reason>`) and the NAME OF THE VISIBLE VIEW (`view: lfo2`), the rotation, the number of instances (`instances`, `instances_with_slot`, `instance_slots_alive` as a bit mask, `instance_slot` shown, `instance_pixel_read` = the colour read, `instance_slot_read`, `instance_rereports` = how often the instances were asked to report again), the pictures in the last second, the render, rotate and SETPLANE times, the CPU share of the overlay thread, the numbers of the visible view (filter: last computation, live cutoff and resonance, hash of the curve; LFO: shape hash, cursor position; oscillator: kind, cycle hash, live frame and warp amounts; envelope: stage, dot position, the counters of the line colour check), a counter line for every view (`view_<name>: updates, changes, recomputes, frames`), the marker values read for every view, the panel size and the plane rectangle. The discovery: `rotation` (`unknown` until found), `rotation_source`, `display_device`, `display_crtc`, `display_primary_plane`, `display_app_planes`, `display_overlay_candidates`, `display_overlay_plane`, `display_plane_switches`, `display_plane_state`, `display_plane_losses`, `display_mode_refresh_hz` (what the mode says) and `display_refresh_hz` / `display_vblank_period_ms` (measured, `display_vblank_measured`), `pace_stride_vblanks`, `pace_mode` (`vblank grid` or `timer`), `discovery_ms`, `cpus_online`, `cpus_isolated_by_kernel`, `cpus_overlay_allowed`, `cpus_avoided`, `cpus_overlay_thread_now`, `cpus_helper_now`, `cpus_audio_threads`; and the pace: `pace_renderer`, `pace_intervals`, `pace_interval_vblanks` and `pace_interval_ms` (the intervals between two plane updates of one run, counted in vblanks and in buckets of two milliseconds: when the pace is even one bucket holds nearly all of them), `overlay_tick_max_ms_last_second`, `overlay_scene_gap_max_ms_last_second` (the longest step of the overlay thread and the longest gap between two scenes in the last second).
- `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-dump`: while this file exists, `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-curve.txt` is rewritten once a second with the data of the visible view: the 256 values of the shown and of the exact filter curve, the 300 values of an LFO shape, the 512 values of an oscillator cycle, the geometry of an envelope (a debugging aid; `synthyboy_filter_response --set KEY=VALUE` computes the same filter curve on a computer).

**Limits and what happens on failure.**

- The GPU helper needs the firmware's own Mesa libraries (`libEGL.so.1`, `libgbm.so.1`, `libGLESv2.so.2`) and a render node; it was made for the Key 61 and proven on the Key 61 and the MPC XL. When anything about it is missing or fails the plugin draws with the CPU, as without the helper (see "GPU drawing in a helper process"). Mesa wants a writable shader cache directory: `/root/.cache` is read-only on the device, Mesa prints one line to `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu.log` and runs without the cache (harmless: the shaders are small).
- It uses a display plane that is not part of the plugin SDK. A firmware update, or the MPC app itself using the plane, can break it. The plugin checks that the plane is free (no CRTC, no framebuffer) and offers ARGB, takes the next free one when it is not or when another program takes it later, and does not start when there is none.
- The module disables itself for the life of the process, and writes the reason to the status file once, when the descriptor of the display is not found, no free plane is left, or a call to the display driver fails that no spare plane helps (no retry). The plugin then works as without it (the skin without a curve: the dark area stays).
- Several instances: up to 16 (slots 0 to 15, see "Several instances"); the instances beyond them never show the overlay. The overlay is one process-wide singleton; with a skin that has no instance pictures (any other than the overlay skin) or a plugin whose parameter list lacks "Overlay Instance" nothing identifies the instance and the overlay stays hidden. Verified on the device with ONE instance only: the host's redraw with the colours of other slots (debug file) and the hiding for a slot that is not alive are measured there ("Observed on the device"); that the curve follows the track with two real tracks is shown by the Mac tests with fakes only, the test with two real tracks on a device is still open.
- A crash of the MPC app leaves the plane and its buffers to the kernel (they are freed with the app's descriptor). The module removes the plane, the framebuffers and the buffers when the page goes, when the plugin is closed (`effClose`) and from an exit handler.
- It never sets client capabilities on the application's descriptor (the one descriptor of its own that has the atomic capability reads the host's pointer plane, read only: "The mouse path"), never touches the application's planes, never sets a mode and never commits atomically (the one atomic request of the investigation was an EMPTY test-only request in a measuring tool, not in the plugin); only the legacy plane call (`SETPLANE`) on the overlay plane and, where the driver has a mutable one, the `zpos` property of that same plane ("Stacking against the host's pointer"), which waits for the vblank (so it runs on its own thread, never the audio thread).
- Tested on the Key 61 (firmware 3.10.1.12) and on the MPC XL (another panel, SoC and display controller; the firmware version is not recorded here). Other MPC models are not tested. What a human eye has to judge (orientation of the picture, smoothness, the blending, the exact place on the glass) is not in any test.

### Main layer page guard (experimental)

To eliminate `SETPLANE` display-lock contention and eliminate stale overlay frames during host page transitions, the GPU helper and `xl-commit-shim` cooperate via a synchronous page guard protocol (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/layer_block.h`, `shell-mpc/overlay/gpu-helper/main.cpp`, `tools/xl-commit-shim/`).

- **Measured facts (MPC XL, 2026-11-21; the numbers are in `docs/dsp-knowledge/mpc-display.md` and `.hw-local/mpc-perf/xl-takeover-2026-11-21/`):** The host commits from its main thread only on UI events: frame presents use `drmModeAtomicCommit` with flags `0x1` (`DRM_MODE_PAGE_FLIP_EVENT`) and non-NULL `user_data`; cursor moves use `0x200` (`DRM_MODE_ATOMIC_NONBLOCK`, `user_data == NULL`). Host atomic commits are event-driven (bursts during interaction, none when idle). Because flips are event-driven and the helper renders only after detecting the markers of an already-flipped host frame, the earlier publish/claim takeover merged almost nothing and added overlay latency; it was replaced by this page guard.
- **Architecture (`LayerBlock` v3 & Page Guard):**
  - **Helper presentation:** The GPU helper always presents its frames directly via `SETPLANE` (`planeSet`) as fast as it can with no artificial wait (the earlier publish/claim wait is gone).
  - **State publishing:** The helper publishes its active page ID, expected marker colors (left and right, from the layout table), and panel rotation into `/tmp/synthyboy-layer-block` immediately before `SETPLANE` (eliminating spurious plane-off blinks on page transitions).
  - **Shim commit inspection:** On every live host frame commit, `xl-commit-shim` inspects the primary plane framebuffer. It maps the host dumb buffer memory once via `drmModeGetFB2` and `DRM_IOCTL_MODE_MAP_DUMB`, caching mappings per FB ID and unmapping on `drmModeRmFB`.
  - **Marker verification:** At the exact moment of the host flip, the shim samples the marker centers for the published page from the generated layout, mapped to panel rotation.
  - **Atomic plane-off:** If the host framebuffer markers mismatch the helper's published page, or represent an unknown non-SynthyBoy page, the shim injects `FB_ID = 0, CRTC_ID = 0` for our overlay plane into THAT host commit (including `DRM_MODE_ATOMIC_NONBLOCK` frame commits). Stale overlay frames are eliminated atomically with the host flip. The shim sets `guard_plane_off = 1` in `LayerBlock`. **Hidden until a fresh positive page detection (g130):** the helper reads the flag, clears it, reports `kMsgGuard(epoch)` to the plugin and draws and presents NOTHING until the plugin answers `kMsgPageOk(epoch, view)`: the answer is sent by the first page detection (`Controller::tick`) made AFTER the report that finds one of our views (markers known and matching) on the current host framebuffer, and the helper presents once the view it was told to show equals that view (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/guard_state.h`, `GuardGate` / `GuardConfirm`; tests `guard_state_host.cpp`, `gpu_backend_host.cpp` "page guard"). Before, the helper re-presented shortly after the flag (the plugin kept sending scenes of a view that was no longer on screen) and went off again a little later, so the overlay flashed over a host screen such as the Load dialog (`.hw-local/mpc-perf/xl-playblank-2026-11-21/`). The plugin does not wait for its normal detection cycle: the tick that reads `kMsgGuard` runs ONE page detection at once, on the same thread (`IGpuOverlay::takeGuardReport`, `Controller::kGuardDetectGapMs`, at most one such detection per 16 milliseconds; a second report inside the gap waits for the normal cycle), so the confirmation goes out in that tick; the cost on a page change to our page B is then the message hop and the plugin thread's wake-up, not a detection cycle. The messages are part of protocol version 9 (`gpu/scene.h`): a helper and a plugin of different versions refuse the hello (`kMsgError`, the CPU draws), so a helper never waits for a confirmation that its plugin cannot send (tests `gpu_backend_host.cpp` "another protocol version", `gpu_scene_host.cpp`). While the guard holds the helper back the plugin does not count the missing frame acknowledgement as a hang. **Guard retry (g132):** the immediate detection often finds nothing yet (the host has not drawn its new markers), and the plugin's own hide of the plane clears the backend's pending flag, so the plugin keeps its OWN watch (`Controller::guardWatch_`, set by `takeGuardReport`, cleared when a page is current): while it is set the page detection is repeated every vblank (`gpu::GuardRetry`, one vblank apart, at most 190 attempts, a new guard epoch counts again, a found page stops it) and the overlay thread's wait is cut to one vblank ONLY then (`GuardRetry::limit`); at rest `guardWatch_` is false, the wait is the step's own and the counters `guard_retry: detections= wakeups=` of the status file do not move (test `gpu_backend_host.cpp` "guard-pending retry"). **Guard timestamps:** while the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-guard-trace` exists, `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-guard-trace.log` gets one line `<CLOCK_MONOTONIC ns> <helper|plugin> <event> <a> <b>` for helper `guard_flag_read`, `guard_sent`, `page_ok_recv`, `first_present` and plugin `guard_recv`, `detect` (view, -1 none), `page_ok_sent` (`gpu/guard_trace.h`); the clock is the one of the `time_ns` of the commit shim log, so `scripts/guard-trace-correlate.py` puts flip, guard, detections and the first present on one time line. **Pace after a confirmation, and the helper's idle loop (g134):** the first picture the helper presents after a `kMsgPageOk` is not held for the next stride boundary of the pace grid (`FramePacer::presentNextImmediately`, `vblank_pacer.h`; before, the confirmation waited one whole stride of 2 vblanks); the update starts a new grid, the next picture is a stride later, every other picture keeps the grid (test `display_host` "g134"). The helper's main loop no longer wakes once a second at rest: its sleep is 3 s while a picture is on the plane (the still-picture GETPLANE check is made every 3 s; the check right after an update stays at 1 s), 60 s with nothing on the plane, and 1 s only with the debug dump file, the bench or a guard flag nobody waits for (`LoopTimeout`, `gpu/guard_state.h`); the heartbeat freshness rule of the shim (`LAYER_BLOCK_HEARTBEAT_NS`) is the shim's own and unchanged, the helper writes no heartbeat. The status file has `loop_wakes` and `loop_wakes_idle`; target at rest: at most 20 wake-ups a minute. Device numbers: `docs/dsp-knowledge/mpc-display.md`.
  - **Matching page:** When markers match the helper's published page, the host commit passes through untouched.
  - **Safety:** Any mapping failure, invalid property, or IOCTL error passes the host commit through untouched (incrementing `guard_map_errors`) and never fails the host's commit.
- **Switch on/off & recovery:**
  - Build: `tools/xl-commit-shim/build.sh [aarch64|armv7|all]`
  - Install & start: `tools/xl-commit-shim/layershim.sh <xl|k61> start` (combines `LD_PRELOAD` with `hwctl.conf` on Key 61 into one line in `/run/systemd/system/acvs.service.d/zz-layershim.conf`)
  - Enable/disable guard: `tools/xl-commit-shim/layershim.sh <xl|k61> guard on|off` (alias: `takeover on|off`)
  - Recovery: `tools/xl-commit-shim/layershim.sh <xl|k61> recover` (removes all flag files and drop-in, reloads systemd, restarts `acvs`)
- **Verification on device:** Live takeover run on MPC XL (2026-11-21) established the timing realities; page guard redesign tested natively with 25 fake-DRM unit tests in `test_shim_host.c` and 1M-iteration TSan `layer_block_host.cpp`.

**Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/overlay_instance_host.cpp` (the parameter "Overlay Instance" of the built plugin: last in the list, not automatable, not in the chunk, slots of several instances, reuse, the 17th instance, the debug file, instances created and closed on four threads at once, also under ThreadSanitizer; `lfo_live_host` and `spectrum_live_host` take the word `overlay` for the one build with the extra parameter and stay exact in both cases). `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/`: `geometry_render_host.cpp` (the rotation mapping and the renderer on a memory buffer), `display_host.cpp` (the discovery rules against the plane tables of the Key 61 and the XL, a busy plane, a plane that cannot carry ARGB or the CRTC, no free plane, a plane that would stack under the application's, the cores of both devices, the pace grid with a fake vblank clock, the rotation found from the markers under all four rotations and its ambiguity, and a plane that another program takes), `curve_host.cpp` (the curve source), `views_host.cpp` (every view: data to expected values and pixels with fakes, the marker colours, the readout targets, the envelope geometry rule against the measured screenshots, the controller with several views), `controller_host.cpp` (the state machine with a fake display backend and a fake framebuffer; also on a thread, under ThreadSanitizer; the slot table and the colour table, two, three and 17 instances with different parameters: the colour on the fake screen picks the data, a colour change switches the data within two checks, an invalid or absent instance picture hides and asks for a report again at a limited rate, an instance that closes while its page is on screen, slot reuse; instances opened and closed on another thread while the overlay thread runs; also under AddressSanitizer), `spectrum_curve_host.cpp` and `spectrum_view_host.cpp` (the spectrum curve and view, see "Spectrum"), `gpu_scene_host.cpp` (the GPU scene protocol: the seqlock also against a writer on another thread, the layout of the shared structures, the gradient op, and every view type as a scene, interpreted by the reference interpreter `scene_reference.h` and compared pixel by pixel with the CPU picture), `gpu_backend_host.cpp` (the health state machine with a fake helper: the hello with the discovered plane, CRTC and pace, a helper's SETPLANE error that moves the restart to the next plane, a plane that is not ours any more being left alone, the helper's pace statistics in the status, the scene handed over before the page detection in every step, healthy, slow to start, never answers, error message, death, ignores quit, kill switch toggled while visible, plugin closing; a helper of another protocol version is refused and the CPU draws; every scenario ends with plane 38 off and none of the helper's framebuffers or GEM handles left), `compute_bench.cpp` (the cost of a full computation, for the device); all but the benchmark run in `shell-mpc/accept.sh`, which also builds the plugin with the module and checks that the state chunk is unchanged. The data of the views is tested in the core (`core/tests/test_own_displays.cpp`: the LFO shape against the engine's own LFO value for every shape, the oscillator cycle against the engine's output; `test_own_filter_response*.cpp`, whose speed limits are the thread's own CPU time, the best of five repetitions (`sbtest::bestCpuMs` in `core/tests/test_runner.h`), so that the whole suite running in parallel does not change them; the review answers are in `core/docs/filter-response-review-answers.md`). `scripts/tests/test_mpc_skin_overlay.py` covers the skin variant (marker pictures with the exact colours and positions, the controls, the pictures of the LFO and Osc tabs under the markers, the production skin unchanged).

### Standby dim (overlay build)

Measured on the Key 61 (research-draw-into-host.md point 5): after about 10 minutes idle the host drops the backlight (`/sys/class/backlight/mipi-backlight/brightness` 227 -> 106 in one step) and repaints its own picture at exactly half brightness (0x1e1d1e -> 0x0f0e0f) and blurred (about 8 px box). Our markers (6 px squares) keep about half of their contrast in the middle. On waking the picture is back first, the backlight a few tens of milliseconds later, our old plane after another short pause. The MPC XL does it differently (the numbers are in "Observed on the device", "Standby dim on the MPC XL"): the picture is blurred, not darkened, and the backlight does not change. So the two signals are independent evidence.

`shell-mpc/overlay/standby.h`: `Backlight` finds the node generically (first node with a readable `brightness`), `markersLookDimmed` accepts the held view's markers at the dim factor with the blur's coverage unknown (the marker contrast scaled by 0.15 .. 1.35 on the strongest channel, all channels on that line within a small tolerance; a plain dimmed background is refused; the right marker may be the dimmed Q-Link pop-up), `blurredPicture` / `measureFactor` are the picture-alone evidence of a host that only blurs (the XL): the host's own background level 9 px beside the markers gives the dim factor (host background / known background; accepted 0.35 to 1.05; snapped to 1 within 0.04 and to 0.5 within 0.03; the points must agree), then 13 samples around each of the held view's markers (left marker, instance picture of the held slot, right marker: centre, four sides 3 px out, four inset corners, four sides 6 px out) must match the sum of the markers' colours blurred by a 7.5 px box (shift -0.25 px) times the factor, within 3.5 levels plus 0.15 of the predicted contrast per channel, so a sharp marker (coverage 1 instead of 0.64), a plain background, another view's colours, a pop-up and an unrelated screen are all refused, and the three markers must agree on the one held view (neighbour views 7 levels apart in a channel cannot be told after the blur: the held view is the identity; the right marker may be under the Q-Link pop-up, then its samples must read the dimmed pop-up fill). `Tracker` is the state machine (awake markers decide first; otherwise the blurred picture alone dims (evidence `picture`), or the dimmed look + backlight drop below three quarters of the awake reference (evidence `backlight+picture`, the Key 61); the Key 61's picture without the backlight (or the reverse) is held for `kHoldMs` (300 milliseconds), then the page is gone as before; markers half-way between blurred and sharp, as on a wake, are held for the same time, so the host's bare skin is never shown for a frame; no backlight node: the dimmed look alone), `dimScene` / `dimCanvas` darken by the factor measured from the host's own background (every colour of the GPU scene, cell hashes changed; the CPU canvas RGB; a measured factor above 0.98, the XL, leaves our picture exactly as it is: sharp and at full brightness). While dimmed: the key stays where it was, touch / wheel are swallowed with the whole gesture (the first touch only wakes the host; this does not use the backlight). The blur is NOT drawn: the GPU helper has no full-frame pass for it and the host test renderer measured about nine milliseconds for a separable 8 px box blur of 1280x628 (darkening about one and a half) - on the 32-bit device several times that per picture. Identity at half brightness cannot be read from the colour (neighbour views differ by 7 levels): the held view is the identity. Status lines: `backlight_node`, `backlight_value`, `backlight_awake_reference`, `dimmed`, `dim_evidence` (`backlight+picture`, `picture`, or `none` while awake), `dim_factor` (the brightness our picture is drawn at: 0.5 on the Key 61, 1 on the XL), `dim_transitions`, `wake_transitions`, `dim_hold_ticks`, `dim_hidden_frames` (must stay 0), `dim_touch_swallowed`. Tests: `standby_host.cpp`. Parameter coverage of the pages: `coverage_host.cpp`.

## Play selectors and proxies (overlay build)


**Selectors.** Three plugin parameters "Play Osc" (1 to 5: Sub, A, B, C, Noise; parameter 2676, its place did not change when it grew from three instances to five), "Play Env" (1 to 6) and "Play LFO" (1 to 10): stepped, NOT automatable, saved in the plugin's chunk with the project (members `mpcPlayOsc5`, `mpcPlayEnv`, `mpcPlayLfo` before the pad pressure members; a chunk without them, an older one, leaves the selectors as they are, a new instance starts at 1 / 1 / 1; a chunk with a selector out of range is ignored as a whole). The display text of the oscillator selector is "Sub", "Osc A", "Osc B", "Osc C", "Noise" (A, B and C are the engine's `osc1.*` .. `osc3.*`, Sub is `sub.*`, Noise is `noise.*`; the order is in `shell-mpc/overlay/play_osc.h`, the one table the parameter code and the views use).


**Old projects (the oscillator selector).** The build with three oscillator instances saved the oscillator selector as `mpcPlayOsc` with 1 to 3 (A, B, C). A chunk like that loads as the same oscillator: old 1, 2, 3 become instance 2, 3, 4 (A, B, C) and are saved again as `mpcPlayOsc5`. An old-form value outside 1 to 3 is ignored as a whole. The five-instance member has a different name on purpose, so that neither build reads the other's number as its own (a build with three instances finds no `mpcPlayOsc` in a new chunk and leaves its selectors as they are). Test: `shell-mpc/tests/play_host.cpp` (the chunk of the three-instance build, every old value, the new form, the exact tails). The skin shows each selector as the header dropdown of its third; the display text of a selector is "Osc 2", "Env 3", "LFO 10". Like in Serum the player can look at Osc 2, Env 3 and LFO 4 at the same time. A selector changes the picture of its third at once (no wait for the compute budget of the view) and the knobs below jump to the values of the selected instance (see proxies).

**Proxy parameters.** The knobs act on the SELECTED instance through 24 plugin parameters (8 per group, named "Play Osc WT Pos" ... "Play LFO Division"; not saved, NOT automatable). A proxy has no value of its own, it IS the real parameter of the selected instance: reading it gives the normalised value of that parameter, writing it is the same write as the host's own write to the real parameter (`writeRealParam`: mirror, protection, engine; the engine sees an ordinary change). Its name is its own; its range, steps, unit, display text, label and parameter properties are those of the selected instance's real parameter (the instances of a group are made by the same macros of the engine, so the kinds are the same: `play::uniform()` checks range, default, skew, step, scale, unit, flags, choices and decimals of every slot across the instances, and the test fails when an instance differs). A real parameter that changes by another route (its own tab, a preset, automation) moves the proxy too: the display thread (the one that reports the LFO cursors; never the audio thread) compares every proxy with what the host last heard and reports the changed ones with `audioMasterAutomate`. A selector change re-reports all eight proxies of its group (and the selector) once; a burst of changes is coalesced into the next tick. A proxy the host wrote makes the display thread report the REAL parameter too, so the module's own tab follows. Loops: a value is reported only when it differs from what the host last heard (a host write counts as heard), the comparison has a tolerance of one millionth, and a write that equals the current value changes nothing and disarms nothing; a host that sets every reported value back (the test has one) causes no further reports. The "chunk wins" protection (a program selection right after a restored chunk changes nothing) is disarmed only by a real change of a real parameter, never by a report or its echo, and not by a selector.

**The mapping table (Serum control to engine parameter).** Slot n is Q-Link n of its sub-tab. Where the engine has no exact equivalent the closest real parameter is used (said in the last column); nothing is invented.

| Sub-tab | Q-Link / slot | Proxy | Serum control | Engine key (instance N) | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Play (Osc) | 1 | Play Osc WT Pos | WT Pos | `oscN.frame` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 2 | Play Osc Unison | Unison voices | `oscN.unison.voices` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 3 | Play Osc Detune | Unison detune | `oscN.unison.detune` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 4 | Play Osc Blend | Unison blend | `oscN.unison.blend` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 5 | Play Osc Phase | Phase | `oscN.phase` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 6 | Play Osc Rand Phase | Random phase | `oscN.random_phase` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 7 | Play Osc Pan | Pan | `oscN.pan` | exact |
| Play (Osc) | 8 | Play Osc Level | Level | `oscN.level` | exact |
| Play Env | 1 | Play Env Attack | Attack | `envN.attack` | exact |
| Play Env | 2 | Play Env Hold | Hold | `envN.hold` | exact |
| Play Env | 3 | Play Env Decay | Decay | `envN.decay` | exact |
| Play Env | 4 | Play Env Sustain | Sustain | `envN.sustain` | exact |
| Play Env | 5 | Play Env Release | Release | `envN.release` | exact |
| Play Env | 6 | Play Env Atk Curve | Attack curve | `envN.attack_curve` | the engine has three curvature parameters |
| Play Env | 7 | Play Env Dec Curve | Decay curve | `envN.decay_curve` | |
| Play Env | 8 | Play Env Rel Curve | Release curve | `envN.release_curve` | |
| Play LFO | 1 | Play LFO Rate | Rate | `lfoN.rate` | the rate in Hz; with a tempo sync it is `lfoN.division` (slot 8) |
| Play LFO | 2 | Play LFO Rise | Rise (fade in) | `lfoN.fade_in` | exact |
| Play LFO | 3 | Play LFO Delay | Delay | `lfoN.delay` | exact |
| Play LFO | 4 | Play LFO Smooth | Smooth | `lfoN.smooth_time` | Serum's smooth is a time; the engine has a time and a switch |
| Play LFO | 5 | Play LFO Smooth On | (smooth on / off) | `lfoN.smooth` | the switch of the smoothing |
| Play LFO | 6 | Play LFO Sync | Sync (Free / Tempo ...) | `lfoN.sync` | Free (Hz), Tempo, dotted, triplet, key follow |
| Play LFO | 7 | Play LFO Trigger | Mode (trigger) | `lfoN.sync_type` | Retrigger, free-run, envelope ... |
| Play LFO | 8 | Play LFO Division | Rate in beats | `lfoN.division` | the beat division used by the tempo syncs |

**Sub and Noise** (instances 1 and 5 of "Play Osc") have no unison and no wavetable position. They map their Basic group (the four values the overlay oscillator view shows first) to the first four slots, Q-Link 1 to 4; the other four slots have no parameter (the proxy reads 0, shows nothing, ignores a write):

| Slot | Sub | Noise |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `sub.waveform` (the shape, six choices) | `noise.type` (generated noise or sample) |
| 2 | `sub.octave` | `noise.filter` |
| 3 | `sub.pan` | `noise.pan` |
| 4 | `sub.level` | `noise.level` |

The engine's `noise.type` is the two-way switch between the generated noise and a sample; the nine noise colours (white, pink, brown, ...) are the different parameter `noise.sample`. The first Noise slot carries `noise.sample` (the nine noise sources: the owner's decision), not `noise.type`; the table is `realKey` in `shell-mpc/play_params.h`. `play::uniform()` checks the kinds of parameter for A, B and C (the proxy reports one range for them); Sub and Noise differ by nature (a six-choice waveform, an octave), so the proxy's properties, text and label follow the selected instance at every call, and a host widget that cached the range of the first instance keeps its own steps.

The oscillator's "unison blend" has an exact counterpart; the engine's envelope has a delay stage as well, which has no Play knob (the Env tabs have it). Every real parameter keeps its control on its own tab, the Play page adds controls, it removes none.



**Limits.** The proxies and the selectors are not automatable (the host may still let a player assign them to a Q-Link or record them as host-side automation; the plugin does not offer them). The picture of a third follows the plugin's parameters of the instance, so a real parameter automation moves it as it moves the real parameter. The Play page's host value for a proxy is only as exact as the normalised float of the real parameter (a change smaller than one millionth is not a change).

**Project save and reload.** The host keeps the plugin's state as its chunk; it may ALSO keep the value of every parameter and write them back after the chunk. The 24 proxies hold the values of whichever instance was selected at save time, so a write-back that reaches a different instance, or one that disarms the "chunk wins" protection, would damage the project. The rules (overlay build; `PluginInstance::restoreActive`, `writeRealParam`, `pluginSetParameter` in `shell-mpc/synthyboy_mpc.cpp`):
- **Restore window**: opens at every successful `effSetChunk` and ends at the first of: a host write to a REAL parameter that is not an echo, a program that is really loaded (`effSetProgram` that the protection does not consume), or 2 seconds (`kRestoreWindowMs`; the host's write-back comes in the same burst as the chunk, a player cannot touch a knob in that time).
- **In the window**: a write to a real parameter whose value equals what the plugin reports (within 1e-6; the float round trip of a normalised value differs in the last bit) is an echo: it changes nothing and disarms nothing; a write to a selector or a proxy is ignored (the chunk is the authority for the selectors, and a proxy write could only reach another instance than the one it was saved for). Outside the window a proxy write is an ordinary edit of the selected instance, a selector write moves the selector, and a proxy write equal to the value the proxy reports changes nothing (also in the window).
- **Order independence**: (a) chunk only, (b) chunk then every parameter written back in index order or in reverse, (c) every parameter first and then the chunk (the chunk sets every parameter, the selectors included, so the early writes are overwritten), (d) chunk, write-back, program re-selection (the protection holds), chunk, program re-selection, write-back, and the order seen on the device (`PCST`: program, chunk, the toggle of parameter 0). `shell-mpc/tests/play_restore_host.cpp` replays them against two projects with every engine parameter edited and compares the chunk the plugin returns byte for byte, every parameter value and the selectors; it also covers a proxy write at any other time (no chunk loaded, an automation like stream, a selector change between two writes) as an ordinary edit of the selected instance only.
- **The toggle of parameter 0**: the host sets parameter 0 to 1.0 and then back to the value it read; `writeRealParam` remembers the value a parameter had before the host's last change and writes that value back exactly when the host's value equals its normalised form, so the saved state is not changed by the round trip (overlay build).
- **Debug log** (`shell-mpc/play_log.h`, overlay build only): while the file `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log` exists (looked at on every tick of the display thread and when an instance is created) every `effSetChunk` (size, hash), `effGetChunk`, `effSetProgram`, `setParameter` and incoming MIDI note-on and note-off (`note_on` / `note_off`, channel 1-16, note number, host velocity, engine velocity after shell mapping if any, number of dropped events if full) is appended to `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log.txt` with a counter, the milliseconds since the first line, the number of `getParameter` calls since the previous line and what the plugin did with the write (taken, echo, ignored in the window, written); the chunk of the last `effSetChunk` / `effGetChunk` is written to `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log-chunk-set.json` / `-chunk-get.json`; the file stops growing at 512 KB. For incoming note events, the line format is `<counter> <milliseconds since first line> <note_on|note_off> channel <1-16> note <0-127> vel <host_velocity> engine <engine_velocity> dropped <dropped_count>` (example: `14 250 note_on channel 1 note 60 vel 40 engine 40 dropped 0`). Creating `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log-dump` makes the display thread write the state of the instance (selectors, program, protection, every engine parameter, the proxies, the chunk) to `/tmp/synthyboy-play-log-dump.txt` and delete the request (SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PLAY_LOG_FILE changes the base path). Without the switch file nothing is written.


**Closing an instance.** `effClose` calls `PluginInstance::shutdown()` and only then deletes the instance; the order is fixed: (1) the display thread (`liveThread`) is stopped and JOINED: it is the only thread of the plugin that calls the host (`audioMasterAutomate`), so after the join no callback to the host happens for that instance, and it still has everything it uses (the overlay state, the parameter mirror); (2) the overlay forgets the instance (`Service::detach`: slot freed, the controller's lock is taken, so a poll of the overlay thread that is running has finished; the plane is off and the helper stopped when it was the last instance); (3) the members are destroyed. A review claimed that the display thread could still report to the host while the instance is half destroyed: before this order the instance was detached first and the display thread was joined in the destructor, after `stopOverlay` and before any member went, so the claim of a use of a destroyed member was not real; the order is now explicit and the join comes first. `shell-mpc/tests/close_stress_host.cpp` (three threads create, use and close one, two and three instances at a time with random pauses while the display thread reports and the overlay thread polls; the host callback fails the test when it is called for an instance whose `effClose` has returned) runs in `shell-mpc/accept.sh` plainly, under ThreadSanitizer and under AddressSanitizer with UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (a build of the plugin with `-fsanitize=address,undefined`, `build-mpc-live-asan`).

**The overlay thread after the last instance closed (found on the Key 61, fixed).** The overlay thread stops with the last instance and starts again with the next one (`Service`). Its clock (`now` for `Controller::tick`) began at zero at every start, but the controller keeps its deadlines (`nextSwitchesMs_`, `nextDetectMs_`, the report gaps, the second counter) between runs: after a run of N seconds a re-created instance stayed without overlay for N seconds (nothing detected, nothing written to the status file, the kill switch without effect). A first test after a swap of the track's instrument after minutes of running showed it (status `instances: 0` with a live instance and a live plugin page). Now the clock of the thread is the service's own (`Service::epoch_`, set once) and `Controller::tick` forgets its deadlines when the clock it is given goes back (`rebaseClock`); `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/controller_host.cpp` ("a restarted clock") fails without it.

**Tests.** `shell-mpc/tests/close_stress_host.cpp` (closing instances, see above), `shell-mpc/tests/play_restore_host.cpp` (project save and reload, the debug log), `shell-mpc/tests/play_params_rt_host.cpp` (the proxies on the audio thread).

## Overlay look and drawing

The overlay's drawing was widened for a tech demo that should look good: thin lines of light, gradients, glow, proportional anti-aliased text, three looks. Everything below is in the overlay build only; the production (overlay OFF) build and the order of the 2707 parameters did not change.

**Scene protocol 6** (`shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/scene.h`, the GPU helper `gpu-helper/main.cpp`, the CPU reference `scene_cpu.h` + `draw.h`: one meaning, the tests compare the CPU picture; the shaders are checked on the device by reading the finished frame back).
- Cells: `kOpCellBegin` / `kOpCellEnd` (below, "Cached cells and layers"). Frame: `kMaxOps` 2048, `kMaxVertices` 65536 (the pool of the rows of all polylines of a scene), `kMaxCurve` 2048 (one polyline), `kMaxText` 8192 bytes. The shared block grew with them (336 KB); `writeScene` / `readScene` copy only the used part (the header, `numOps` ops, `numText` bytes, `numPoints` vertices), so a frame of a few hundred ops is a few kilobytes and the overlay thread stays free of allocation and locks (the seqlock is unchanged; the builder clears only the header). The counts of a torn read are cut to the limits before they are used.
- State ops: `kOpStyle` (the feather in pixels, round caps) and `kOpPaint` (four equally spaced colours along x, along y or along the angle of an arc; the alpha of the drawing op is a factor, so every curve has an alpha of its own). Line-like ops (Stroke, Line, Arc, Disc, VLine, RRect) take the feather from the style: below 1 px the plain 1 px anti-aliasing, above it a smooth step over that many pixels (the soft pass of a glow). A line of half width h is never stronger than 2h, so 0.5 px lines are fainter, not wider (`lineCoverage`).
- New shapes: `kOpRRect` (rounded rectangle, filled or an outline of a width inside the edges, signed distance field in the shader), `kOpBand` (the exact coverage of the strip between two polylines per pixel row: the min / max picture of a dense wave), round caps for Arc (a knob arc with round ends) and Line, an arc painted along its angle (`atan` in the shader).
- `kOpClip`: everything after it is limited to a rectangle (scissor on the GPU; the CPU reference puts back what the clipped ops drew outside), an empty rectangle switches it off; the Play view uses it to leave the strip of the host's Q-Link panel transparent.
- `kOpText`: a string from the text pool (any length, UTF-8, any size in pixels per em, tracking), proportional, drawn from a signed distance field atlas: the coverage is `clamp (0.5 + d * size / kEm, 0, 1)` on both renderers (`sdf_font.h`: glyph lookup, widths, `fitText` with an ellipsis, `layout`). The old 5 x 7 pixel font is gone from every view.
- `kOpGlowBegin` / `kOpGlowEnd`: the ops between them go to a layer of 1/2, 1/4 or 1/8 of the picture, which is blurred (two passes, 9 taps from 5 linear samples) and added over the picture: a bloom. `kOpFeedback`: the previous picture of a rectangle times a gain, under the new drawing (an afterglow); the helper copies the picture into a texture only for scenes that use it. The CPU reference draws neither (it skips the ops between the glow ops).
- Both glow ways (a wide faint extra pass per curve with a soft style; the bloom layer) are in the helper and were measured (trial 17 in "Observed on the device"): the soft pass costs per curve, the bloom is flat whatever the number of curves, so the bloom is the cheaper from about six curves (numbers in trial 17). The owner's taste decided the rest: NOTHING that carries a value is lit (signal lines, knob arcs, values, texts, selected items are crisp and anti-aliased); the only light is a faint back glow behind a display (`Look::backGlow`, bloom of its lines inside the display rectangle, 0.18 in look 1, 0 in look 2, 0.30 in look 3; the level of detail "reduced" has none).

**Cached cells and layers (scene protocol 8, `shell-mpc/overlay/gpu/scene.h`, `cell_plan.h`, the helper).** What the UI draws is mostly the same from one picture to the next (the blocks, the labels, the frames, the knobs at rest). The scene marks such parts as CELLS (`kOpCellBegin` with the rectangle and a hash of the content that `SceneBuilder::cellEnd` makes from the ops, the text and the rows of the curves inside, not from their places in the pools; `kOpCellEnd`): every leaf component, the under- and over-drawing of every composite, a Q frame with its tabs, the ground, the Env and LFO panels without their running point and the display with its block are cells. The helper keeps the cells of a scene in two LAYERS (textures the size of the picture): the cells before the first drawing op that is not in a cell are layer 0, the cells after such an op (the controls drawn over the live parts) are layer 1 (they lie over what moves); a cell after a second such group makes the scene uncached (it is drawn whole). When the next scene comes, the cells are matched by rectangle and hash: a cell that is not in the layer yet or that is not in the scene any more is the damage, and the cells that lie over the damage are drawn again into the layer in a scissor box (their ops are built and drawn only for these). A picture is then made of one textured quad per layer and the ops that are not in a cell (the live read-outs). Cells must start and end with the default style and paint, may contain a bloom (GlowBegin / GlowEnd: it is added into the layer) but no Clear, Clip or Feedback, and keep inside their rectangle; the CPU reference ignores them (it draws the same picture), the tests `cell_plan_host` and `gpu_scene_host` check the hash, the plan, the damage and that equality.
**Partial pictures (buffer age).** The helper's EGL gives the age of the back buffer (`EGL_EXT_buffer_age`; what the Key 61 says is in "Observed on the device", trial 19): the buffer holds the picture from that many pictures ago. The helper paints into it only what changed since: the damage of the layers of this picture and of the age minus one pictures before, and the places of the ops that are drawn every picture (from their vertices, now and in the picture before), as at most four boxes in a scissor (the whole picture when the age is unknown, the clip of the scene changed, or the boxes cover more than seventy percent of it). `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-nopartial` switches it off (diagnostics); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-egl.txt` (written once, after the 24th picture) has the EGL extensions of the display and the ages of the first 24 back buffers. The status file and the helper's statistics say how much was painted (`painted_px`, `full_frames`) and how much came from the layers (`cached_ops`, `redrawn`).
**The display while it changes.** The data of the display (the curves, the stack, the unison fan, the bloom) is a cached cell while it rests; while it changes every picture (a knob dragged along the wavetable position, a modulated position) it has changed within the last third of a second and is drawn with the picture instead (`PlayView`): a cell that is drawn again every picture only costs the layers under it too. The block of the display (gradient, keyline, grid) stays a cell. The live read-outs of the engine (read heads, grains, the phases of the sub, the read head of a sample noise) are always drawn over it (`display::drawLive`).
**The rate of the pictures.** The helper paces the plane updates on a grid of `stride` vblanks (`vblank_pacer.h`). The legacy plane update (SETPLANE) blocks until the vblank after the call; with stride 1 the grid computed the slot AFTER the vblank the previous update had just returned at, so every picture took two vblanks and 30 a second was the ceiling at any stride (trial 19 in "Observed on the device"). Stride 1 is now "no slot wait": the update follows the render at once. The plugin asks for it (`kMsgStride`): 60 a second on a GPU that is not of the Mali-T series (the XL), tried and kept only when the helper really presents 45 or more pictures a second while the picture moves (else back to 30 for two minutes); 30 on the Mali-T (the Key 61); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-fps` (30 or 60) overrides. The scenes follow the stride (a scene a little before each picture). The status file says `gpu_rate_stride` and `gpu_rate_source`.
**The GPU clock.** The governor of the GPU (`simple_ondemand`, 100 to 600 MHz on the Key 61) lowers the clock when the load is low, so a picture that costs little at the highest clock takes several times as long at the lowest, and a change of the clock costs a stall. While pictures are made continuously (eight a second or more, three milliseconds or more each) the plugin raises the lowest clock the governor may use (`min_freq` of the devfreq node of the GPU) to the lowest available one of at least 300 MHz and puts it back when the picture has rested for three seconds, at the end and, from the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpu-minfreq.orig`, at the next start after a run that died (`gpu_clock.h`); `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-gpuclock` with `off` leaves the clock to the governor. The status file says `gpu_clock_boost`.

**The font.** Titillium Web SemiBold (SIL Open Font License 1.1, the official release of the Google Fonts repository; `shell-mpc/overlay/fonts/TitilliumWeb-SemiBold.ttf` with `OFL.txt` next to it; the licence text is part of the repository, the font software keeps its own licence). `shell-mpc/overlay/sdf_font_data.h` (the committed header is the source) holds an 8-bit signed distance atlas of 512 x 297 pixels (40 pixels per em, range +- 5 pixels, about 130 glyphs: ASCII, the Hungarian letters, the degree sign, a few typographic signs, and the arrows and triangles the UI uses, which the font does not have). The header is committed and edited directly (the build does not need Python). The distance-field text is sharp from 12 to 40 pixels and for any fraction of a size on both renderers; there is no kerning.

**The theme** (`shell-mpc/ui/theme.h`): ONE table (`struct Look`) with every colour, gradient, line width, type size, radius and spacing the components and the views draw with; the components take their numbers from nowhere else. The type scale (title 28, value 24, label 15, item 22, small 12, minimum 9) is in the table. The colour of a modulation source (`sourceColour`: envelope orange, LFO green, velocity, key, wheel, pressure, macro, random) is the same on the depth arc of a knob, on its tab and on the modulation page; the instance colours are Sub orange, A cyan, B violet, C magenta, Noise green (look 1). The envelope and LFO gradients and the filter stops are in the table for the elements still to come. Next to the look there is the LEVEL OF DETAIL (`theme::Detail`, one table: the frames of the wavetable stack, the unison hairlines, the spectrum, the back glow, the afterglow): "full" is the designed picture, "reduced" (3 stack frames, 2 hairlines, no spectrum, no glow) is for a machine that cannot keep the full one within its frame time. The plugin chooses by measurement: while the GPU helper draws, the mean render time of the helper's pictures is taken once a second over the seconds with a moving picture (12 pictures or more); three such seconds in a row above fourteen milliseconds switch to "reduced" for the rest of the run; the CPU drawing (no helper) is "reduced" too. `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-detail` (`1` / `full`, `2` / `reduced`, anything else automatic) overrides it; the status file says `ui_detail`, `ui_detail_source` and `ui_detail_helper_render_ms`. The MPC XL (aarch64, stronger) is the primary target and gets "full" when it fits; the Key 61 is the cut-down machine and gets what its measured frame time allows. Three looks, three tables, no branches in the code: look 1 (`neon`) the reference palette, look 2 (`aurora`) calmer, thinner lines and little light, look 3 (`plasma`) the boldest: saturated, thicker lines, a lot of light, the instance colours dealt the other way round. The overlay reads `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-look` (a number 1 to 3) once a second; without the file it is look 1 (the status file says `ui_look`).

**The display** (`shell-mpc/ui/display.h`): the picture of the oscillator in its display rectangle. A wave is a thin crisp line along a gradient of the instance colour, over a faint area and a faint spectrum of the cycle; a wavetable shows seven frames of the table behind each other, the far ones fainter, and the frames near the position light up, so moving the position makes the highlight run along the stack; the unison voices are hairlines around the main curve (spread by the detune, as strong as the blend); a sample, multisample or granular oscillator is a min / max band with the loop range (the rest dimmed), the grain window and, when the position is known, the playhead. The compact view's envelope and LFO panels (`drawModule`) use the same language (ENV orange to red, LFO green to cyan, the running point and cursor). What the display does NOT show because the data is not there: the real playing position of a sample, the real positions and times of the grains.

## Q proxies and the display pages (overlay build)

On the native oscillator page, touching PAN, LEVEL or ROUTE selects the Mix Q-Link group of the displayed source. Its three slots address pan, level and output destination.

The plugin's own overlay pages and the native pages read and write the same parameters. This section is what sits under them: the hardware knobs, the page the knobs belong to, and the state of the pages.

**Q proxies.** Sixteen parameters "Q 1" .. "Q 16" after the last Play proxy (2703 .. 2718, the last 16 of the legacy prefix; overlay build only; nothing before them moves, "Parameter order (fixed)": Q 1 .. Q 4 are the old ones, Q 5 .. Q 16 were appended). The hardware knobs (Q-Links) of the host are mapped to them on the pages the plugin draws, Q-Link n = Q n on every overlay tab (skin: `Bank Direction "Row"`, all 16 Q-Links, `82_play.py` / `83_pages.py`; the host's own numbering stays, the SDK numbers the 4 x 4 grid from the bottom row). A Q proxy has no value of its own: the page sets the SLOTS, up to sixteen targets, each the table index of an engine parameter or "none" (`IInstanceView::setQGroup` in `shell-mpc/overlay/curve_source.h`; the logic is `QRouter` in `shell-mpc/q_router.h`, pure and tested on the host), and then
- reading Q n gives the normalised value of its target (0 when it has none), and its name, display text, label and properties are the target's (the name is the target's table name, e.g. "Cutoff Env Amount"; a macro is the exception: it reports the name the macro has been given, "Filter Sweep", "Macro 5" without one, cut at a character to the 64 bytes of the host's buffer, and the host is told by `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` when a rename or a loaded program / preset / chunk brings other names: "Macro names" below; a transpose that the Tune group splits into two knobs says "... Oct" and "... Sem", shows +n octaves / +n semitones and has the labels oct / st; a Q without a target, or with a virtual one of the LFO shape editor, is named "Q n" and shows nothing);
- writing Q n is the same write as the host's own write to the target (`writeRealParam`: mirror, protection, engine); a write of the value the target has (an echo of a report) changes nothing; a write to a Q without a target is ignored;
- a write inside the restore window (see "Project save and reload") is ignored, as for the Play proxies. Loading a project or preset also retires the old Q mapping immediately. Q input resumes after the display controller refreshes the active page for the loaded state.
- when the slots change, the display thread tells the host the sixteen new values (`audioMasterAutomate`, the mechanism of the Play proxies), so the knob display of the host follows, and then once `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` (`QRouter::poll`, the group-changed callback, once for any change of any of the 16 targets and never when nothing changed), because the names, units and value texts of the proxies are other now. **Measured on the Key 61 (2026-11-16, the host's Q-Link panel that appears while a knob is touched, the plugin told the host by a switch in a test build):** without any call the host KEEPS the names it read first (after the group changed from Macros 1-4 to 5-8 and back, and after a page change from the Play page's Sub group to Macros, the panel showed the names of an earlier group: "Sub Waveform ..." on the Macros page, "Macro 5 .. 8" for the group Macro 1-4); with `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` the panel shows the names, value texts and dial positions of the current group at once (the next time it appears, about half a second after the touch) and also after a group change while it is open (a tap on the BANK button 0.6 s after the touch: the panel was on the new names when it was photographed about 0.5 s later; the timing is approximate); `audioMasterIOChanged` alone changed nothing (stale names), so it is not used (it may also make the host re-initialise the plugin). `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` also makes the host read the program name again, and the host then shows the name exactly as the plugin reports it: the "*" that the host puts after the preset name of a project that was loaded ("Acid Saw Reso *") disappeared after the first call (measured: the header read "Acid Saw Reso" after the first group change and stayed so after knob turns). **The "modified" mark is gone by design (owner decision, 2026-11-19):** the earlier plugin appended " *" to the program name while a flag (`programModified`, set by a chunk restore or a real parameter change) was set, so that the host kept showing a mark after it had dropped its own; the owner found the mark misleading, and it was removed. No name the host reads carries a mark any more: `effGetProgramName`, `effGetProgramNameIndexed` (every `SYNTHYBOY_MPC_PROGRAM_GROUPS` mode), the name in the chunk (`mpcProgramName`, always the preset's own name), the `.xpl` names and the content pack are checked to have no trailing "*" (`shell-mpc/tests/programs_test.cpp`, `shell-mpc/tests/play_host.cpp`, `scripts/tests/test_gen_xpl_presets.py`). The flag is gone with it; the only thing left is `PluginInstance::programNameRefreshDue`, set by a chunk restore, which makes the display thread call `audioMasterUpdateDisplay` once so that the host reads the plain name again (the call that drops the host's own "*", measured above). A project saved by an earlier version with "Name *" loads as "Name": `stripModifiedMark` (`shell-mpc/program_list.h`) cuts a trailing "*" and the spaces before it from the name read out of the chunk (`programNameFromChunk`) and from the name `resolveProgramInView` looks up. `effSetProgramName` is not supported (returns 0), so no name comes in by that route. Whether the host puts its own "*" after a parameter edit (it does not, after the call above, once the name was read again) was not re-measured without the plugin mark. What the host does with the project besides showing the text (a save prompt) was not observable without closing Sbtest and is not known. The knob displays of the XL were not measured. A target that changes by another route (its own page, a preset, automation, the plugin's own touch screen) is reported on its Q in the same way, and a host write of a Q makes the display thread report the REAL parameter too (its own page follows) but not the Q back to the host that wrote it; a value is reported only when it differs from what the host last heard (tolerance one millionth), so a host that sets every reported value back causes no loop;
- the Q proxies are NOT saved in the chunk (nor is the group) and are not offered for automation (`effCanBeAutomated` is 0). A host may still record an automation on a Q proxy: it replays into whatever group is active at the time, so what it moves is not what was recorded. Record on the real parameter instead (its own page, or the parameter of the overlay page), which is stable.
- Audio thread rule: `getParameter` and `setParameter` of a Q only use lock-free atomics (a read of the target, one step): no allocation, no lock (`shell-mpc/tests/q_router_host.cpp` counts every `operator new` on those paths and runs under ThreadSanitizer).

**The mapping of the 16 slots (one rule for both devices).** A page exposes its Q GROUPS in reading order (a group is four values: a block of a screen, a strip, a row, a bank). Slots 1-4 (Q 1 .. Q 4) are the ACTIVE group, exactly as before; slots 5-8, 9-12 and 13-16 are the next groups of the page in reading order after the active one, wrapping around, each group once; a page with fewer than four groups leaves the remaining slots without a target (name "Q n", value 0, writes ignored). `QRouter::mapGroups` and `QRouter::blockOfGroup` (`q_router.h`) are the rule, `overlay/q_publish.h` (`QSlots::publish`) is how a page hands its groups over; the router is told sixteen slots (`IInstanceView::setQGroup (targets, n)`, `n` up to 16; a page that only knows one group passes four, so Q 5 .. Q 16 have no target). The MPC XL has 16 knobs in a horizontal strip, each with a display, so all four logical blocks are reachable at once, the host's own numbering (the active group on Q-Links 1-4 wherever the host puts them) is kept and no rows are remapped. On the XL standard LFO full screen, short groups are packed along the physical strip: smoothing time and its enable switch share a control and occupy Q4 and Q5. KEY, TIMING and MOTION follow in sequence. The Key 61 has four knobs and a bank button (`Bank Direction "Row"`: bank 1 = Q-Link 1-4 = the active group, banks 2-4 = Q-Links 5-8, 9-12, 13-16 = the other groups). Every proxy reports its target's name, unit, value text and properties (the XL's displays and the Key 61's pop-up show the real names for all 16). The groups of each page (the "list"):
| page | groups in reading order (slot block 1 = the active one) |
|---|---|
| Play: the three thirds | four groups published together (`PlayView::publishQ`): the oscillator's Basic, the envelope's ADSR, the LFO's RATE and a fourth group [LFO SYNC `lfoN.sync`, OSC POWER `oscN.on` / `sub.on` / `noise.on`, LFO MONO `lfoN.mono`, -] of the LFO and the oscillator that are shown (XL: Q 13 .. 16; Key 61: bank 4; no chips: its controls are the Play page's own). All three are STEPPED targets: one step per 6 clicks, so one click cannot switch an oscillator off (`q_step_rule_host.cpp` `testNudge`). The header dropdowns are not Q members. |
| Osc A / B / C / Sub / Noise tabs | the default view of the type: Basic = the top row [TYPE, SOURCE, the position knob, OCT] (Sub [WAVEFORM, OCT, CRS, PHASE]; Noise [KIND, SOUND, FILTER, STEREO], SOUND only while generated), then the blocks of the bottom band in reading order: wavetable UNISON [UNISON, DETUNE, FIN] and WARP [WARP 1 TYPE, WARP 1 AMOUNT, WARP 2 TYPE, WARP 2 AMOUNT]; multisample, sample WARP with warp 1 only, spectral UNISON [UNISON, DETUNE]; granular GRAIN [DENS, LENGTH, WARP] and PLAYBACK [OFFSET, DIR, PITCH, FIN]; Sub BEHAVIOUR [ENV 1, MONO, FREE RUN]; noise generated TUNE [PITCH, FINE, KEY TRACK, ENV 1], noise sample TUNE and PLAYBACK [START, RANDOM, LOOP, BOUNCE]. A hidden control (the amount of a warp that is Off, BOUNCE while LOOP is off) is no member and the group closes up. Two or three groups per page, no group is paged on the XL. LEVEL, PAN and DESTINATION are read-only here (the Mixer is their home).  |
| Osc detail pages (Unison, Grain, Envelope) | the groups of the detail panel |
| Osc / Filter modulation list (rows) | one group per row: depth, polarity, on / off of the row |
| Osc route popup | one group |
| Mixer | On XL, Q1–Q10 follow the ten LEVEL faders from left to right, ending with BUS 1, BUS 2 and MAIN. The four-knob banks are [SUB, A, B, C], [NOISE, F1, F2, MAIN] and [BUS 1, BUS 2]. PAN, sends and destinations use touch. |
| Filter, states F1 / F2 | MAIN [CUTOFF, RESO, DRIVE, MIX], TRACK [KEYTRACK, ENV 1, VAR (Formant model: the formant RESONANCE), COMB (Comb model only)], OUT [BLEND, ROUTING, MODEL, STYLE] (frameless: MODEL and STYLE stand in the top bar), and FORMANT [X, Y, TRANSPOSE, SPREAD] with the Formant model; of the selected filter |
| Filter, states NOTE / VELO | EDIT [POINT, X, Y, CURVE] (the virtual targets 0 .. 3) and LEGATO AND GRID [LEGATO (`curve.note.legato` / `curve.velocity.legato`), GRID X, GRID Y, SNAP] (the legato key and the virtual targets 4 .. 6): the two legato keys are Q members since 2026-11-21 |
| FX | the rows of four values of the open effect that are on the editor's page (the first and the second row, or three rows without a display) |
| Mod: matrix | four groups (the XL: Q 1 .. 16; the Key 61: banks 1 .. 4; the matrix has no active group of its own): [ROW (virtual target), SOURCE, TARGET, AMOUNT] of the selected slot, [UNI / BI, ON / OFF, AUX SOURCE, AUX SCALE] of the same slot (the aux pair has no control on this page: it is the AUXILIARY SOURCE block of the slot page), and the two next visible rows as [SOURCE, TARGET, AMOUNT, UNI / BI] (`matrixGroupTargets` in `q_publish_mod.h`; the idle chips are on the controls of the rows that are on screen: `ModMatrixView::qmore`) |
| Mod: slot page | AUXILIARY SOURCE, SHAPE, TIME |
| Mod / Macros targets list | one group per row |
| Macros | the two banks: MACROS 1-4, MACROS 5-8 (the shown bank is active, the other one is on Q 5 .. 8) |
| Master | the groups of the side that is up: OUTPUT, PITCH, VOICES, GLIDE (main side: four, all on the XL at once, the four banks of the Key 61; VOICE OPTS (VEL TRACK, SAME NOTE, STEAL) has no Q group any more, it is touch only: GLIDE took its place, owner may reverse; LINK is touch only inside PITCH, no Q slot) or SCALE, TUNING, MPE, MPE Y (tuning side); hidden controls (BEND DOWN while LINK is on, MPE BEND and BEND TO X while MPE is off) are no members, the group closes up from slot 1 |
| Voice | four groups (Q-Link mapping extension, 2026-11-21): STEP [STEP, ROW (virtual targets), LENGTH, VALUE], RANDOM [PAN, DETUNE, CUTOFF, ENV], SCALING [10x DETUNE, ENV TIME, LFO TIME, LFO MODE], TARGETS [OSC A, B, C, SUB]; NOISE (the 17th value) is touch only |
| Env | ADSR, SHAPE, TIME (`EnvUi::publishQGroup`: three groups, Q 13 .. 16 empty) |
| LFO | RATE, TIMING (HZ / BPM / KEY list, PHASE, DELAY, MODE: the reading order of the block), MOTION (DIRECTION, SWING, STEREO, MONO), KEY FOLLOW (only in the key follow sync), SHAPE (SMOOTH, HOST); the random LFO: RANDOM (the style grid, RATE, HZ / BPM / KEY, STEREO) and TRIGGER (MODE, a group of one) in one block, KEY FOLLOW; the editor: EDIT, GRID, TRIGGER (MODE, PHASE, BEND ALL); the targets / modulations pages one group per row |
Tests: `shell-mpc/tests/q_router_host.cpp` (the rule, wrapping, fewer than four groups, the notification count for any of the 16 targets), `shell-mpc/tests/q_router_review_host.cpp`, `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/qlink_panel_host.cpp` and `shell-mpc/overlay/tests/qlink_setting_host.cpp`.

**Stepped targets are driven relatively (Q router, 2026-11-16, reworked in round 6).** WHICH parameters: `shell-mpc/q_step_rule.h` (`stepCountOf`) - every parameter whose scale is `stepped` (toggles and lists of any length: a list of 1249 destinations cannot be reached with an absolute knob of 128 clicks) and the integer steppers of at most 32 values (voices, polyphony, bend ranges, octave, length: ten parameters, listed in `q_step_rule_host.cpp`); the fine-grained quantised amounts (grain density 1 .. 64, transposes, note and velocity ranges, key tracking: 63 parameters, the count is pinned by the test) stay absolute knobs. HOW: a stepped target is not driven by the proxy's absolute 0 .. 1 (a toggle needed half of the knob's travel, a short list a third: reported by the owner on the MPC XL). `QRouter::hostWrite (..., stepsOf)` takes the distance the host moved its own value with each write and makes ONE step of the target per `kStepTravelDefault` = 6 / 128 (owner, XL, 2026-11-16: the first 3 were too fine; both devices; override: the file `/tmp/synthyboy-overlay-q-step-travel`, 1/128 increments, read once a second), in the direction of the turn; a change of direction starts the travel again; the ends of a list do nothing. One host write makes at most `kMaxStepsPerWrite` = 4 steps and writes the target ONCE (the audio thread may be the caller); a move of more than four steps is a jump of the host (automation, a far report), no travel: it only re-bases. The distance of a write: the host's previous write and the last value we reported are both candidates and the one nearer to ONE increment (1/128) counts; a write that repeats the previous one exactly is the first write of a new touch (from the report). So the rule does not depend on whether the host adopts our reports: the test `q_router_host.cpp` simulates three hosts (adopts at once, never adopts, adopts one poll late) and asserts that 30 increments are 5 steps on a list of 57, and a toggle that is ON when its page is entered does not flip for three increments. A new target in a slot (the page changed its group): the slot word carries a generation (`setGroup`), the stepping state of the old target is dropped, the baseline is the value told to the host for the new one, and a write before the host was told only moves the baseline (one detent never jumps a list: tests R1 / R2 of `q_router_review_host.cpp`, which also covers the torn read of the slot, F7). The Q proxy of a stepped target is a plain knob of 1/128 for the host (`getParameterProperties`): with the target's integer range the host quantised its own increments (MEASURED, Key 61: a list of 5 jumped four entries for six increments, a list of 20 did not move for fourteen single increments). **MEASURED on the Key 61 (play log, `ov26-results/`):** the host writes 1/128 per increment; within one touch (a burst, increments 20 milliseconds apart) it goes on from its OWN value although we report the target's position in between; at the next touch it starts from the value we reported ("the host adopts the report at the next touch"), e.g. report 1.0, one increment down arrives as 0.9921875; six single increments (one per touch) flip a toggle, a burst of six does too; a burst can arrive as one write of several increments. After a step the poll reports the target's position, so a list of 57 does not run into the host's 0 / 1 limit. **Status file (to calibrate a quarter turn of a knob):** `q_step_travel: 0.046875 (6.00 increments of 1/128, default)` (`overridden` while the debug file is in use), then one line per knob of the instance on screen that has a target: `q_knob_N: target T, raw R (x/128), delta D (x/128), travel A (x/128 of 6.00 per step), turned S/128 in all` - `raw` is the value the host last wrote to Q N, `delta` the move of that write, `travel` the signed accumulation towards the next step, `turned` the signed sum of every delta since the plugin started. CALIBRATION of a quarter turn: read `turned` of the knob (the line of the Q it drives), turn the knob by a quarter of its rotation, read `turned` again: the difference is the number of 1/128 increments of a quarter turn. Tests: `q_router_host.cpp`, `q_router_review_host.cpp`, `q_step_rule_host.cpp`, `play_host.cpp`. Not measured: the quarter turn of a knob in increments (the `turned` counter has to be read on the device) and the MPC XL (the write behaviour above is the Key 61's).

**Chips on the screen.** The ACTIVE group keeps the yellow frame and the chips 1 .. 4 on its members.
